## Phase merges

For each phase:
- its tag and branch;
- a pull request title and description;
- the protected paths it touched;
- a summary of its tests.

### Phase 1: finish the specified scope, plus the foundations

- **Tag and branch:** `expansion-phase-1`; branch `expansion/phase-1` (the same commit as `expansion-step-06`). Open the pull request from `expansion/phase-1` into `main`. Tahseen opens it; nothing here did.
- **Title:** Expansion phase 1: order changes, editable chart lists, reference data, the inbox, appointments, messages and proxy access

**Description**

Phase 1 of `docs/expansion/PLAN.md`: steps 0 to 6. With it, every functional requirement in the course document is built.

What it adds:
- **Orders** can be changed and discontinued, with a reason, and never deleted (UC 1; FR-37 proposed).
- **Allergies, medications and diagnoses** are edited from the chart with coded entries, history and "no known allergies"; sensitive items are marked, shown apart, and hidden from administrators in the audit view (UC 4, UC 12; FR-38 to FR-40 proposed).
- **Reference data:** ICD-10-CM, RxNorm, LOINC, UNII, FDA drug classes and DDInter 2.0 loaded from pinned files by `tools/reference`, and a synthetic seed whose patients' problems, medications and results fit together. `docs/expansion/reference-data.md` has the sources and licenses. DDInter is CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, an accepted exception to NFR-16 for this non-commercial project.
- **A clinician inbox** (UC 15, FR-41 proposed), **appointments** with a day schedule (FR-17; UC 16, FR-42 proposed), **secure messages** between a patient and their care team (FR-18; UC 17, FR-43 proposed) and **proxy access** to the portal (FR-19; UC 18, FR-44 proposed).

Protected paths, for line-by-line review by someone other than Tahseen (AGENTS.md):
- `db/policies`: `10-grants.sql`, `30-rls.sql`, `40-audit.sql`, `60-orders.sql` changed; `65-chart-lists.sql`, `70-inbox.sql`, `80-appointments.sql`, `90-messages.sql`, `95-proxy-access.sql` new. About 1,180 lines added. The largest single change in meaning is step 6's: `ehr.current_patient_id()` now answers with another patient under a proxy grant the database has checked. Three functions run as their owner: `ehr.deliver_message`, `ehr.shown_audit_details` and `ehr.proxied_records`, with owner-only policies on the tables they read or write.
- Authorization: `Program.cs` (area policies for the new folders, the new filters), `UserContext.cs` and `UserContextInterceptor.cs` (a second setting, `app.acting_grant`), `UserContextMiddleware.cs`, `PortalSubject.cs`, `InboxCountFilter.cs`, the gate in `Areas/Clinical/Pages/Patients/Messages/MessagePage.cs`, and `CloudAuditEvents.cs`.
- Tests about authorization that were changed on purpose: `PortalSubjectTests` (a portal request may name a thread or a proxy grant, never a patient; portal pages take their patient from `PortalSubject`), `HandledOffSiteTests` (the proxy's record switch is handled on the cloud app and records nothing), `PortalResultsIsolationTests` (a proxy's attempt added beside the patient's). `AGENTS.md` was updated to say what a portal session now sees.
- Not touched: the sync conflict resolver, the dictation prompt and the note schema.

Migrations: `OrderChanges`, `ChartLists`, `AuditSensitive`, `ClinicalCodeSearch`, `ReferenceTables`, `InboxItems`, `Appointments`, `AppointmentVersion`, `Messages`, `ProxyGrants`.

Tests: 780 in the solution; 750 pass and 30 performance cases are skipped unless a benchmark results file is given (CI's benchmark job checks them). New: test cases TC-UC1-07 to 12, TC-UC4-07 to 13, TC-UC12-07, TC-UC6-07 to 11, and six each for UC 15 to UC 18, each with a keyboard-only browser test; security tests against the database for every new table (`OrderChangeSecurityTests`, `ChartListSecurityTests`, `SensitiveAuditSecurityTests`, `InboxSecurityTests`, `AppointmentSecurityTests`, `MessageSecurityTests`, `ProxyAccessSecurityTests`). The render benchmark has a row for every new page, on-site and off-site, and `--assert` passes at 50,000 patients.

Generated files (`db/reference/**`, the migrations' designer files and the model snapshot) are marked `linguist-generated` in `.gitattributes`, so GitHub collapses them: about 260,000 of this pull request's added lines are reference data. `expansion/phase-1` was fast-forwarded once after its tag to add that rule and the plan's notes on it (step 6a); the tag `expansion-phase-1` is one merge behind the branch.

Each step was also read by a review agent before it was merged; what it found and what was left open is in the decision log. That does not replace the human review above.

Open questions for Tahseen are listed at the end of the log's step entries; the main ones: DDInter's downloads have no management advice and no license text on the download page; whether a proxy may be someone who isn't a patient of the clinic; the limits chosen for messages and grants; whether administrators should still see audit values for orders, results and note text.

Heads-up to use-case owners: Diego (UC 3/4: the chart gained sections and editing), Simar (UC 5/6: the portal gained appointments, messages, Access and a changed app bar), Luke (UC 11/12: the audit view hides sensitive values and message text), everyone (the app bar gained Schedule and Inbox).

### Phase 2: close the clinical loops

- **Tag and branch:** branch `expansion/phase-2`, moved forward with normal pushes (fast-forwards, nothing rewritten) to the merge of step 9d, which is tagged `expansion-phase-2.1`. It adds only phase 2's follow-ups (step 10e, step 9c, step 9d) and no phase 3 work. The tag `expansion-phase-2` is where it was made, at `expansion-step-10c`, and was not moved (Tahseen, 19 November). Open the pull request from `expansion/phase-2` into `main`, after phase 1's is merged: it contains phase 1. Tahseen opens it; nothing here did.
- **Phase 2.2 (2026-11-22), tests only:** `expansion/phase-2` was moved forward again with a normal push, by five commits that change only `tests/`, and tagged `expansion-phase-2.2` (CI run [44990040199](https://github.com/tarnan3751/EHR-Electronic-Health-Record-Project/actions/runs/44990040199)). The vital-sign, result and note tests built their times from the test machine's clock while the database judges them by its own; when the two differ (Docker's clock stops while a Mac sleeps, and lags for some seconds after it wakes; one test also failed every day from 00:00 to 00:10 UTC) they failed. They now read the database's clock. No assertion, tolerance or database rule changed, which an independent review confirmed. Open the pull request from `expansion/phase-2` at this tag.
- **Title:** Expansion phase 2: results, ordering safety checks, vital signs, addenda and sensitive notes, proxy-only accounts, and security follow-ups

**Description**

Phase 2 of `docs/expansion/PLAN.md`: steps 6a to 10c (7, 6b, 7a, 6c, 8, 9, 10, then Tahseen's 9b, 10b and 10c of 19 November), step 10e, his decisions on the phase's open questions, and steps 9c and 9d, which fix a step 9b defect at its root and replace the children's ranges from the primary sources. It closes the loops phase 1 opened: an order gets its result, a result gets acknowledged, a medication order is checked before it is signed, a visit's measurements are recorded and corrected, and a signed note can be added to.

What it adds:
- **Results of orders** (UC 1, UC 6, UC 15; FR-45 proposed): results are filed against the order that asked for them, values outside their range are flagged and listed first, the ordering clinician is told through the inbox, and acknowledging is recorded. Patients see released results in the portal.
- **Ordering safety checks** (UC 1; FR-47 proposed): a medication order is checked against the patient's allergies and what they take. Interactions come from DDInter 2.0, the ONC high-priority list (Wikidata, CC0) and a small hand-written table from FDA labels; each warning names its source. A serious warning needs a reason to sign past it, and the warning and reason are kept on the order.
- **Vital signs** (UC 4, UC 6; FR-48 and FR-50 proposed): recorded as coded observations with the body mass index worked out by the database; a flowsheet; trend charts drawn by the server as SVG, with a table of the numbers, for clinicians and in the portal; a wrong reading is marked entered in error with a reason and never deleted; heart rate, respiratory rate and blood pressure are flagged against a range for the patient's age.
- **Vital-sign readings** (UC 4; FR-50 extended; step 9c): at most one standing value per patient, sign and time taken, whatever the visit, held by the database; a blood pressure is one reading whose two values share a reading ID, are entered together, marked in error together and corrected together; a vital sign taken up to 15 minutes before a visit's start belongs to that visit whenever it is typed, because a check-in ties the sets already recorded; a visit the apps start begins at the database's clock. **This fixes a step 9b defect:** before it, a second standing value for the same moment was accepted when a check-in happened in between.
- **Children's ranges from the primary sources** (UC 4, UC 6; step 9d): heart and respiratory rate against Fleming et al.'s 1st and 99th centiles for 13 age groups, blood pressure against the 2017 AAP guideline's Table 6 by year of age and sex; a child's blood pressure at or above its screening value reads "needs follow-up", never High or abnormal.
- **Addenda and sensitive notes** (UC 14, UC 15; FR-49 and FR-51 proposed): a signed note's text stays locked; clinicians on the patient's panel sign addenda, and one by someone other than the note's signer is labeled and reaches the signer's inbox; a note or addendum can be sensitive, at signing or afterwards with a reason, and only the person who marked it or the note's signer unmarks it. The desktop app's offline chart shows signed notes and addenda, except sensitive ones.
- **Proxy-only portal accounts** (UC 18, UC 6; FR-46 proposed): someone who isn't a patient is invited by email to an account that sees only the records shared with it, and chooses whose record to view after signing in.
- **Security follow-ups** (step 6c): invitation and enrollment codes are looked up only through functions and stored as hashes (enrollment codes with a server-side key); every emailed link is built from a configured base address, never the request's Host header.
- **Off-site audit cost** (UC 2, step 10c): off-site, a request's audit events are written to the queue together, with one flush to disk, before the page is sent.

**Before deploying, read these:**
- **Two settings are now required outside Development,** and the app refuses to start without them: `Ehr__Links__ClinicalBaseUrl` and `Ehr__Links__PortalBaseUrl`; and on the on-site app `Ehr__Enrollment__Key` (a server-side key for enrollment codes, never in the repo).
- **Desktops need the new build.** The draft sync now withholds the text of a draft signed as a sensitive note and an updated desktop empties its copy; an older desktop build empties a synced copy but keeps text typed on it and never sent (step 10's log).
- **Four migrations move or re-range existing data** and are written by hand inside generated migrations: `VitalSigns` (a visit's blood pressure and heart rate become observations), `VitalCorrections` (existing vital signs get the range for the patient's age; about 17 seconds for 900,000 readings on a laptop, during which reads of observations wait), `NoteSensitivity` and the note migrations' rollbacks. `VitalCorrections` can't be rolled back once a reading has been marked entered in error. The migration job's command timeout is now ten minutes.
- **Two more migrations change existing vital signs, and say what they did in the migration job's output** (lines beginning "Migration 9c:" and "Migration 9d:"; the job printed no notices before step 9c):
  - `VitalReadings` (step 9c) gives each standing blood pressure a reading; where a moment has more than one value of a sign it keeps the most recent pair entered together and marks the rest entered in error, each with a reason ending "(migration 9c)" and an audit event with no actor, shown as "System". On the development stack (878,262 vital signs) it marked 1 value, whose partner was already in error, and made 292,737 readings; on a fresh 50,000-patient seed it marks nothing. About 11 seconds for 400,000 values.
  - `ChildrenRanges` (step 9d) ranges every child's heart rate, respiratory rate and blood pressure again. On the development stack: 168,636 standing values re-ranged; 9,358 newly flagged, 396 no longer flagged, 75,183 changed from High to "needs follow-up". That stack's synthetic children were generated for the old ranges, so most of their blood pressures are flagged until it is seeded again; a fresh seed flags about one in ten.
  - Rolling either back needs the earlier release's `db/policies` applied before vital signs can be recorded again.
- **Vital signs taken before check-in:** a set taken more than 15 minutes before a visit starts is not tied to that visit, and the chart's line for the visit says it was not taken at it.
- **The adult diastolic reference range changed from 60–84 to 60–79,** and existing readings were re-ranged; an adult's 80 to 84 is now flagged High. Tahseen confirmed it on 19 November: the 2017 ACC/AHA guideline's normal is under 120 and under 80; 80 to 84 is "normal" only on the European scale (`docs/expansion/reference-data.md`).
- **The desktop app needs the new build to fully remove sensitive notes' draft text.** Said above for the draft sync; repeated here because it is the one thing in this phase a server deploy alone doesn't finish: until a desktop is updated, text typed on it for a draft later signed as a sensitive note, and never sent, stays on that machine.
- **The `InheritedSensitivity` migration** (step 10e) marks existing addenda that are sensitive only because their note is as inherited. It changes no text and writes no audit event. Only an addendum marked by the note's own marking, or signed plain to a note already sensitive, is taken as inherited; any other keeps a mark of its own and stays hidden until someone unmarks it.

Protected paths, for line-by-line review by someone other than Tahseen (AGENTS.md):
- `db/policies`: `10-grants.sql`, `30-rls.sql`, `40-audit.sql`, `50-clinical-notes.sql`, `60-orders.sql`, `70-inbox.sql` changed; `45-codes.sql`, `61-order-safety.sql`, `75-results.sql` new. About 1,270 lines added since phase 1. The changes in meaning that most need a careful read:
  - **A signed note now accepts one update, its sensitivity** (step 10b): the immutability triggers cover deletes, and `ehr.change_note_sensitivity` and `ehr.change_addendum_sensitivity` hold every update, by every role, to "the sensitivity changes, with a reason".
    - **The test that proves the database refuses any other change to a signed note or addendum:** `NoteSensitivitySecurityTests.Nothing_but_the_sensitivity_changes_and_only_for_a_clinician_with_the_patient_on_their_panel` (`tests/Ehr.Security.Tests`). For a note and for an addendum it shows: the text, the signer, the patient, who marked it and when can't be updated by the app role (no privilege) or by a superuser (the trigger); a delete is refused for both; a sensitivity change needs a real change and a reason; and nobody off the panel, no administrator, no patient and no read-only connection changes anything.
  - **An addendum's mark can be inherited from its note** (step 10e): `ehr.clear_inherited_addenda_marks` is new, `ehr.change_addendum_sensitivity` and the insert rule decide `sensitive_inherited`, and `note_addenda_panel_update` lets whoever may unmark the note clear the inherited marks with it. The apps have no privilege on the column. `NoteSensitivitySecurityTests.Marking_a_note_marks_its_addenda_as_inherited_and_unmarking_it_clears_only_those` covers it.
  - **The apps can now update two columns of an observation** (step 9b), held by `ehr.mark_vital_sign_in_error` to "a final vital sign becomes entered in error, once, with a reason".
  - `ehr.file_result` (steps 7, 9, 9b, 9c, 9d) decides what an observation inserted by the apps may be: a result of an open lab order, or a vital sign. Since step 9c it also takes a lock per patient and moment, decides the visit from the time taken, and gives a blood pressure value its reading ID; since step 9d it picks the range by age and sex and sets its kind.
  - **Step 9c's rules in `75-results.sql`:** `ehr.check_vital_reading` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, on three deferred constraint triggers) refuses at the end of the transaction, for every role, a reading that is not one standing systolic and one standing diastolic or wholly in error; `ehr.mark_reading_in_error` marks the other value with the first; `ehr.check_in_visit` sets the start of a visit the apps insert to the database's clock and refuses any status but in-progress; `ehr.attach_vital_signs` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, after a visit's insert) ties that patient's vital signs taken from 15 minutes before the start to the visit, changing only `encounter_id`, audited as the clinician. And in `30-rls.sql`, `observations_owner_select` and `observations_owner_update`, for the owner only, which the two definer functions need under forced row-level security.
    - The tests that try to get round them: `VitalReadingSecurityTests` and `VitalSignVisitSecurityTests` (`tests/Ehr.Security.Tests`). The review agent's own attempts (savepoints, SET CONSTRAINTS, COPY, role changes, multi-row and ON CONFLICT statements, backdated visits, a long-held transaction, two-connection races) are described in the log's entries for steps 9c and 9d.
  - `ehr.sign_order` (step 8) runs the safety check itself and requires the override reason.
  - `ehr.record_write` (steps 10, 10b) marks a sensitive note's draft and dictation-review events sensitive too.
  - Six `SECURITY DEFINER` functions for invitation and enrollment codes (`45-codes.sql`), and `ehr.proxied_records()`, `ehr.deliver_result`, `ehr.clear_result_items`, `ehr.deliver_addendum`, `ehr.log_note_sensitivity_change`.
- Authorization: `EhrPolicies.Patient` and the `ehr:portal` claim (proxy-only accounts, step 6b); `PortalSubject`; the area conventions cover the new pages (`Patients/Orders/Results`, `Patients/Vitals`, `Patients/Notes/Addendum`, `Patients/Notes/Sensitivity`, `Portal/Invitation`, `Portal/Choose`); the cloud app's pipeline gained `CloudAuditFlushMiddleware` (step 10c), which is on the audit path.
- **The sync conflict resolver's code:** `src/Ehr.Desktop/src-tauri/src/draft_sync.rs` (`pull` empties a withheld draft) and `src/Ehr.Web/Api/Desktop/DraftSyncApi.cs` (step 10). Sara's.
- Not touched: the dictation prompt and the note schema.
- Tests about authorization, PHI or the audit log that were changed on purpose, none weakened: `RuntimeRoleTests` (an observation's status alone is now a column grant; the refused cases are its value, and its status with any other column; the single change allowed is tested in `VitalSignCorrectionSecurityTests`); the step 10 test that an owner's bare update of a note is refused still holds, for a different reason (no reason given), and `NoteSensitivitySecurityTests` covers the rule that replaced it.

Migrations since phase 1: `OrderResults`, `ProxyOnlyAccounts`, `InteractionSources`, `OrderSafety`, `VitalSigns`, `VitalSignOnce`, `NoteAddenda`, `VitalCorrections`, `NoteSensitivity`, `InheritedSensitivity`, `VitalReadings`, `ChildrenRanges`.

Reference data added: `db/reference/50-onc-interactions.sql` (generated from a pinned Wikidata query result committed beside it), `60-label-interactions.sql` and `35-vital-ranges.sql` (hand-written, each row with its source). `docs/expansion/reference-data.md` has the sources, licenses and caveats. **The children's vital-sign ranges come from the primary sources Tahseen supplied** (step 9d): Fleming et al.'s Web Tables 4 and 5 (Lancet 2011, Web Appendix, page 12) and the 2017 AAP guideline's Table 6 (page 15), read from the originals; a child's blood pressure at or above its screening value is labeled "needs follow-up", not High. The `ChildrenRanges` migration ranged existing readings again and reports how many flags changed.

Tests: 942 in the solution; 906 pass and 36 performance cases are skipped unless a benchmark results file is given (CI's benchmark job checks them). New since phase 1: test cases TC-UC1-13 to 21, TC-UC2-07, TC-UC4-14 to 22 and 25 to 31 (23 and 24 are phase 3's), TC-UC6-12 to 14, TC-UC14-07 to 17, TC-UC15-07, TC-UC18-07 and 08, each use case's with a keyboard-only browser test; security tests against the database for results, code lookups, order safety, vital signs and their correction, addenda and note sensitivity; tests that run the data-moving migrations down and up on a protected database; desktop tests for the withheld draft and the chart copy.

The benchmark (`bench/Ehr.Bench --assert`) passes: every page's p99, excluding the commit, is under 20 ms at 50,000 patients. **The chart is the page nearest the gate on CI's runners** and failed it twice during the phase on slow runners (steps 6b and 10c); each time the page was made cheaper, not the gate looser.

Each step was read by a review agent before it was merged; every one found real defects, which were fixed with tests, and what was left open is in the log. That does not replace the human review above.

Open questions for Tahseen, from the log's step entries:
- Enrollment codes issued before they were keyed: invalidate at deploy, or accept until they expire (built: accept).
- Interaction data's class-level gaps: add Wikidata's 2022 statements or class-with-class label rows, or neither.
- Whether moderate and minor interactions should need a reason (built: no).
- Results reach the portal before they are acknowledged; administrators still see audit values for orders, results and non-sensitive notes.
- **The children's vital-sign ranges** (step 9d), decided by the coordinator, for Tahseen to confirm: a child whose sex is other or unknown is held to the lower of the boys' and girls' blood pressure values; a child's blood pressure has no lower value, and none under 1 year; Fleming's "Birth" row is not used. A database seeded before step 9d (the local stack) keeps its earlier synthetic children's values and shows many "needs follow-up" flags until it is seeded again.
- **Step 9c, for Tahseen:** two visits a few minutes apart (a set taken at the start of the first belongs, by the 15-minute rule, to the second; the development stack has 13 seeded moments like it, left where they are); correcting one number alone under the old page leaves no blood pressure standing at that moment once the pair rule is applied (none exists in the seed or the stack); an older complete pair replaced by a newer one is marked "duplicate resolved by migration 9c" while a leftover single value is marked "not entered together with a standing partner (migration 9c)"; a refused check-in shows the page's general "Not saved" message.
- Answered by Tahseen on 19 November and built in step 10e: the adult diastolic range stays 60–79; unmarking a note clears the marks its addenda inherited from it and no others. The proxy half of the "earlier views" test is to be rewritten in step 13a, which now says so.
- The cloud server's real flush cost on Linux (about 0.7 ms on CI's runner, against 3.7 ms on the development Mac), which decides whether more work on the off-site audit write is worth it.

Heads-up to use-case owners: Diego (UC 3/4: the chart gained Results, Vital signs and a sensitive-notes count; a visit's vital signs are observations), Simar (UC 5/6: the portal gained result trends, vital signs, proxy-only accounts and a record chooser), Sara (UC 7/8: the offline panel carries signed notes and addenda; the draft sync withholds sensitive notes' drafts), Satwik (UC 13/14: the confirmation page has a "Sensitive" box; a signed note lists addenda and can be marked sensitive later), Luke (UC 11/12: the audit view hides more for sensitive notes; the note about `main` below), everyone who orders (a medication order can come back asking for a reason).

- **For Luke (UC 11, UC 12), about `main`:** before step 6c, every link the app emails (password reset, confirmation, staff set-up) was built from the request's Host header whenever `Ehr:Links` wasn't set, so a request with a forged Host could make the app email someone a link to another site, carrying their token. That code is on `main` today (`Links.Build` in `src/Ehr.Web/Accounts/Links.cs`). Step 6c makes both base addresses required outside Development and removes the fallback. Until this phase is merged, a deployment from `main` must set `Ehr__Links__ClinicalBaseUrl` and `Ehr__Links__PortalBaseUrl`.

### Phase 3: connect to other systems

- **Tag and branch:** `expansion-phase-3`; branch `expansion/phase-3` (the same commit as `expansion-step-12`). Open the pull request from `expansion/phase-3` into `main`, after phase 2's is merged: it contains phases 1 and 2, with phase 2.1's follow-ups. Tahseen opens it; nothing here did.
- **Title:** Expansion phase 3: the clinic's time zone, a read-only FHIR API, and a simulated lab

**Description**

Phase 3 of `docs/expansion/PLAN.md`: steps 10d (Tahseen's, the clinic time zone), 11 and 11b (a read-only FHIR R4 API), and 12 (a simulated lab). It connects the record to other systems: something can read it through a standard API, and something can send results into it.

What it adds:
- **The clinic's time zone** (UC 4, UC 16; FR-52 proposed): clinician pages and the desktop app's offline copy show times on one configured clinic clock, labeled with the zone's abbreviation for that moment, and take typed times on it; everything is still stored in UTC. The audit log and the portal stay in labeled UTC.
- **A read-only FHIR R4 API** (new UC 19; FR-53 proposed): `/fhir`, for a signed-in clinician and a patient on their panel: Patient, AllergyIntolerance, MedicationStatement, Condition, Observation (results and vital signs, each blood pressure as one Observation built from step 9c's reading) and Appointment, by ID and by patient, with a capability statement generated from what is served. Every answered request is one audit event. Sensitive diagnoses and medications are left out unless asked for, as on the chart.
- **A simulated lab** (new UC 20; FR-54 proposed): a lab with a key of its own asks the on-site app for new lab orders and posts their results, which are filed through the rules a clinician's filing follows, tell the ordering clinician, and are audited with the lab as their source. `src/Ehr.Lab.Simulator` plays the lab over HTTP: values come from each test's own range and fit the patient's diagnoses, after a short delay.

**Before deploying, read these:**
- **One setting is now required outside Development,** and the app refuses to start without it: `Ehr__Clinic__TimeZone`, an IANA name such as `America/Chicago`. `Ehr__Clinic__TimeZoneAbbreviations` (such as `CST,CDT`) is optional; without it times are labeled with their offset.
- **The lab is optional.** With no `Ehr__Lab__Key` (and no `EHR_LAB_KEY` for the migration job) nothing changes: no door is mapped, and results are filed by hand. With one, the migration job stores the key's hash in the database (never the key), the on-site app opens `/api/lab`, and the Results page says a lab is connected. The key must be the same for the app, the migration job and the lab. The cloud app has no door and forwards nothing for it.
- **One migration,** `LabKeys` (a table for the lab key's hash; no data moves). The clinic time zone and the FHIR API changed no table.
- **The seed's thyrotropin unit is now UCUM** (`m[IU]/L`); a database seeded earlier keeps the old spelling on its seeded results, which the FHIR API then sends as text.
- **A long-running or idle-in-transaction database session holds back the lab's hand-over** of every order signed after it began, with no error (docs/UC20.md says what to look for). No session timeout is set by this phase.
- **Desktops:** the offline copy now names the clinic's zone; an older desktop build ignores it and shows UTC, labeled, as before.

Protected paths, for line-by-line review by someone other than Tahseen (AGENTS.md):
- `db/policies` (step 12 only; about 350 lines): `76-lab.sql` is new (`ehr.set_lab_key`, owner only; `ehr.require_lab`; `ehr.lab_orders` and `ehr.file_lab_results`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, granted to `ehr_app`, each demanding the stored hash of the key and nobody signed in; `ehr.lab_connected`). `30-rls.sql`: five owner-only policies (`orders_owner_select`, `orders_owner_update`, `observations_owner_insert`, `result_acknowledgments_owner_select`, `conditions_owner_select`), each holding only while the lab's own functions have opened `app.lab_door`. `75-results.sql`: `observations_file` now also fires for an insert an app session makes through an owner-run function (`session_user`). `40-audit.sql`: `record_write`'s source is `lab` for the lab's writes. `20-change-log.sql`: lab orders are logged when signed, for the hand-over's watermark. `10-grants.sql`: a comment.
  - The tests that try to get round the lab's door: `LabSecurityTests` (`tests/Ehr.Security.Tests`). What the review agent tried, in the log's step 12 entry.
- Authorization:
  - **`/fhir`** (step 11): the group takes the existing Clinician policy (`Program.cs`); the staff cookie answers a refused `/fhir` request with 401 or 403 instead of a redirect (`AccountSchemes.FhirPath`, `AccountServiceCollectionExtensions`); the gate and its order, lookup then gate then read, in `Api/Fhir/FhirApi.cs`.
  - **`/api/lab`** (step 12): the key check, the refusal of any request that also carries an app credential, and the hash of the presented key passed to the database, in `Api/Lab/LabApi.cs`; its mapping, on-site only and only with a key, in `Program.cs`; the two rate-limit windows in `AccountRateLimits.cs`; `Forwarding/CloudForwardingMiddleware.cs` forwards nothing under `/api/lab`.
- Not touched: the sync conflict resolver, the dictation prompt and the note schema.
- Tests about authorization, PHI or the audit log changed on purpose, none weakened: `AuthorizationTests` gained `/fhir` rows; `CloudAuditTests` gained a case refusing a shipped `sent_to_lab` event; three off-site tests (TC-UC4-20, TC-UC14-10, TC-UC14-15) now make the cut-off app notice the clinic's server is down before reading a page, with no assertion changed.

Migrations since phase 2.1: `LabKeys`.

Dependencies added, in the tests only: Hl7.Fhir.R4 and Hl7.Fhir.Specification.Data.R4 6.8.0, Firely.Fhir.Validation.R4 3.6.0, Fhir.Metrics 1.4.0, all BSD-3-Clause (their transitive packages MIT). Nothing new in the apps. The simulator uses the base class library only.

Tests: 1,038 in the solution; 992 pass and 46 performance cases are skipped unless a benchmark results file is given (CI's benchmark job checks them). New in this phase: `ClinicTimeTests`, TC-UC4-23 and 24, TC-UC16-07; TC-UC19-01 to 06 with `FhirCapabilityTests`, `FhirReaderTests`, `FhirObservationsTests`, `FhirPlanTests` and `UcumTests`, every FHIR answer parsed strictly and validated offline against R4 and the profiles it claims; TC-UC20-01 to 06 with `LabSecurityTests`, `LabDoorTests` and `LabSimulatorTests`.

The benchmark (`bench/Ehr.Bench --assert`) passes in CI: every row's p99, excluding the commit, is under 20 ms at 50,000 patients, with the clinic in America/Chicago and with rows for the FHIR API and the lab's door. It now stops a row that runs over a minute. **The chart is still the page nearest the gate on CI's runners** (15.4 ms on the last run).

Each step was read by a review agent before it was merged, and every one found real defects, fixed with tests; what was left open is in the log. That does not replace the human review above. Two defects in this phase were caught only by CI or by a second review after tests and a first review had passed: a FHIR query that read every diastolic in the table (CI's benchmark), and a lab door whose rate limit let keyless requests lock the lab out.

Open questions for Tahseen, from the log's step entries:
- **Clinic time:** time fields and the schedule's day are on the clinic's clock too, which goes past "show times in it"; the desktop's "Checked at" line uses the device's own clock; the age that picks a vital sign's range is worked out on the UTC day; the hour that happens twice when clocks go back can't be typed as the second; the abbreviations are configured apart from the zone.
- **FHIR:** oxygen saturation claims only the base vital-signs profile (R4's own wants LOINC 2708-6; the record holds 59408-5); pages and the API show `m[IU]/L` as spelled.
- **Lab:** a result reaches no inbox when its signer is no longer active and nobody has the patient on their panel; the lab's results reach the portal before they are acknowledged, as hand-filed ones do; a lab post that conflicts with a value entered by hand is refused whole; lab orders signed before step 12 are never handed over; whether to set `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` for the app roles.

Heads-up to use-case owners: Diego (UC 3/4: every time on the chart and its pages is on the clinic's clock, labeled; `docs/UC4.md` changed), Simar (UC 5/6: the portal is unchanged, but `docs/UC6.md` still says "as every date in the app is" and "the app has no clinic time zone yet", left for Simar to reword), Sara (UC 7/8: the offline copy carries the clinic's zone and `app.js` formats its times in it), Luke (UC 11/12: the staff cookie answers `/fhir` with 401 or 403, not a redirect; nothing else about sign-in changed), Satwik (UC 13/14: a signed note's times are on the clinic's clock), Samir (UC 9/10: no change).

### Phase 4: portal extras

- **Tag and branch:** `expansion-phase-4`; branch `expansion/phase-4` (one plan-only commit after `expansion-step-13c2`, holding this draft and the log). Open the pull request from `expansion/phase-4` into `main`, after phase 3's is merged: it contains phases 1 to 3, with phase 2.2's test-only follow-up. Tahseen opens it; nothing here did.
- **Title:** Expansion phase 4: visit summaries, a copy of the record, who viewed it, refill requests and pre-visit forms in the portal

**Description**

Phase 4 of `docs/expansion/PLAN.md`: steps 13a (visit summaries), 13b (download my record), 13c and 13c2 (who viewed my record, and which part), 13d (refill requests) and 13e (pre-visit forms). Everything works for someone the patient shares their record with, under the rules for sensitive items, at the portal's sizes, and no email carries a health detail.

What it adds:
- **Visit summaries** (UC 6, UC 14; FR-55 proposed): from a signed note a clinician writes a summary for the patient in three parts, which the database refuses while it holds a diagnosis, medication, lab or order code, a record number or an ID, or a line copied from the note. Released, it can only be withdrawn; the portal then keeps a dated placeholder. A summary is sensitive when its note or an addendum is.
- **Download my record** (UC 6, UC 19; FR-56 proposed): the record as one FHIR R4 file, with identifiers made for that download, or as a page laid out to print. **This is the first time the portal shows allergies, medications and diagnoses.** Pages now have print rules.
- **Who viewed my record** (UC 6, UC 12; FR-57 proposed): who was shown the record in the last 12 months, by name and role, when, and which part, with emergency access marked and never its reason. The audit log itself stays the admins' alone; a portal session reads through one function that answers only for the record it is looking at. Each read of patient data now records which part of the record it was for.
- **Refill requests** (new UC 21; FR-58 proposed): a patient asks the care team to renew a medication; a clinician approves only by signing an order for the same product through every ordering check, or declines with a message. A request stays with the care team as it changes.
- **Pre-visit forms** (new UC 22; FR-59 proposed): a clinician asks for a PHQ-9 or GAD-7 before a booked appointment; the patient answers once in the portal; the database scores it; clinicians see the score, its band and a trend. Everything about a form is a sensitive item. An answer above "Not at all" to the PHQ-9's ninth question shows the patient where to get help at once and reaches the top of the care team's inboxes.

**Before deploying, read these:**
- **Four migrations:** `VisitSummaries`, `RefillRequests`, `PreVisitForms`, `AuditEventParts`. The last adds a column to the audit log without rewriting it, and its check without reading it; it waits at most 5 seconds for the log's lock and then fails cleanly, to be run again. While it waits, audit inserts (so pages) and "Add to my patients" wait too. Run the migration job first, then the apps: an app older than the migration records no part, which everything accepts.
- **No new setting is required.**
- **The help wording on pre-visit forms** (`Areas/Portal/Pages/Forms/FormNotices.cs`: 911, 988, "not monitored") was written by the coordinating agent, not by a clinician. **Have a clinician read it before any real use,** and change the numbers for a clinic outside the United States.
- **Disabling a staff account** now tells again, to the care team or to every enabled clinician, any pre-visit form answer that account alone was holding for review.
- **Desktops:** nothing changed in the desktop app's code; its offline copy's count of sensitive items not kept now includes pre-visit forms.
- **The local stack of anyone who ran an in-progress build of these steps** may hold an earlier shape of `ehr.refill_requests` (a column was added to that migration before it merged): bring the stack up from an empty database, or add `medication_code` as the migration does.

Protected paths, for line-by-line review by someone other than Tahseen (AGENTS.md):
- `db/policies`:
  - `52-visit-summaries.sql` (13a, new): the plain-words rule and its normalisation; the guard and no-delete triggers; the inherit-and-follow trigger; `ehr.standing_visit_summaries`, `ehr.withdrawn_visit_summaries`, `ehr.withdrawn_summary_was_viewed` (owner-run).
  - `42-record-views.sql` (13c, 13c2, new): `ehr.record_views` (owner-run; the session worked out once; every filter before the page is cut), `ehr.record_view_part`, and the guard on `ehr.patient_assignments` that keeps an assignment's start and end at the present.
  - `77-refill-requests.sql` (13d, new): the guard (an approval is a new order for the product asked for, signed in the same transaction by the clinician answering), delivery, the care-team triggers on `ehr.patient_assignments` with their lock, the sensitivity trigger on `ehr.medication_statements`, `ehr.portal_refill_requests`.
  - `85-pre-visit-forms.sql` (13e, new): scoring, the guards, the no-change trigger, `ehr.tell_care_team_of_answer` and its three callers, among them a trigger on `ehr.staff_users`; the advisory lock and the answer's bounded wait.
  - **Step 1's and step 8's files, changed:** `60-orders.sql`, `ehr.sign_order`: "already on order" now goes by product (the clinic's item and RxNorm's for one drug at one strength are the same); `61-order-safety.sql`: `ehr.order_safety_warnings` is replaced in place and no longer dropped on every run.
  - `30-rls.sql`: the new tables' policies (none gives a portal session a row of `visit_summaries` or `refill_requests`: they read through functions); `medication_statements_own_select` no longer gives the portal a medication entered in error; one owner-only policy on `break_glass_accesses`. `10-grants.sql`, `40-audit.sql`, `70-inbox.sql`.
- Authorization and what sits beside it:
  - **Where a sign-in lands** (13b): `Accounts/Links.cs`, the portal's `SignIn.cshtml.cs`, and the staff sign-in's `SignIn`, `Code` and `Enroll`: after sign-in the app opens the page a link named and drops its query, keeping only a patient search's own criteria for staff.
  - **Who may open a pre-visit form's answers** (13e): a clinician holding an open inbox item for an answer may open that one answer without emergency access, until they mark the item done (`PhiReader.FormAnswersAsync`, `Forms/Response.cshtml.cs`). `requestId` joins the IDs a portal handler may take (`PortalSubjectTests`).
  - The audit path (13c2): `AuditRecorder` (a read without a part is refused; a list's reads are one statement), `AuditEventBatch`, `AuditEventImport`, `CloudAuditEvents` (the shapes the cloud may ship).
- Not touched: the sync conflict resolver, the dictation prompt and the note schema.
- Tests about authorization, PHI or the audit log changed on purpose, none weakened: Tahseen's sensitive-note proxy test (`NoteSensitivitySecurityTests`) was rewritten as he asked, keeping every assertion and adding that no portal session reads a row of the summaries' table; step 13c's who-viewed test cases expect the new words for each part, with no visibility expectation changed; `CloudAuditTests`, `PortalSubjectTests`, `ProtectionTests`, `RuntimeRoleTests` and `PolicyPlanTests` gained cases.

Migrations since phase 3: `VisitSummaries`, `RefillRequests`, `PreVisitForms`, `AuditEventParts`.

Dependencies added: none.

Reference data: the PHQ-9 and GAD-7 are now worded exactly as their authors' English forms, with their sources, hashes and every licence statement found in `docs/expansion/reference-data.md`. **The statements disagree in one respect:** the publisher's manual says the instruments are in the public domain; LOINC's notice and the 2001 paper say "Copyright © Pfizer Inc." beside "No permission required to reproduce, translate, display or distribute". The plan names them as free to use, so the step went ahead.

Tests: 1,364 in the solution; 1,294 pass and 70 performance cases are skipped unless a benchmark results file is given (CI's benchmark job checks them). New in this phase: TC-UC14-18 to 25; TC-UC6-15 to 35, 40 to 58 and 63 to 70; TC-UC11-07; TC-UC21-01 to 06; TC-UC22-01 to 12; TC-UC15-08, TC-UC1-22; and the security classes `VisitSummarySecurityTests`, `RecordViewSecurityTests`, `RefillRequestSecurityTests`, `PreVisitFormSecurityTests`, `QuestionnaireDefinitionTests`, `AuditPartSecurityTests`, `OrderSafetySecurityTests`.

The benchmark (`bench/Ehr.Bench --assert`) passes in CI: every row's p99, excluding the commit, is under 20 ms at 50,000 patients, with rows for every new page. On the last run: chart 15.1 ms (15.4 off-site), the clinician's home page 7.4, search 10.7, who viewed 7.4 as the patient and 9.2 as a proxy; the new pages of this phase between 5 and 11. **The chart is still the page nearest the gate on CI's runners.** The web job's time limit went from 30 to 45 minutes: its tests took 27 minutes 41 seconds on one run of this phase.

Each step was read by a review agent before it was merged, three to five times each, and every first review found real defects, fixed with tests; what was left open is in the log. That does not replace the human review above. Found only by a review, by CI or on the local stack, after the tests had passed: a portal session could read a note's sensitivity reason from the database; a link from another site completed a download after sign-in; printing hid the band that says whose record a proxy is looking at; a proxy was shown emergency-access views; a page's time told a proxy that one hidden view existed; a refill could be approved by an injection of another salt, and then beside an active order for the clinic's own item with no duplicate warning; an answer about self-harm could be stored with nobody told; a form's answers were unreadable at desktop width; the clinician's home page went over the gate when every audit insert began paying for the new column's check.

**Decided without Tahseen — to review.** Each step's entry in the decision log lists its own; these are the ones that change what the product does:
- 13a: what counts as an internal code in a summary, and its known limits (a code typed with a space or another alphabet's look-alike letter passes).
- 13b: the portal shows allergies, medications and diagnoses for the first time; after sign-in a link's query is dropped, for staff too (a patient search's criteria excepted); HEAD is refused on the download's pages only.
- 13c and 13c2: someone the record is shared with, without sensitive items, sees a staff member's view only if that person had the patient on their panel at that moment, never an emergency-access line, and is told a part only where it cannot hint at what is hidden; an assignment's start and end are set to the database's clock when more than a minute off, not refused; the part is a closed list in a database check.
- 13d: an approval is an order for the same product, so another strength is declined with a message and ordered separately; "already on order" goes by product for every order; a proxy asks only if their access includes messages; a request with nobody on the care team is refused, and one whose care team leaves waits for whoever joins.
- 13e: the safety path (the wording; 911 and 988; who is told, the every-clinician fallback, redelivery when an account is disabled; the way in from the inbox closing when the item is done); the patient never sees the score or band; a proxy answers only with sensitive items allowed, recorded as the proxy; a checked-in appointment's forms close twelve hours after the scheduled end; "Below mild" for the GAD-7's lowest band; forms are not in the FHIR API, the download or the desktop's offline copy.
- Process: several developer agents worked in parallel lanes; the full test suite is run once on a finished series, with CI on every push.

- **Title:** Expansion phase 5: SOAP notes, dictation proposals, ambient listening (switched off) and Spanish dictation (experimental)

**Description**

Phase 5 of `docs/expansion/PLAN.md`: steps 14 (SOAP notes and visit-type templates), 15 and 15.1 (dictation proposals beyond the note), 16 (ambient listening) and 17 (Spanish dictation), with step 18 inside it as said above. Everything a dictation or a recorded visit offers is still a proposal a clinician reviews; nothing is placed, saved or signed by the system.

What it adds:
- **SOAP notes** (UC 9, UC 10, UC 13, UC 14; FR-60 proposed): a note, written or dictated, has four sections, Subjective, Objective, Assessment and Plan, each required before signing. Notes signed before this step keep History, Assessment and Plan exactly as signed. A visit type shows prompts beside each section and never inserts text; "Vital signs for Objective…" offers the latest clinic day's readings, added only when the clinician chooses.
- **Dictation proposals** (UC 10, UC 13, UC 1, UC 4; FR-14 reworded): the dictation service proposes, by its own rules and never the model's, an order or an allergy to add that the clinician dictated plainly in its own sentence. The review shows each in the clinician's words, labelled "As heard:", with what was heard after it, labelled "Heard after it:"; it opens an empty order or allergy form that fills in nothing and signs or saves through every check it has, or is dismissed. Common names from a reference-data file ("penicillin", "latex", "CRP", "A1c") count as items.
- **Ambient listening** (new UC 23; FR-62 proposed): at the clinic only, after everyone who will speak agrees aloud (recorded in the audit log before the microphone opens), a clinician records a visit; after Stop they name every voice, and the note is drafted into the same review as a dictation, Subjective only from the patient's or a companion's words. Nothing is proposed for orders or the allergy list. The transcript is kept apart and deleted when the review is finished or 7 days after it was written. **It ships switched off** (`Ehr:Ambient:Enabled`, false).
- **Spanish dictation** (UC 9, UC 10, UC 13; FR-61 proposed, NFR-14 reworded): chosen for each dictation and **labelled "Experimental"** on the Dictate page; Whisper large-v3-turbo beside MedASR; the note's fields come out in English beside the Spanish transcript, each sentence checked against the Spanish it cites by closed word lists. No order or allergy change is proposed from Spanish. **A dictation in Spanish is limited to 2 minutes** (English stays at 3): the page counts down beside Stop and says so at the limit. A dictation made with English chosen is refused when fewer than two words in three are English.
- **Also in this branch, step 18** (new UC 24, FR-63 and FR-64 proposed): a web sign-in ends 15 minutes after the last request and 12 hours after it began, warned 2 minutes before; a recording counts as activity; what was typed is kept in the tab's memory for the same account; no signed-in page is cached; each emergency access goes to the administrators' inboxes and is reviewed once, with an outcome and a note.

**Before deploying, read these:**
- **Migrations:** `SoapNotes` (two columns on drafts and on signed notes, a template code on drafts, the reference table `ehr.note_templates`; it moves no row, says so in its report, and its `Down` refuses once anything is written in four sections), `AiChartProposals`, `AmbientListening`, and step 18's `EmergencyAccessReviews`, under the IDs in the table above.
- **Models,** downloaded by `src/Ehr.Dictation/setup_models.py`, which checks each one's commit, licence and SHA-256 against the Hub before it downloads anything: `openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo` (MIT, step 17) and `speechbrain/spkrec-ecapa-voxceleb` (Apache-2.0 by its card, trained on VoxCeleb, whose other publishers license their weights CC BY 4.0; step 16). **The dictation container's memory cap is 10 GB,** from its measured peak.
- **Listening ships switched off:** switching it on is the clinic owner's decision after reading the measured limits in UC 23. `Ehr:Ambient:KeepTranscriptDays` may only shorten the 7 days.
- **The eval's baselines were recorded on the developer Mac,** not the server: the English recordings (steps 14, 15 and 15.1), the visits (step 16) and Spanish run B (step 17). The server's are Tahseen's to record; until then a Spanish run from another machine fails the gate's machine check.
- **Wording no clinician has read yet:** step 14's Subjective and Objective definitions, the five visit types' prompts (every row "Not validated for clinical use") and the older-draft notice; step 16's consent statement and its guidance (a clinician's and probably a legal reading); every Spanish word list step 17 writes (a Spanish-speaking clinician's).
- **Desktops:** the offline copy shows notes in four sections, and older builds show Subjective and Objective under History; the desktop app's dictation stays English. Step 18 leaves the desktop app unchanged until step 19a.
- **The 12-hour sign-in limit and listening:** step 18's warning never ends a recording, and a dictation is refused when the 12-hour end would cut it off (for Spanish too, at English's 3 minutes).

**The dictation eval, as it stands** (every figure from synthetic recordings on the developer Mac; the rate on real dictation has not been measured):
- **English dictation** (100 recordings, `eval/results/latest.json`): word error rate 0.0818, field F1 0.737; no order or allergy text in any field. **Proposals: 18 right, none false or misheard,** of the 63 instructions the test set says plainly; the gate's floor is 14.
- **The trick sentences:** 96 of the 590 trick sentences are still proposed, 88 of them reversing the meaning, all accepted by name; the rate on real dictation has not been measured. Some retracted instructions can still be proposed. By family: A, the next sentence opens a step of the plan, 11 (one of them the condition, which doesn't reverse the meaning); B, a longer next sentence whose words of refusal, removal or doubt no list holds, 35; C, taken back two or three sentences on, 1; E, the next sentence an instruction of another form, 8; F, speech-recognition slips, 5; H, with the common names, 9; I, a heading as the next sentence, 2; J, done already, 7; K, given to someone else, 5; L, the wrong patient, 6; and the seven tolerated since step 15.1 (lead-ins with filler words 3, an everyday word the reference data names 1, "Prescribe water.", a dose corrected without a cue 1, a medication joined to a test 1, a request reported before 1). The gate fails on any other wrong proposal, on more than 96, on an accepted sentence no longer proposed, on a control not proposed at every instruction, and on fewer than 14 right proposals on the recordings.
  - **Two limits of "Heard after it:",** both following from where the reading stops: a retraction said after another instruction isn't shown ("Order a CBC. Her husband drove her in today. Her mother came along as well. Refill her lisinopril. She declined the blood draw." proposes the CBC with no refusal beside it), and neither is one under a new section heading ("Order a CBC.", then "Assessment:", then "She declined the blood draw.").
- **Listening to a visit, which ships switched off** (22 recordings): field F1 0.3155 (precision 0.6294, recall 0.2105); the patient's word error rate on clean visits 0.0752; words labelled with the right speaker 0.5882, Unsure 0.349. "No order shown" and "no question offered as a finding" are true of this run's drafts only: the checks find either only in the forms they read, MedASR writes no question mark, and 212 of the scripts' 554 questions are found by none of the rules.
- **Spanish dictation, experimental, 2 minutes at most.** The checks catch a change in the classes they compare and don't reliably catch a change of meaning said in other words: of the review's wrong translations, 41 of 70 pass without a warning, all reversing the meaning, and 2 of 30 faithful ones are refused. Run B (36 synthetic recordings): word error rate 0.1131, drug names 79 of 101 word for word; English content F1 0.4069 against 0.5, and 61 of 140 fields discarded against a third; 17 values counted as changed without a warning (8 Whisper's hearing, 9 the scorer's known limits); 2 of 134 statements misfiled; no instruction carried. The texts: F1 0.5921, 20 of 60 discarded, none changed. False refusals: 43 of 91 refused statements on the recordings, 15 of 25 on the texts. The hold-outs: h06 to h10 F1 0.4311, 12 of 20 discarded; h01 to h05 8 of 20. Whisper's errors that change the meaning, by sentence:
  - e01's "losartán" heard as "los Artan" ("los Sartan" in noise), so the English names a drug nobody said;
  - **e12's "Niega mareo, palpitaciones, dolor torácico y síncope." heard with a full stop after "palpitaciones", so the English, faithful to the transcript shown, states chest pain and syncope: a case only the reviewing clinician can catch;**
  - e04's "habones" heard as "abones", a change no check or scorer compares: a known limit.

  Spanish dictated with English chosen is refused when fewer than two thirds of its words are English (e01 at 0.477, the one recording of its kind; the English recordings' nearest 0.80), and so is a short English dictation more than a third of whose words no source holds; the clinician sees the transcript kept as plain text, and nothing else is lost. The 17 changed values and the 41 wrong translations are accepted by Tahseen as known limits, with the gate capped at these counts.

Steps 14 to 17 changed no authorization attribute or policy; step 16's change to `Program.cs` registers listening's switch and its transcripts' purge.
- Not touched: the sync conflict resolver.

Migrations since phase 4: `SoapNotes`, `AiChartProposals`, `AmbientListening`; and step 18's `EmergencyAccessReviews`.

Dependencies added: no library. Models: Whisper large-v3-turbo (MIT) and SpeechBrain's ECAPA speaker model (Apache-2.0), recorded in the models manifest and never committed. Data: SCOWL 2020.12.07 at size 50 (`englishwords.py`, its notice shipped beside it in the image; accepted by Tahseen on 2026-11-25 with its parts' terms recorded in `docs/expansion/reference-data.md`), the common names for proposals (`db/reference/dictation-names.toml`, hand-written, each with its source) and the visit types' prompts (`db/reference/37-note-templates.sql`). espeak-ng (GPL-3.0) makes the Spanish test audio on the machine that generates it and is never shipped.

Tests: 1,687 in the solution at step 17's merge, none failing there (performance cases are skipped without a benchmark results file, as in every phase); the sidecar's 2,812 Python tests, the eval's 2,678 and the setup script's 77. New in this phase: TC-UC14-26 to 35, TC-UC13-07 to 20, TC-UC10-07 to 21, TC-UC9-06 to 14, TC-UC1-23 and 24, TC-UC4-33 and 34, TC-UC23-01 to 06; and step 18's TC-UC11-08 to 15, TC-UC24-01 to 06, TC-UC12-08 and TC-UC15-09. The eval's gate checks the committed results on every push: English, the visits, Spanish and the trick sentences, as above.

The benchmark (`bench/Ehr.Bench --assert`) passes in CI with rows for the new pages: the note editor, with and without proposals waiting, and its vital signs for Objective; the review of a dictation, in English and in Spanish; the review of a 20-minute recorded visit; and step 18's session keep-alives and emergency-access review, under 6 ms. **The chart is still the page nearest the gate on CI's runners.**

Each step was read by a review agent before it was merged, most of them several times, and every first review found real defects, fixed with tests; what was left open is in the log. That does not replace the human review above. Found only by a review, after the tests had passed: no .NET test dictated an Objective, so three one-line mistakes in handling one stayed green (step 14); the first proposal rules decided "plain" by lists of what must not appear, and two reviews in a row found false proposals in fresh sentences, which made them a closed sentence (step 15); a line break hid every retraction (step 15.1); "Keep working" didn't keep the 12-hour warning closed (step 18). Found only by running the models: nine of the visits' drafts broke the gate (seven questions offered as findings), which stopped step 16 until its fix round.

**Decided without Tahseen — to review.** Every one, by its number in its step's decision-log entry, which gives each with the alternative rejected; the titles are the entries' own words, cut where long.
<details><summary>The list, by step (195 decisions)</summary>

  - Step 14, series 1 (the decision log, 2026-11-22, "step 14: SOAP notes and visit-type templates, series 1 as built"), 17: (1) Signed notes are not moved (D1); (2) Unsigned drafts are not moved in the database either (D2); (3) Read-only views show a draft or note as saved (D3); (4) All four sections are required to sign (D4); (5) Limits unchanged (D5); (6) The plain-text Body contract is kept (D6); (7) The desktop offline copy (D7); (8) A visit type is the template the clinician picks in the editor (D8); (9) No visit type shows the definitions alone (D9); (10) Vital signs for Objective (D10); (11) That read records the part `vitals` (D11); (12) Dictation in series 2 (D12, D13); (13) `Down` refuses once anything is written in four sections (D14); (14) Known limitations and the baseline (D15, D16; series 2); (15) The seed is unchanged (D17); (16) A section that shows nothing is blank (from the review of series 1); (17) The vital signs endpoint off-site is unchanged (from the review of series 1).
  - Step 14, series 2 ("step 14: series 2, dictation in four fields"), 5: (1) A review saved before step 14 is shown as it was saved; (2) The dictation scripts take their fields from the page; (3) The gold split, by the rule; (4) Known limitations (D15); (5) A dictation sorted whole into one field is offered with a warning (from the review of series 2).
  - Step 15 ("step 15: dictation proposals beyond the note, series 1 as built"), 17: (1) Where proposals come from (D1); (2) Two kinds (D2); (3) Words only (D3); (4) A rule that errs toward proposing nothing (D4, series 2); (5) A table of its own (D5); (6) Decided once (D6); (7) The normal forms with `?proposal=` (D7); (8) Nothing waits on them (D8); (9) Who (D9); (10) Off-site (D10); (11) Audit (D11); (12) The database's link (D12); (13) Staging (D17); (14) After a decision the review comes back with the cursor on the next proposal waiting (built for NFR-12); (15) The editor's wording (D19); (16) Desktop (D16); (17) The Dictate page (D18).
  - Step 15.1, the coordinator's rulings on its plan ("step 15.1: dictation proposals, the owner's follow-up to step 15"), 10: (1) the line-break hole is fixed in 15.1, with a family of trick sentences; (2) doubt or a hold after an instruction counts as reversing the meaning, so the gate's zero covers it; (3) "penicillin" stands for both penicillin G and V, with ICD-10-CM Z88.0 as its source; (4) "CRP" stands for LOINC 1988-5, the most ordered of LOINC's C reactive protein tests; (5) the cap on wrong trick proposals is 7, what the series leaves, though his words read literally give 8…; (6) the floor counts right proposals against the clinical gold, which the rule-following gold can't move; (7) the gate is his two checks as he wrote them; (8) "As heard:" is put before the words on the review and in each form's notice, in the style of a proposal's kind, the notice's sentence…; (9) the common names are a reference-data file, not a table, so no migration and no grants; (10) "A1c" is read as one word in a commit of its own, only by the closed sentence and its item check.
  - Step 15.1, as landed ("Tahseen: step 15.2 withdrawn; step 15.1 lands with a cap in place of zero"), 8: (1) the acceptances are in the trick set's own file, which the gate pins by hash, so any change to them changes the gate in review, and no…; (2) `most_wrong` is the number of sentences accepted by name, a test keeping them equal, and the gate reports how many of them reverse the…; (3) the sentence after a proposal is always shown whole, even when it is an instruction itself; (4) "a heading" is read as the hold-back rules read it, past heading lines ("Correction:", "Plan:") and stopping at another of the note's…; (5) the words are shown on the form's notice too, and whether the proposal waits or was decided, labelled "Heard after it:"; (6) staging refuses a proposal whose words heard after it aren't in the transcript after it, and a result stored before this shows none; (7) step 15.1's `_CONDITION` renamed `_ANY_CONDITION` in the merge; (8) No longer open: the unit of the reviewer's estimate was put to him, and he dropped the figure from the draft and the plan (above).
  - Step 16, series 1 ("step 16: ambient listening, series 1 as built"), 17: (1) Consent first, as a record the database checks (D4, D5); (2) Every chunk of audio is checked against its binding (D22); (3) Off-site: not offered (D9); (4) Desktop: not offered (D10); (5) What the page shows while listening (D7, D8, D25, A5); (6) Automatic stops (D7); (7) Withdrawal (D6); (8) Who each voice is: the clinician says, for every voice, with none chosen for them (D3 as amended by A1); (9) The conversation's transcript is kept apart, and only while needed (D11, D13); (10) After 7 days at most, the system deletes it (A2, replacing D11's "until someone finishes"); (11) Sensitive from the start (D12); (12) Off-site, a review whose transcript is kept can't be decided (A3); (13) A Subjective value is the patient's own words, and says so (A4); (14) No proposals beyond the note (D18); (15) English only (D23); (16) The backups keep 7 days at most (the coordinator's, after the second review: privacy first); (17) Listening to a visit ships switched off….
  - Step 16, series 2's review ("step 16: ambient listening, series 2 as built"), 4: (1) Instructions said to the patient (FR-14, the coordinator's rule, the review's F4); (2) A hedge or a denial said to the patient (the review's F1); (3) Scored word by word (the review's F2, accepted by the coordinator); (4) The visits' safety lists (the review's F3).
  - Step 16, the second review and first measurement run, 10: (1) Cues read as people speak; (2) Questions (new reason `source_is_a_question`, refused, not warned); (3) Reports (new reason `reported_dropped`); (4) Instructions as acts; (5) A turn a line; (6) Windows by tokens (the first measurement: four windows reached 90 s and two 78 s, losing 3 of 22 visits); (7) D16 as approved; (8) Never lose a run; (9) Measured with the sidecar's own cues; (10) Not yet decided, waiting for real data.
  - Step 16, the second visits run, 5: (1) Contractions counted as their words (A8); (2) Unsure by rule A; (3) A source across voices is cut (field F1, option 1); (4) The three safety cases, by their frame; (5) D17's measure.
  - Step 16, the record model session and its fix round, 5: (1) An order: a prescription sent on, found by its frame; (2) The lean: Subjective never quotes an Unsure passage in the clinician's voice; (3) Turn-taking where the voices weren't told apart, and answers as MedASR hears them; (4) A modal opening a sentence asks; (5) Not built: a turn boundary repaired.
  - Step 16, after looking at the pages, 1: A warning before the 20-minute stop.
  - Step 17, the approved plan's decisions ("step 17: Spanish dictation, the approved plan's decisions"), 62: (1) Whisper large-v3-turbo; (2) Whisper is optional at startup; (3) The dictation container's memory cap goes from 2 GB to 5 GB (MedASR about 0.4 GB, Whisper about 3.3 GB in fp32); (4) The language is chosen for each dictation; (5) The English path is unchanged; (6) A passage not heard as Spanish (Whisper's own language check, under half likely Spanish); (7) The live Spanish transcript shows finished passages only; (8) Hallucinations; (9) Spoken punctuation in Spanish; (10) A separate Spanish prompt and schema; (11) Statements, and a field fails whole; (12) Each field is decided on its own, with no extra checkbox; (13) Instructions dictated in Spanish; (14) What the note records; (15) The desktop app's offline dictation stays English; (16) Numbers; (17) Drug names; (18) Abbreviations; (19) Our own Spanish number reading; (20) The limits stay; (21) The eval; (22) The English eval is run once more; (23) Benchmark rows for the review page; (24) The test audio in Spanish; (25) Translated sentences; (26) No ceiling on misfiled values; (27) English "misfiled" read as near the Spanish as English allows; (28) "Consulta" is not something that can be ordered (the fix round's one list of what can be ordered, `spanish.ORDERABLE_WORDS`); (29) Where what a change is said of can't be read, only a change that goes the other way is refused…; (30) The rate of values filed in the wrong field (the coordinator's decision, 2026-11-24); (31) Where the English text run's rate lives; (32) A value is matched with what it is of (kind, the item it is said of, value: the facts' reading); (33) Accepted misfilings leave the count (`[[accepted_misfiling]]`, still the owner's alone); (34) `run_a.py` and `run_a_en.py` are left as they were; (35) A doubt before a cause ("probably due to"); (36) All 70 of the review's wrong pairs are marked as reversing the meaning; (37) The memory measurement; (38) The Dictate page's minute warning takes the Listen page's form (the coordinator's decision); (39) `setup_models.py --from-file --only whisper` is refused; (40) The held-out scripts are sealed in two parts; (41) A model's refused statement is a false refusal by the scorer's reading; (42) "Structure only" leaves out the scorer's readings as well as the checks'; (43) "might be" is read as doubt beside "may be" and "could be"; (44) The scorer reads "unlikely" as doubt only; (45) Run B stores the false refusals in its results (`false_refusals`, the texts' under `text`); (46) The format test pins on held-out scripts; (47) A hedge of leave never stands for a doubt the Spanish says (the fix round); (48) In the scorer a hedge of leave is no doubt at all, and a modal hedge said of nothing named satisfies no named item's doubt; (49) "unlikely" read against the Spanish only where the facts don't say whether the finding is negated; (50) A trend needs a named subject, and its direction is read against the Spanish's past or perfect forms…; (51) Where a value no gold has now falls; (52) The false-refusal report lists right refusals too, and marks the checks' reasons the scorer compares nothing of (`refusals.UNSEEN`); (53) The memory measurement sends its load from inside the dictation container…; (54) A held-out script's sources must be whole words of its text; (55) A doubted item's leaning is read from its own Spanish clause (the second fix round); (56) "not likely", "less likely", "not probable" and "less probable" lean against what they doubt; (57) A trend's pronoun, relative or missing subject takes the kind of the nearest name before its clause; (58) A value said of nothing named is backed only by an item named in its own sentence; (59) The held-out meta test's off-list drug is a made-up one ("zentarimicina"); (60) A token of digits glued to letters ("1C") is a word in the scorer's word error rate; (61) A trend's direction is read in the Spanish the statement cites where no fact entry covers it (h04's swelling); (62) Known scorer limits are named beside what they count (`scoring_es.KNOWN_LIMITS`, matched by script, kind and words).
  - Step 17, the last round, the coordinator's re-check and the merge with step 18 (the same entry's last blocks), 16: (1) the word list is SCOWL's, release 2020.12.07, at size 50, English, American, British and Canadian words, words with capitals and…; (2) the coordinator's: a dictation is refused when fewer than two thirds of its words are English, by principle 1, the more protective…; (3) the refusal is raised from the checks as a `StructuringFailed` (`NotEnglish`), which the session already maps to its reason, so…; (4) proposals are refused with the fields; (5) every word counts, a passage marked unclear's too, and numbers, codes with a digit, drug names, orderable and allergen words, clinical…; (6) a visit's window is never refused so; (7) the label is the choice's own words and a tag at the head of its hint; (8) the lines missed are recorded as `[[accepted_line]]`, not gated; (9) with no cap written and nothing accepted, the gate refuses each one found by name alone, without a second message for the cap; (10) the Spanish baseline is the developer Mac's run B, so a later run from another machine fails the gate's machine check until the owner…; (11) the coordinator's: the memory cap is set from the measured peak though the script called the load not whole, since every session's…; (12) ICD-10-CM's description words of four letters or more count as English, SCOWL stays at size 50, and the constructed Spanish sentence…; (13) the remaining exam words, British terms and names are left as measured, not added by hand; (14) the rule for words made of two English words stays: on the Spanish side's vocabulary…; (15) step 18's refusal near a sign-in's 12-hour end holds a dictation in Spanish to English's 3 minutes, the longest any dictation records…; (16) the Dictate page's language choice counts as typed for step 18's `session.js`, which reads a `select` as typing, so a page whose….
  - Step 18, also in this branch ("step 18: session timeouts and emergency-access review, as built"), 18: (1) 15 minutes idle for staff (was 30) and the portal (was 20), warned 2 minutes before, on both sites and for every role (D1); (2) The end is exact (D2); (3) 12 hours, whatever the activity (D9, his ruling 1); (4) What counts as activity (D3, his ruling 2); (5) The script asks the server before it warns and before it ends (D4); (6) The warning is a modal dialog (D5, a new `sc-dialog` component in the guide's style); (7) A sign-out never loses typed text: the page is kept in the tab's memory behind a sign-in (his ruling 2, replacing D6); (8) Without JavaScript; (9) The desktop app is unchanged (D8); (10) No page served to someone signed in is cached (D10, his ruling 1); (11) One review an access, by an administrator who isn't its clinician, once it has ended, with a required outcome and a note of 10 to 500…; (12) A restricted table of reviews, keyed by the access, with no write-audit trigger (D12); (13) Every administrator but the clinician gets an item, the review closes all of them, nobody ticks one off, and the migration job sweeps…; (14) What the reviewer sees (D14, his ruling 4); (15) The audit page (D15, his ruling 5); (16) Off-site (D16); (17) "Stay signed in" doesn't keep a paused recording; the warning says so…; (18) The TC-UC23-06 limit test, a follow-up beside step 16's TC-UC23-02….

</details>

Open questions for Tahseen, still open, from the log's step entries ("For Tahseen first" and "For Tahseen"):
- **Step 14:** a clinician's reading of the Subjective and Objective definitions, the five visit types' prompts and the older-draft notice; whether the off-site editor's vitals offer should answer while the clinic's server is down (it does, from the replica, and queues its read); d34's rule-following gold keeps one sentence with a date in Subjective.
- **Steps 15 and 15.1:** the three ways proposals are narrower than the design he approved, each the more protective and each his to widen (rules, not the model; the clinician's words and an empty form where he said "drafts"; orders and allergy additions only); recall, 18 of the 63 instructions said plainly, the rest listed as not carried over; the catalog work left (sulfa and the rest; EKG has no orderable to map it to); the cost of the strict rule, which holds back more instructions said beside a short or negated sentence; unpunctuated dictation proposes nothing.
- **Step 16:** "no order shown" and "no question offered as a finding" are true of the run's drafts only, with the options measured in the step's entry (accepting the cases instead of the fitted rules, the short-turn rule, the audio's pitch, the prompt); the lean changes a decided rule, for Subjective only; erasing the proposals of a withdrawn consent (today they stay as the review's evidence and in the audit log, marked sensitive); the 7-day default for a review nobody finishes; the consent statement and its guidance, for a clinician's and a legal reading.
- **Step 17:** a Spanish-speaking clinician's reading, before any real use, of every Spanish word list (`spanish.py`, `translation.py`), the false friends and opposites, and the Spanish prompt's rule 5 ("su madre…" written "her mother…" assigns a gender the Spanish doesn't give); espeak-ng (GPL-3.0) pronounces the Spanish test audio on the machine that makes it and is never shipped.
- **Every step's eval:** the server baselines, to be recorded on the server (above).
- **Step 18:** none open; its line-by-line read is owed here.

## Decision log

One entry per decision: date, step, the decision, and why.

- **2026-11-18, step 0. The plan starts from main at `b4ad35b`,** which includes the brand work (pull request 56). `expansion/integration` was created from it. No expansion branch or tag existed before, and step 1 had not started.
- **2026-11-18, step 0, correction (step 1). A discontinued order is stored with FHIR's request status `revoked`** and shown as "Discontinued". `Order.Status` holds FHIR R4 request status codes behind a check constraint (`OrderStatus.All`); FHIR has no `discontinued` code, and `revoked` is its code for a stopped request. "Resulted" (step 7) is `completed` for the same reason.
- **2026-11-18, step 0, correction (performance). Only the render benchmark is a CI gate today.** Page JavaScript size and first contentful paint are listed in AGENTS.md as gates to add "as the code they check appears"; nothing in `ci.yml` checks them. Each step measures both and records them in the log. Adding the two checks to CI is a follow-up for Tahseen to place: it isn't one of the plan's steps.
- **2026-11-18, step 0, correction (sensitive items). `docs/brand/README.md` says nothing about sensitive items.** Step 2 writes that section, in the guide's style, before building the screens, and the later steps follow it.
- **2026-11-18, step 0, correction (allergy codes). The allergy table was built for SNOMED CT codes,** and its "No known allergies" row uses SNOMED CT 716186003. The plan allows no SNOMED CT without approval, so step 2 codes allergens with RxNorm and UNII and replaces the "No known allergies" code with a marker of the app's own. No other SNOMED CT content is in the repo.
- **2026-11-18, step 0, correction (vitals). Blood pressure and heart rate are columns of `ehr.encounters`,** not observations. Step 9 moves them, and until then steps that show vitals read the visit's columns.
- **2026-11-18, step 0, correction (docs and tests). Not every use case has a `docs/UCnn.md` or a `TestCases/UCnn/` folder.** Docs exist for UC 1, 2, 6, 9, 10, 12, 13 and 14; test-case folders for UC 1 to 6, 11 and 12. A step that changes a use case without one creates it for what the step adds, and doesn't backfill the rest.
- **2026-11-18, step 0, note (requirement IDs). The plan's FR and NFR numbers are the ones the repo's test cases and docs use** (FR-05 orders, FR-14 dictation never orders, FR-16 to FR-19 the portal). They were matched to the course document in pull requests 52 to 55.
- **2026-11-18, step 0. Which CI run a step records.** The status table's record is itself a commit, so its own run can't be in it. A step records the run of its last commit before the record; the record commit changes only this file. After the merge, the run on `expansion/integration` is checked too, and a failure there is fixed before the next step starts.
- **2026-11-18, step 0, note (README). The README's "Not built yet" paragraph is out of date** (it still lists dictation's screens). Step 1 corrects it while updating the orders paragraph.

- **2026-11-18, step 1. Discontinuing is a column-level UPDATE grant with a trigger, not a `SECURITY DEFINER` function.** `ehr_app` may update `status`, `discontinue_reason` and `replaced_by_order_id` only; `orders_panel_update` limits rows to patients on the clinician's active panel; `ehr.discontinue_order()` runs as the caller and holds the update to active → revoked, once, with a reason, setting who and when. Nothing runs with more privilege than the caller, which is the narrowest that works. `RuntimeRoleTests` now lists every column grant (`ExpectedColumnPrivileges`) and fails on any other, because `has_table_privilege` doesn't see them.
- **2026-11-18, step 1. A change is enforced as a whole at commit.** A deferred constraint trigger refuses a replacement whose old order isn't discontinued and naming it; the discontinue trigger accepts "replaced by" only from the order that names this one, signed by the same clinician. A change keeps the order's type.
- **2026-11-18, step 1. Any clinician with the patient on their panel may discontinue or change a colleague's order,** and is the one recorded. That is the existing rule for who may order for a patient; limiting it to the signer would leave orders nobody on duty can stop.
- **2026-11-18, step 1. The reason for a change is optional;** without one the old order's reason reads "Replaced by a changed order". The reason for discontinuing is required, in the page and in the database.
- **2026-11-18, step 1. The chart links to discontinued orders and doesn't load or count them,** so the chart's render time is unchanged. The off-site sentence for the two actions is its own ("Changing or discontinuing an order needs the clinic's server."), beside the existing one that TC-UC1-04 and the brand guide quote.
- **2026-11-18, step 1. Orders aren't in the desktop app's offline copy** (they weren't before), so the desktop app didn't change. Whether offline charts should list orders is open for UC 7's owner.
