  - *Step 16,* ambient listening: approved (capture only after the patient's verbal consent is confirmed and audited; audio kept no longer than dictation keeps it; English first; the same review flow; synthetic voices only, clips under 1 MB each and 20 MB in total; a real voice needs his say-so).
  - *Step 17,* languages: Spanish dictation only (chosen per dictation; fields in English; the Spanish transcript kept and shown with source highlighting; an open-licensed multilingual speech model under the model rule; screens stay English; new wording for NFR-14 to propose; Spanish synthetic eval cases).
  - *Step 20,* desktop distribution: ship unsigned, with install instructions for the one-time warning; Tauri's updater, its private key in `~/.swiftchart-secrets/` with permissions 600 and its place told to him; updates tested against a local update server in the stack; nothing published; remote wipe as planned.
  - *Step 21,* deployment: prepare only (documentation, scripts in dotnet or PowerShell, Tailscale rules as config, optional open-source monitoring profile, an automated point-in-time restore drill; all tested locally; nothing hosted, no accounts, nothing spent).
  - *What may be added without asking:* models under MIT, Apache-2.0 or BSD up to 15 GB in total, each in the manifest with source, commit and SHA-256, never committed, a gated model or other license pausing the lane; datasets and code lists that are public domain, CC0 or CC BY; libraries under MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD, ISC or MPL-2.0, recorded here; teammates' areas, with the heads-up in the step's notes and the phase pull request draft.
  - *Deciding anything new:* patient safety and privacy first, then the course document, then the smallest reversible choice; each such decision recorded here under the heading **"Decided without Tahseen — to review"** with the alternatives rejected, and listed in the phase's pull request draft. A gate met by cutting the smallest piece that drops no requirement is recorded the same way; if only dropping a requirement would meet it, the lane pauses.
  - *What still stops a lane:* spending money or creating an account; a model, dataset or library outside the rules; a recording of a real person's voice; anything on main, a force-push, deleting a branch or tag, repo settings; CI failing after two honest attempts; a gate that can't be met without dropping a requirement.
  - *Effect on the plan:* the "asks first" notes are gone from steps 15, 16, 17, 20 and 21 in the status table, which now say what only he can do; "Stop and ask Tahseen before" points here.
- **2026-11-21, step 13b: download my record, as built** (proposed FR-56; extends UC 6, Simar's). From the coordinator's approved plan and six notes on it, then the independent review's nine findings (below). No migration, no new table, no new package; after the review, one `db/policies` change (`30-rls.sql`).
  - *What it is.* **Download my record** on the portal's home (`/portal/record`, `Areas/Portal/Pages/Record`) offers a copy of the record the session is looking at, as one FHIR R4 file (`?handler=File`) or as a page laid out to print (`/portal/record/print`). Both are GETs, under the Patient policy and `PortalSubject`, taking no patient from the request. What holds against another site (corrected after the review, which found the first wording false): the sign-in cookie is `SameSite=Strict`, so a link from elsewhere arrives signed out and is sent to sign in; both sign-ins, the portal's and (after the re-review) the staff's, at every step that ends one and when already signed in, go on to the page the link named without its query (`Links.LocalPage`, the only way they redirect; the staff sign-in keeps a patient search's criteria, `name`, `birthdate` and `mrn`, and nothing else), never to the file or to sensitive items; and no answer has a CORS header. Before that rule, signing in completed the link's download, sensitive items included, and a browser that also held a portal session could still be sent through the staff sign-in to it. The page that offers them reads nothing.
  - **The largest change in what a patient sees:** the portal showed no allergies, medications or diagnoses before this step (step 2's "the portal home shows the patient's own" was out of date). The copy holds them as the database's `*_own_select` policies let the portal read them, which is also what decides a proxy's: under either scope, the record; sensitive items only when the grant allows them.
  - *One mapping, two doors.* The FHIR mapping moved, mechanically, from `Api/Fhir` to `src/Ehr.Web/Fhir` (the capability statement stayed with the API, in `FhirCapabilities.cs`) and takes the ids it writes (`FhirIds`): the API's are the record's own, as before (`FhirIdsTests` holds every id and reference the API writes to the record's), and the download's are made for the file. `LayeringTests` keeps the portal off `Ehr.Web.Api`; ArchUnitNET doesn't see a dependency inside an async method's body. The PHI reader's FHIR reads were split into reads that record nothing, under the public methods that record, so a download records one event (`DownloadRecordAsync`); the printable page reads the summaries through step 13a's own reader (`PrintableRecordAsync` calls `VisitSummariesAsync`), so it records what the summaries page records, and 13a's fixes flow through.
  - *Audit.* Two new actions, `download` and `download_sensitive` (table `patients`, row the patient), accepted off-site by `CloudAuditEvents` in `read_sensitive`'s shape. Nothing in the database constrains or switches on an action's value (checked: no check constraint on `audit_events.action`; `record_write`, `shown_audit_details`, `withdrawn_summary_was_viewed` and the lab's `sent_to_lab` don't read these). The admin audit page's filter lists them. Step 13c maps the two to "your record, downloaded".
  - *Print.* One `@media print` block at the head of the shared stylesheet's pages layer (it has to win over the components layer), and a new class, `sc-screen-only`, noted in `docs/brand/README.md` ("Printed pages"). TC-UC6-33 checks print in both themes, that the screen is unchanged, and a clinician's flowsheet printed.
  - *Benchmark:* rows for the page, the file and the printable page, as the patient, as a proxy and for the seeded patient with the most entries (a second portal account), on-site and off-site; TC-UC6-32 holds them to NFR-01 and NFR-03. Not run locally (the coordinator's rule after the disk filled): CI's benchmark job is the gate.
  - **Decided without Tahseen — to review** (each by patient safety and privacy first, then the course document, then the smallest choice that's easy to reverse; each built as described):
    1. *A `collection` Bundle,* since nothing is authored for it. Not built: a `document` (needs a Composition the clinic would stand behind), a `searchset` (it is no search).
    2. *Ids made for each file* (`urn:uuid:`, random, different in every download), references to the file's own entries, the clinician named and not referred to, the MRN kept as the patient's own number. A recipient loses matching an entry across two downloads (the patient matches by MRN) and quoting an entry's id back to the clinic. Not built: the record's own UUIDs (version 7, so they carry creation times, and they are the clinic's keys); a keyed hash of them (stable, but a new secret and setting).
    3. *Visit summaries on the printable page only,* and the page says so. Not built: a `DocumentReference` in the file, a second mapping the API doesn't have.
    4. *A download is recorded apart from a view* (above); the printable page is a view. Not built: `details` on a read event (the cloud queue refuses details, and the admin page shows them); a made-up table name; an event for printing, which the server can't see.
    5. *No count of sensitive items left out, in the file or on paper:* a copy passed on mustn't say there are any. The page says so on screen before the download, and the printable page's "Sensitive items hidden" notice is screen-only. Not built: the API's OperationOutcome note saying how many were left out.
    6. *No cancellation reason in the file,* which the portal never shows. Not built: the API's appointment unchanged.
    7. *Allergies, medications and diagnoses included,* the portal's first view of them (above). Not built: leaving them out until a portal page shows them, which would leave the download without most of the record.
    8. *Changed while building, on the coordinator's third note:* the printable page shows a withdrawn summary's placeholder, as the summaries page does. The plan left placeholders out; with the summaries read through step 13a's reader, whose count of hidden summaries includes withdrawn sensitive ones, leaving them out would have made the page's count wrong or needed a query of its own. Not built: placeholders left out.
    9. *Caps:* 600 of each kind, as a search of the API, results and vital signs apart so neither crowds out the other, the newest kept, with an OperationOutcome warning in the file and a sentence on the page; the newest 50 visit summaries, as the summaries page reads them, with no sentence (its reader doesn't say how many there are). Not built: one cap for all observations; no cap.
    10. *A GET form with an "Include sensitive items" box,* offered to the patient and to a proxy the patient allowed sensitive items, never to another proxy. Not built: two links for each output.
    11. *The file's name, `health-record-<date>.json`,* the date in UTC, no name, number or ID. Not built: a name with the patient's.
    12. *Print colors by setting the color tokens again for print* (to `brand-navy`, mixes of it, or nothing), so a dark screen prints dark on white with no raw color. Not built: a print theme in `tokens.json` and the brand tool.
    13. *An architecture test* that the portal uses none of the APIs' code.
    14. *After the review: the portal's medication policy narrowed* (`db/policies/30-rls.sql`, `medication_statements_own_select`, now also `status <> 'entered-in-error'`), by rule 1, the more protective option: a portal session, the patient's or a proxy's, could read a medication entered in error, so "never an entry in error" rested on the PHI reader's filter alone. Clinicians' and write policies unchanged; nothing else reads medications as a portal session. Not built: leaving it to the app's filter. Allergies, diagnoses and appointments have no such status; results already had their release rule.
    15. *After the review: the portal's sign-in drops the whole query of the page it goes on to* (`Links.LocalPage`), not only `handler` and `sensitive`: no portal page needs a query to open, and a query is what lets a link choose for the person. Not built: removing only the two parameters; leaving the redirect as it was.
    16. *After the review: HEAD of the file and of the printable page is refused* (405, `Allow: GET`) before anything is read, as the FHIR API refuses every method but GET. Not built: answering 200 with the GET's headers and no body (it would say what the file is without having read it); leaving it to the GET handler, which read and recorded for nothing sent.
    17. *After the review: the bands print.* The first build hid them, so a proxy's printout no longer said whose record it was and an off-site one lost its read-only warning. Not built: hiding them.
    18. *After the re-review (the coordinator's decision, the more protective option): the staff sign-in follows the same rule,* at its signed-in GET, after the code and after a first enrolment ("Continue"): a staff link can't show a clinician a chart, a list's history or a note with sensitive items, recorded as their view, and a browser holding a portal session can't be sent through it to the portal's file. `Links.Local`, which kept the query, is private, so a sign-in can only go on through `LocalPage`. One staff flow needed a query to survive sign-in, the patient search's: signed out on `/patients?name=…&birthdate=…`, the clinician comes back to the same search after signing in (`LoggingTests` holds it). Its criteria, `name`, `birthdate` and `mrn` (`SearchQuery.Parameters`), are neither a handler nor a request for sensitive items, so the staff sign-in keeps those three and nothing else, as the coordinator's decision allowed. What that leaves: a link can still have a clinician run a search, each patient listed recorded as their read, as before this rule. Any other page that comes back after a session ends lands without its query (the schedule's day, the audit page's filters). Not built: stripping only `sensitive` and `handler`, which leaves the next such parameter open; dropping the search's criteria too, which breaks the search's return.
  - *Resting on the app alone (the review's finding, recorded):* whether a download is recorded as `download_sensitive` rests on the page's check that the session may see sensitive items (`MaySeeSensitive`: the patient, or a grant with `include_sensitive`). With that check removed, a proxy without sensitive items who asked got none from the database, which withheld them, but the event said `download_sensitive`. The record can then say more was handed over than was; the copy never holds more than the database allows.
  - *After the review* (failed on two findings, a link from another site completing a download with sensitive items and the bands hidden in print; the clinician FHIR API found byte-for-byte unchanged over 38 requests, row-level security holding with the app's checks removed, fresh ids in every file, and 14 of 18 mutations caught):
    - the portal's sign-in goes on to the page alone (15; TC-UC6-35), and the two statements that a link from another site couldn't start a download now say what holds: `SameSite=Strict`, no CORS header, and the sign-in rule;
    - the bands print (17; TC-UC6-33 prints a proxy's portal pages and an off-site chart);
    - HEAD is refused (16; TC-UC6-29);
    - the medication policy (14; `ProxyAccessSecurityTests`);
    - the printable page's visit summary cap is said on both pages (TC-UC6-31);
    - tests for two gaps the mutations found: a download event in a shape a read may have is refused (`CloudAuditTests`), and the printable page's summaries are compared, item by item, with what Visit summaries renders (TC-UC6-26), until the two copies are folded into one after step 13a merges;
    - TC-UC6-33 also prints the printable record without sensitive items, whose notice doesn't reach the paper.
  - **Open, for Tahseen:** HEAD of any other page runs its GET handler and records its read, as GET does; on step 13a's Visit summaries that includes a read of each summary, which then counts as viewed when one is withdrawn. An app-wide refusal was looked at after the re-review and stopped before building: TC-UC2-07 (step 10c) checks that a HEAD of a chart off-site is answered 200 with its read written to the cloud queue before the response, so the change would alter an audit test's expectation. Nothing else sends HEAD to a signed-in page (the stack's health checks, CI's checks, the desktop app). Waiting for the coordinator's call.
  - *Tests:* TC-UC6-24 to TC-UC6-35; TC-UC11-07 (a link through the staff sign-in, after the re-review); `FhirIdsTests`; a line each in `FhirPlanTests` (the download's reads), `CloudAuditTests` (four shapes refused), `LayeringTests` and, after the review, `ProxyAccessSecurityTests`. No test about authorization, PHI or the audit log was weakened.
  - *UC owners:* UC 6 (Simar): a link on the portal's home, new pages and a section of `docs/UC6.md`; no existing page, date format or test case changed. UC 18 (Tahseen's): two lines of `docs/UC18.md`. UC 19: a paragraph of `docs/UC19.md`. UC 11 (Luke): the staff sign-in goes on to its page without the query, and test case TC-UC11-07 (after the re-review).

- **2026-11-21, step 13e: pre-visit forms, as built** (new use case UC 22; proposed FR-59). From the coordinator's approved plan, with its rulings on the license and the migration order and its changes A to H. A migration (`PreVisitForms`, after step 13d's: two tables, and `inbox_items.sensitive`, the kind `form-alert` and a check that every `form-alert` item is sensitive on step 3's table; no data moves). **Protected path: `db/policies`** (`85-pre-visit-forms.sql`, new; the forms' parts of `10-grants.sql`, `30-rls.sql`, `40-audit.sql`; after step 13c, a line of `42-record-views.sql`); **authorization:** `requestId` among the IDs a portal handler may take (`PortalSubjectTests`), and, from the step's review, a form's answers opening to a clinician who holds its inbox item open (`Forms/Response.cshtml.cs`, `PhiReader.FormAnswersAsync`).
  - *The questionnaires* are worded exactly as the authors' English forms on phqscreeners.com, with LOINC's codes, hand-written in `db/reference/36-questionnaires.sql`; the PHQ-9's bands from Kroenke et al. 2001, Table 2; the GAD-7's cutpoints and "Needs follow-up" at 10 from the authors' instruction manual. `docs/expansion/reference-data.md` quotes every statement about using them, with addresses, the date read and each PDF's SHA-256, the conflicting "Copyright © Pfizer Inc. All rights reserved." of LOINC's notice and the 2001 article included. *The coordinator's ruling:* not a stop, since the plan's own list of sources names them "free to use" and the publisher's manual says public domain; the conflict is for the phase 4 pull request draft. Step 2a's LOINC names, its GAD-7 prompt and its unsourced GAD-7 "Minimal", and the made-up range of up to 4 on the two totals, are gone.
  - *The database:* `ehr.questionnaire_requests` (a copy of the definition as asked) and `ehr.questionnaire_responses`, both always sensitive, no row ever updated or deleted by anyone, the owner included (`TRUNCATE`, the owner's alone and run by nothing, is what a row trigger can't stop). `ehr.questionnaire_score` works out the total, band, follow-up and alert; the guards fill in every other column whatever a client sends; the response guard refuses a form that doesn't exist, another patient's, one held back from a proxy without sensitive items, one not open yet and one closed with one and the same error. An owner-run trigger puts a sensitive `form-alert` item, naming no questionnaire, answer or score, in the inbox of the appointment's clinician and every clinician with the patient on their active panel whose account isn't disabled, or, when that finds nobody, of every clinician whose account isn't disabled; a second, on the panels, gives a clinician who joins one for each answer none of the team holds open (`ehr.follow_questionnaire_alert_care_team`, in the manner of step 13d's `ehr.follow_refill_care_team`, reading the answers through an owner's policy). They name only the inbox, the panels, the staff accounts and, for the second, the answers (a test), and step 12's catalog guard still lists only the lab's two functions.
  - *Pages:* the clinician's Pre-visit forms page and a form's answers (hidden until asked, then each visit's score, band, follow-up, "An answer needs review: question 9", who answered, and a trend through step 9's `_Trend` as a screening value of 10); the inbox lists alert items first, and its link opens the answers to whoever holds the item; the chart links to the forms with their count and shows a recipient "Needs review:"; the desktop's offline copy counts them as sensitive items not kept. The portal's home link (always there for the patient and a proxy allowed sensitive items, with a count while forms are open), list and form (`sc-choice`, a new radio-group component), the answers shown and never a score, and a "Not sent" page when a form can't take them; the Access page says sensitive items include pre-visit forms; "Download my record" names them among what a copy never holds.
  - *Change H, as built:* a request keeps a copy of the questionnaire's definition when it is asked for; the answers are scored against that copy and every page shows it, so a later change to the reference data never rewrites what someone answered. This makes the version or hash the coordinator named, and refusing a change to an asked-for definition, unnecessary: the reference data may change, and reaches only forms asked for afterwards. A definition that has been asked for can't be removed (the requests' foreign key). Its cost: one JSON column on each request.
  - *Test cases:* TC-UC22-01 to 12; `PreVisitFormSecurityTests` and `QuestionnaireDefinitionTests` in `Ehr.Security.Tests`; `PhiReaderTests` and `PortalSubjectTests`. The 50,000-patient benchmark is CI's: its rows "Pre-visit forms…" (clinician, three) and "Portal pre-visit form(s)…" (portal, three), on-site and off-site.
  - **Decided without Tahseen — to review** (each built as described; the alternatives not built):
    1. *The help the portal gives, and 911 and 988, are the coordinator's* (its ruling A), for a synthetic clinic in the United States, kept in one place (`FormNotices`): "Not monitored: your care team reads this form before your visit, not when you send it. If you need help now, call 911. You can call or text 988 at any time to talk with someone at the 988 Suicide & Crisis Lifeline." on every form; and, whenever the PHQ-9's question 9 was answered other than "Not at all", first on the page: "If you are thinking about hurting yourself, or feel you might act on these thoughts, call 911 or go to the nearest emergency room now. You can call or text 988 at any time, day or night, to talk with someone at the 988 Suicide & Crisis Lifeline. Your care team will read your answers before your visit, but not as soon as you send them." (for a proxy, worded about the patient throughout, from the step's review: "If <name> is thinking about hurting themselves, or you think they might act on these thoughts, …", "<name>'s care team will read these answers before the visit, but not as soon as you send them.", and "Not monitored: <name>'s care team reads this form before the visit, not when you send it. If <name> needs help now, call 911. …"). The question names its notice (`help: self-harm` in the definition), so a questionnaire's alert question brings its own; a name the portal doesn't word fails `QuestionnaireDefinitionTests`. On the "Not sent" page the notice leaves out the sentence about the care team reading the answers, which weren't sent. **They should be read by a clinician before any real use.** Not built: an inbox item for a high total without the question-9 answer; a shared "reviewed" record clearing everyone's item; paging or texting anyone.
    2. *The GAD-7's band below 5 is "Below mild",* the clinic's wording, said so in the reference data and on the clinician's page, because the paper that may name it (Spitzer et al., *Arch Intern Med* 2006) answered with a browser check. Not built: "Minimal" (unsourced). The coordinator asks Tahseen for the paper.
    3. *Not in the FHIR API or "Download my record"* (ruling C): leaving sensitive answers out of more doors is the more protective choice. Not built: QuestionnaireResponse in either, for Tahseen to decide.
    4. *Questionnaires are data* in step 2a's table, single-choice questions only, added as reviewed reference rows. Not built: a screen to write one; free-text, multiple-choice or skip-logic questions; tables of items.
    5. *Scores are not observations:* no reference range, never High or Low; "Needs follow-up" at 10, as step 9d's screening values. Not built: observations of category `survey`, which reach the portal at once and the FHIR API's Observation search.
    6. *Asking* is the clinician's, on the patient's forms page, for a booked appointment that hasn't ended, once for each questionnaire. Not built: on the booking form; automatic by visit type (there are none); asking again; withdrawing a request.
    7. *The window:* from 7 days before the appointment until its scheduled end, booked or checked in, on the database's clock, the portal showing when it opens and closes; and, from the step's review, a checked-in appointment's forms stay open past the scheduled end while its visit is in progress, closing when the visit ends or, on the coordinator's ruling that they must have an end, 12 hours after the scheduled end, whichever comes first; a booked appointment's still close at the scheduled end. The 12 hours are the window step 9c's vital-signs rule gives a visit. The coordinator's first choice, the end of the clinic's day, was not built: the clinic's time zone is the app's setting (`Ehr:Clinic:TimeZone`, step 10d), which the database is never given and a session's own `TimeZone` can't stand in for, so the guard couldn't hold it exactly; the pages show the 12 hours' end as the closing time. No page ends a visit yet, so for now the 12 hours decide. Not built: open from the request (two weeks' recall goes stale); closing at check-in; no end; the end of the clinic's day (it needs the zone in the database, as a setting the migration job writes).
    8. *One sitting, sent once, never changed.* Not built: drafts on the server or in the browser; a resend replacing the first (it would let a question-9 answer be overwritten).
    9. *Proxies* answer only with sensitive items, recorded as themselves and shown as a proxy's: to clinicians as "<name> (proxy), not the patient", and in the portal, from the step's review, as "you", "the patient" or "someone the record is shared with", never by an account's name. With sensitive items the home's link is always there, whatever forms there are; without them, a record has no forms, no count and no link. Not built: any proxy; no proxy; a separate permission to answer.
    10. *The patient sees the answers, never a score or band.* Not built: showing them.
    11. *Opening the portal's forms page is the asking* for sensitive items, recorded as such, unlike step 13a's summaries' link, since every entry is sensitive and some are to do.
    12. *The portal writes through a policy and a trigger that runs as the caller.* Not built: an owner-run function, which needs owner-only policies on the new tables.
    13. *Recipients of an alert,* **reversed in the step's review** (the more protective option): the appointment's clinician and the patient's active panel (an ended assignment never counts), a clinician locked out for minutes included, a disabled one not, each marking their own item done; when that finds nobody, every clinician whose account isn't disabled; and a clinician who joins the patient's panel while none of the team holds an answer's item open is given one. The join's trigger takes no lock, unlike step 13d's refill trigger beside it: an answer arriving at the moment of a join is delivered to the team it sees or, seeing nobody, to everyone, the one joining among them, so the race can only add an item, and nothing closes another's form-alert item (`PreVisitFormSecurityTests` checks a join delivers a waiting refill and an unreceived alert once each, under 13d's one lock). From the step's second review (decision 18): an account disabled while it holds an answer's open item, with nobody else on the team who can sign in holding it, is followed at once by the same rule, and a joiner doesn't count a disabled holder. An answer is still stored with no item when no clinician account at all is enabled: nobody could read it. The first choice, nobody told when nobody on the team could be, let an answer to review be stored with no one told (the reviewer's case: the asking clinician off the panel, the appointment's clinician disabled). Not built: admins told, also when no clinician account is enabled (they can't read the answers); closing a leaver's item.
    14. *Every write of a form, and an alert's inbox item, is marked sensitive* through a `sensitive` column (on `inbox_items`, set only by the database). Not built: a special case in the write-audit trigger.
    15. *A form's 404:* the portal answers a send to a form that doesn't exist, another patient's, or one held back with the same 404, after the same statements (TC-UC22-06). From the step's review, a send to a form the session can see that isn't open, closed or not yet open, is answered 409 with a page that says the answers were not sent and why ("This form can no longer be filled in." or "This form can't be filled in yet."), keeps the "Not monitored" notice, and leads with the help an answer called for; so does a send the database refuses as the form closes. An unsent answer alerts nobody: nothing is stored. Treating "not yet open" as "closed" here, so a reschedule while the patient is filling the form in doesn't end in a blank page, is the lane's choice. A send with a question left blank, asked again, also leads with the help. "Answers sent:" is worded for a proxy too ("<name>'s care team will go over them at the visit.").
    16. *An answer to review is read from the inbox* (from the step's review, an authorization change; **narrowed by the coordinator** in the second review): a clinician who holds an open `form-alert` item for an answer opens that one form's answers from it without claiming emergency access, until they mark it done, after which they reach it as anyone else does (the panel, or emergency access with a reason; from the step's third check, the Done tab keeps the item's line without the link, and says "Open from the patient's chart." only to a clinician with the patient on their panel), as the inbox says beside "Mark done": "After you mark this done, the answers open from the patient's chart, which needs the patient on your panel or emergency access." With the every-clinician fallback, keeping the door open after "done" would have let every enabled clinician read the answers for good. The item is found through the PHI reader under the inbox's own row-level security, asked for as the reader's own and still open, and the read is recorded as a view of sensitive items. Nothing else opens that way: the chart, the forms page and the patient's other forms keep the panel-or-emergency-access gate, and the chart's "Needs review" notice shows only where the chart opens. Off the panel, the page says whose patient it is (the care team, by name) and that the chart opens only under emergency access. Not built: treating the appointment's clinician as care team for the whole chart (it would open every part of the chart for a booking, not for one answer); leaving the door open once the item is done.
    17. *The reviewer's observations, accepted as built:* a clinician is shown how many forms a patient has before asking for sensitive items, which hints that screening happened, the same pattern as step 4's "Sensitive items hidden: N"; the portal home's link and count, for a proxy with sensitive items as for the patient, are recorded as the home's plain read, not a view of sensitive items; and the synthetic seed has no forms, so the 50,000-patient benchmark's rows are its own.
    18. *An account disabled while it holds an answer to review* (from the step's second review): the answer is told again at once by the rule that first told it, written once (`ehr.tell_care_team_of_answer`) and run both when an answer is stored and from an owner-run trigger on `staff_users` that fires when `lockout_end` moves to the disabled range (ten years or more; the Staff page's disable writes it through Identity as `ehr_app`, and the trigger sees any route that writes the column, not only the page). Unless a clinician of the team who can still sign in holds it open, it goes to the team's enabled clinicians, or to every enabled clinician; the disabled account keeps its own item; a lockout of minutes after wrong passwords isn't a disable; enabling an account again does nothing, and disabling it again tells nobody twice. An answer being stored and an account being disabled share one advisory lock ('form-alert holders': shared by answers, so they don't wait for each other, and alone by a disable, taken after the account's row), so neither can miss the other. A clinician joining the panel counts only holders who can still sign in. From the step's third check: a lockout of minutes takes no lock (a test holds it to that, so a failed password never stalls an answer); a join takes the lock shared before it reads, so a clinician disabled as they join can't be left an answer's only holder, and takes it before 13d's care-team lock, which never waits in a circle with it, since its only taker alone, a disable, waits for nothing while holding it (both files say so; a test runs join, refill ask, answer, disable and leave at once, round after round); and an answer waits for it at most 5 seconds (lock_timeout for that step, put back after), then is stored rather than refused or lost behind an admin's open transaction. From the step's last check (the more protective option), an answer that didn't get the lock in time is told by the rule and also to every enabled clinician without an open item for it, so the race is closed on that path too: it can't rest with an account being disabled at that moment alone. What remains is one transaction disabling every clinician account the answer sees, held open past the 5 seconds, which leaves nobody who can sign in, as when no clinician account is enabled; a clinician enabled or joining later is given it. The extra items are the price of a rare path. The Staff page's disable holds it for one request; only an admin's own open SQL session could hold it longer. A join waits for it without a bound, as it waits for 13d's lock: a clinician's request, not a patient's. Not built: the redelivery from the admin action alone (a disable by any other route would be missed); closing the disabled account's items.
  - *UC owners:* UC 6 (Simar): a home link, a sentence of "Download my record" and of `docs/UC6.md`; and, after step 13c, "A pre-visit form" among the who-viewed parts. UC 4 (Diego): the chart's link, count and notice. UC 7/8 (Sara): the offline copy's count of sensitive items not kept (web side only). UC 15 (step 3's table and kind check, and the inbox's order), UC 16 and UC 18 (Tahseen's proposed use cases): a few sentences each.

- **2026-11-21, Decided without Tahseen — to review: parallel lanes, and "which part" in two steps.**
  - *Parallel lanes.* Tahseen asked whether independent tasks could be done by several agents in parallel; I read that as "do so". Within a phase, independent steps now run in separate developer lanes, each in a clone of its own from the same base, each reviewed and merged one at a time. About three at once: with two lanes running tests together this Mac's load passed 30 and test containers timed out. Later phases are not pulled forward, since a phase's branch should hold that phase's work only (his rule for phase 2.1). *Rejected:* one lane at a time (slower, nothing gained); every remaining step at once (false failures, and migrations from many lanes to reconcile).
  - *Step 13c says "Your record" for most views, and step 13c2 follows.* His words for 13c are "staff name and role, when, and which part". The audit log records most reads as a read of the patient's row, whichever page was opened, so the page can tell notes, messages, visit summaries, downloads and the lab apart, and nothing else. Recording which page was opened means touching about thirty reads in `PhiReader`, the cloud queue's allow-list and the admin page: done at the same time as 13a's fixes and 13b it would put three lanes in the same lines. So 13c is built on what is recorded, and 13c2, in this phase and before its tag, records the part. *Rejected:* delivering phase 4 with "Your record" only (it drops part of what he asked for); holding 13c until the others merge (nothing gained, the page and its database door don't depend on the finer parts).

- **2026-11-21, step 13d: refill requests, as built** (proposed FR-58; new use case UC 21, "Answer a refill request"; extends UC 6, Simar's, UC 1 and UC 15). From the coordinator's approved plan and its two additions (a test that an order signed in an earlier transaction can't answer a request, and the harmless concurrent case stated in `docs/UC21.md`), first on a provisional base of step 13a's fixes merged with step 13b, then replayed on step 13c for its review round (below). One migration, `RefillRequests` (the table; no data moves). **Protected path: `db/policies`** (`77-refill-requests.sql`, new, with triggers on `ehr.patient_assignments`; the requests' grants in `10-grants.sql`, their policies in `30-rls.sql`, the audit trigger and the exclusion from `ehr.shown_audit_details` in `40-audit.sql`, the refill rule in `70-inbox.sql`, and the requests' part in `42-record-views.sql`). No authorization attribute or policy changed: the pages fall under the Portal and `/Patients` folder conventions.
  - *What it is.* "Renew a medication" on the portal's home (`/portal/refills`) lists the record's renewable medications and its requests; the patient, or a proxy whose access includes messages, asks for one with a note if they like. Each clinician with the patient on their panel whose account an admin hasn't disabled gets a "Refill request" item; the request's page (`/patients/{id}/refills/{request}`) offers "Approve: write the order", which is the order form with `?refill=<request>`, or a decline with a message. The database (`ehr.guard_refill_request`) sets who asked and when and copies the medication's name and sensitivity; holds an answer to once, by a panel clinician, in READ COMMITTED; holds an approval to an active medication order of the patient signed in the same transaction by the clinician answering, for the catalog item under the medication's own code, one order a request, with the medication locked and still active; and refuses every other change and every delete. `ehr.deliver_refill_request` (owner-run) delivers and clears the inbox items, and refuses a request nobody would be told of; `ehr.follow_refill_medication` (owner-run) keeps a request's sensitivity its medication's; `ehr.follow_refill_care_team` (owner-run) keeps the items with the care team as the panel changes.
  - *Changed from the approved plan, on step 13a's later rule (the more protective option):* the portal reads no row of `ehr.refill_requests`. The plan had a policy, `refill_requests_own_select`; step 13a's review round had since moved the portal's summaries behind an owner-run function because row-level security can't keep some of a row's columns from a session that may read the row, and a request's row holds who answered it and which account asked, which the portal doesn't show. So the portal is given its requests by `ehr.portal_refill_requests`, under the same rules the policy would have had (the record the session is looking at, access with messages, sensitive items only where the patient's rule allows). Not built: the policy.
  - **Decided without Tahseen — to review** (each by patient safety and privacy first, then the course document, then the smallest choice that's easy to reverse; each built as described):
    1. *A page of its own in the portal,* linked from the home. Not built: a medications page with a button each (there is none); asking through Messages (free text the database can't hold to a rule).
    2. *Only active medications coded as a product the clinic orders* (since the review round, below), as the session may read them (never one entered in error; a sensitive one only under the patient's rule); one with no code, or coded by its ingredient alone, is not offered, and the page says to call. Not built: uncoded or stopped medications.
    3. *Sensitive medications and requests left out until asked for,* the asking recorded as `read_sensitive`; a request's sensitivity is its medication's, kept in step by the database, so a proxy without sensitive items loses one at once and its earlier audit events are marked. Not built: shown at once with a tag; a request's sensitivity fixed when asked.
    4. *Proxies ask only under access that includes messages:* asking is writing to the care team (UC 18 gives that to the messages scope), and the decline is the care team's message. Not built: any proxy; no proxy.
    5. *An optional note of up to 500 characters, and nothing else;* no pharmacy (nothing sends to one). Not built: a required reason, a pharmacy, a quantity.
    6. *One open request a medication* (a partial unique index), and no other limit. Not built: a daily cap like messages'.
    7. *The care team is told:* every clinician with the patient on their active panel whose account an admin hasn't disabled, following the panel as it changes (both since the review round, below); a request nobody would be told of is refused, and the portal says to call. Not built: the prescriber (a medication on the chart records none, and only a panel clinician may sign the order); the panel unchecked; a request nobody sees.
    8. *A refill item is done only by answering,* refused from the apps in the database, as a result item is. Not built: "Mark done".
    9. *Approving is the order form,* every check of an order with it (the duplicate's and the override's reasons kept), signing the order and answering the request in one savepoint; the database holds the order to the same product, signed now by the clinician answering. **Reversed in the review round** (below): built first as the same ingredients, in another strength or form too. Not built: any medication order (a substitution would read as a refill); approving as a change of an active order (two links on one form).
    10. *The patient sees what was ordered,* the catalog item's name the database keeps on the request, never the order's details.
    11. *Declining needs a message of up to 1,000 characters, kept on the request.* Not built: a step 5 message thread (no sensitive mark, so a decline about a sensitive medication would reach a proxy with messages but without sensitive items; and the answer apart from the request). **The message is not checked for internal codes,** as a visit summary is: it is the clinician's own words to the patient, like a step 5 message, and the check would cost a rule of its own.
    12. *The portal names "Your care team", never the clinician;* the patient sees a proxy's request as "by someone you share your record with", a proxy only their own as "by you". Not built: naming the clinician; naming the proxy to the patient.
    13. *Email:* the patient's own confirmed account gets step 13a's "There is something new in your patient portal" when a request is answered, at most one a day for these answers (the first of the clinic's day, since the review round), with a link to the portal's home (`/portal/refills` would say what it is about); never a proxy; nothing on asking. Not built: one per answer (the hourly cap password resets share); emailing the proxy who asked.
    14. *A medication stopped or entered in error leaves a waiting request open,* which the database won't approve (the medication locked while approving); the page offers only Decline, so a clinician tells the patient why. Not built: closing it by itself (no reason, no email, the item gone unanswered).
    15. *The request keeps the medication's name as asked.* Not built: reading it from the chart (an entry in error would take the answer out of the portal).
    16. *The medication is named in the form by a reference protected with Data Protection for the record being looked at;* no ID on the page; the database checks again. Not built: its UUID (step 13b's reasoning); its place in the list.
    17. *Each new page is one recorded read of the patient* (`read_sensitive` when it shows a sensitive medication or request), as the order form and chart-entry forms are, so step 13c shows "Your record" until 13c2. Not built: a read of each request.
    18. *Admins never see a request's values* (`ehr.shown_audit_details` leaves the table out, as messages). Not built: waiting for step 18a.
    19. *FHIR Task's status codes,* `requested`, `completed`, `rejected`, shown as waiting, approved, declined. Not built: the app's own words; MedicationRequest with intent proposal (no rejected).
    20. *Not in the desktop app, the change log, the FHIR API or the downloaded record.*
    21. *Not seeded:* the benchmark asks through the app's own services. Not built: seeding (three more triggers lifted, which AGENTS.md lists).
    22. *HEAD refused (405, `Allow: GET`)* on the two new pages that record a read, as step 13b's are.
    23. *Requests are not on the clinician's chart.* Not built: a tag on the medication list.
    24. *The request's page needs the patient on the clinician's panel;* emergency access goes to the chart.
  - *`?refill=<request>` on the order form is never needed across a sign-in:* the staff sign-in drops a link's query (step 13b's last round), so the clinician approves again from the request's page; `docs/UC21.md` says so.
  - *Step 13c's part rule:* `refill_requests` is named in `ehr.record_view_part` as the record, in the database commit now that the series is on step 13c (its walk over `ProtectionTests`' list fails otherwise); no read is recorded against the table, and step 13c2 will name the part.
  - *Gates:* the benchmark is left to CI, with rows "Refill request, a patient on the panel", "Order form approving a refill, a patient on the panel", "Portal refills, the patient's own" and "…, as a proxy", on-site and off-site (TC-UC6-56, TC-UC21-05).
  - *UC owners:* UC 6 (Simar): a page, a link on the portal's home, a section of `docs/UC6.md` and TC-UC6-51 to 58; and, in the review round, the visit summaries' line on email, now once on each of the clinic's days. UC 1, UC 15, UC 18 and the new UC 21 (Tahseen's): the order form's refill path and its test case, the inbox's refill item, a line of `docs/UC18.md`, two triggers on the panel's table (`ehr.patient_assignments`) that keep refill items with the care team, and, in the second review round, UC 1's duplicate rule for every order. UC 4 (Diego): a trigger on `medication_statements` that carries a medication's sensitivity to its requests, and a line of `docs/UC4.md`. UC 14 (Satwik): step 13a's "something new" email moved into `PortalNotices`, and, in the review round, sent once on each of the clinic's days instead of once in any 24 hours (below). UC 7 and UC 8 (Sara): nothing in the desktop app.
  - **Review round, 2026-11-21: decided without Tahseen — to review** (on the reviewer's findings, the coordinator's fixes; each built as described, with a test):
    1. *An approval renews the same medication product, reversing decision 9.* The database holds the order to the catalog item whose code is the medication's own RxNorm code; another strength, form, salt or route, and the clinic's own item for the same drug (an item of its own), are refused (`refill_requests_same_medication`), and the form's item is fixed: "It renews the same medication: <name>." To change the medication the clinician declines with a message and writes a new order from the chart. A medication coded by its ingredient, or with a code the catalog doesn't hold, has no product to renew: the portal doesn't offer it ("call the clinic") and the database refuses a request for it (`refill_requests_medication_coded`). Why: the reviewer's probe approved a request for 24 HR metoprolol succinate 50 MG Extended Release Oral Tablet with a metoprolol tartrate injection, and oral hydrocortisone with a topical or injected one; the same ingredients are not the same treatment. `ehr.renews_same_medication` is gone, and the catalog is looked up by its key. Not built: the same ingredients in whatever strength or form (the first rule); the same ingredients and dose form (needs RxNorm's dose forms, which the catalog doesn't import). *What it opened* (the second review): the clinic's common items are RxNorm products under codes of their own (SYN-MED-001 "Lisinopril 10 mg tablet" is 314076), and the duplicate check compared catalog codes, so an approval, always RxNorm's item, beside an active order of the clinic's item signed a second lisinopril 10 mg order with no reason. Before the reversal the form had filled in the clinic's item, so the check fired; TC-UC21-02 was then moved to the RxNorm item, which hid it. Closed at the root, for every order, in the second round (1, below).
    2. *The items follow the care team.* Two owner-run triggers on `ehr.patient_assignments` (`ehr.follow_refill_care_team`): a clinician who joins the panel gets an item for each waiting request, never a second; one who leaves has theirs done. A request with nobody on the panel stays waiting and goes to whoever joins next; the patient's page is unchanged. Why: a request asked while one care team was on the panel waited for good once they had all left and someone else joined. Not built: closing such a request by itself; telling the patient.
    3. *A clinician locked out for a while counts as on the care team; one an admin disabled doesn't,* by `80-appointments.sql`'s test (`lockout_end` unset or less than ten years ahead), in the database and on the portal's page. Why: a clinician locked out for 15 minutes after failed sign-ins made the page say "No care team yet:" and the database refuse.
    4. *Lock order: the medication, then the request.* Approving takes the medication's row lock and advisory lock before the request's row (`RefillRequestQueries.LockMedicationSql`, run by `ClinicalOrders`), the order marking the medication sensitive takes them in, so the two wait rather than deadlock (40P01). `77-refill-requests.sql` says so.
    5. *Every waiting request reaches the portal,* whatever the page's limit of 50 on answered ones (`ehr.portal_refill_requests`): which medications wait no longer depends on the 50 latest requests.
    6. *After a refused decline the cursor is in the message,* not on "Approve" (NFR-12).
    7. *Once a day is once on each of the clinic's days,* from midnight in its time zone (`PortalNotices.StartOfToday`), for refill answers and for step 13a's visit summaries alike, in one commit (UC 14, Satwik). Why: counting the last 24 hours held back the first event of a day that came less than 24 hours after the one before.
    8. *A test that the order's signer is the one answering:* nothing held it in the tests before (the rule was there).
    9. *Left as they are:* a refused decline shows the page again and records a second read, which is what happened (`docs/UC21.md` says so). Observed, not changed: in a 400-patient smoke run of the render benchmark, ASP.NET Core's antiforgery logged its warning that it overrode `Cache-Control` and `Pragma` 64,906 times in the finished log (57,966 when counted before the run ended): pages that set `Cache-Control: no-store` themselves and render a form token get the warning on every response, with the same effect (`no-cache, no-store`). A follow-up could set that value or quiet the log category.
  - **Second review round, 2026-11-21: decided without Tahseen — to review** (on the re-review's findings, the coordinator's fixes; each built as described, with a test):
    1. *Already on order means the same product, for every order* (UC 1's rule, `db/policies/60-orders.sql`, and `PhiReader`'s order form, together, so the warning and the refusal agree): the same catalog item or, for a medication, another item for the same RxNorm product (an item's `standard_code` where its `standard_system` is `rxnorm`, which for RxNorm's own items is their code). The duplicate's reason works as before: given, it is kept; missing, the order is refused. Another strength or drug is not a duplicate. The gap was there for any order, not only refills: ordering RxNorm's item while the clinic's was active, or the reverse, needed no reason. The lookup is a primary-key probe of the catalog for the new item and for each of the patient's active orders: no index. Not built: accepting the clinic's own item as an approval's product (it would leave ordinary orders with the gap).
    2. *An approval renews what was asked for.* The database copies the medication's code onto the request when it is asked (`medication_code`, in the unmerged `RefillRequests` migration, changed in place, and `schema.sql`), and an approval needs the ordered product to be both that code and the chart's current one. A clinician may change an entry's code (`65-chart-lists.sql`): a request asked before is then only declined, and the request's page says "the medication on the chart has changed since this was asked". Why: the reviewer changed amlodipine 5 mg to 10 mg on the chart, approved the 5 mg request with 10 mg, and the portal said the 5 mg request was approved with 10 mg ordered. Not built: following the chart's entry as it now stands (what the probe found); closing the request when the entry changes (no reason given to the patient).
    3. *A patient's care-team lock* (an advisory lock on `'ehr.refill_care_team:'` and the patient's ID): asking (`ehr.deliver_refill_request`) and a clinician joining the panel (`ehr.follow_refill_care_team`) take it before they read, so of the two at once one waits and then sees the other's write. Why: the reviewer held an ask open three seconds while a clinician joined; neither saw the other, and once the only other holder left, the request waited with no open item while a clinician was on the panel. It comes last in the lock order (`77-refill-requests.sql`), after the medication's: nothing holding it waits for a medication's lock or a request's row, and a test runs asking, marking the medication sensitive and joining at once. One lock for each patient whose panel a transaction joins: the benchmark's panel of a hundred is a hundred.
    4. *The same lock when a request is answered and when a clinician leaves* (beyond the brief, in a commit of its own to keep or drop): a clinician who joined as a request was answered was given an item the answer never saw, open for good, since no one may mark a refill item done; one who left as a request was asked was given an item for a patient no longer theirs.
    5. *A test that approving through the app's own code takes the medication's locks first* (`RefillApprovalTests`): without the call in `ClinicalOrders`, it ends in a deadlock.
    6. *The duplicate check is FR-05,* not FR-37 as `docs/UC21.md` and TC-UC21-02 had it.

- **2026-11-22, step 14: SOAP notes and visit-type templates, series 1 as built** (proposed FR-60; extends UC 14 and UC 13, Satwik's; UC 7/8, Sara's, for the offline copy; UC 6, Simar's, and UC 12, Luke's, for a new read's part). From the coordinator's approved plan (decisions D1 to D17) and its five additions (every read with its part; the summary rule's index probes and look-alikes for the new sections; the database's clock in the tests and the clinic day from `ClinicTime`; no path that loses an earlier draft's text; forced row-level security put back after a refused `Down`). The data model and pages only: dictation, the structuring prompt, the note schema and everything under the eval's hashes are untouched, so CI's eval check holds; series 2 (the dictation's four fields and the regenerated eval) waits for the GPU run the coordinator schedules. One migration, `SoapNotes` (columns and checks; the reference table `note_templates`; no data moves). **Protected paths: `db/policies`** (`10-grants.sql`: SELECT on `note_templates`; `50-clinical-notes.sql`: `ehr.finalize_clinical_note` checks the four sections and refuses a History; `52-visit-summaries.sql`: `ehr.visit_summary_problems` compares the new sections too).
  - **For Tahseen first: a clinician's reading is needed** of the Subjective and Objective definitions the editor shows (`NoteSections.Definitions`; series 2 puts the same words into the note schema), the five visit types' prompts (`db/reference/37-note-templates.sql`, every row marked "Not validated for clinical use"), and the notice an earlier draft opens with ("Check: this draft was written before notes had four sections. Its History is now in Subjective: move today's vital signs, examination findings and the results you reviewed to Objective before signing.").
  - **Decided without Tahseen — to review** (the plan's decisions, as built):
    1. *Signed notes are not moved* (D1). A note signed before keeps its History, Assessment and Plan and is shown under those headings, with "Written as History, Assessment and Plan, before notes had four sections."; the migration changes no row and reports the count kept. *Rejected:* renaming History to Subjective (History held today's vital signs, examination and reviewed results, so it would call findings subjective and rewrite what was signed); splitting History automatically (free text, and lifting the immutability trigger to do it); copying old notes into new SOAP notes (two records of one visit).
    2. *Unsigned drafts are not moved in the database either* (D2). The editor puts an earlier draft's History in Subjective with that notice; opening it changes nothing, and the first save writes the four sections and clears the History in one update; the database signs no draft with a History, and no note with one. *Rejected:* moving History into Subjective in the migration (a silent relabel, every draft's version advanced and every desktop sent it again); showing History read-only beside empty sections (text lost if saved without copying it).
    3. *Read-only views show a draft or note as saved* (D3): History, Assessment and Plan for earlier ones (off-site with the server down, the confirmation, a review's "Current saved note"); a desktop-edited draft's plain text under "Draft text". Only the editable form maps History into Subjective.
    4. *All four sections are required to sign* (D4), as all three were. *Rejected:* an optional Objective (ambiguous between not examined and forgotten; the telephone template asks for "No examination is done on a call: say so").
    5. *Limits unchanged* (D5): 16,000 characters a section, 64 KiB the note.
    6. *The plain-text Body contract is kept* (D6): four headings, each on a line before its text; the desktop's draft code and the sync conflict resolver are unchanged; a desktop edit comes back into Subjective as it came into History. *Rejected:* parsing headings out of Body (could misfile clinical text); a SOAP starter text on the desktop (imported whole into Subjective anyway).
    7. *The desktop offline copy* (D7): `CachedNote` gains Subjective and Objective (null for earlier notes), and History carries a four-section note's Subjective and Objective under their headings, so a build from before this step shows all of it; no SQLCipher schema change. *Rejected:* a versioned endpoint (more surface); a sections array (duplicates the whole note); dropping History for new notes (older builds fail to parse the panel and keep a stale copy).
    8. *A visit type is the template the clinician picks in the editor* (D8): reference data, prompts shown under each section's definition, never inserted; the draft keeps the choice, the signed note doesn't. *Rejected:* a type on appointments or visits (changes UC 16); starter text (unedited template text signed as findings); templates in code; the visit type on the signed note (easy to add later).
    9. *No visit type shows the definitions alone* (D9): Assessment's and Plan's are the note schema's words (a test holds them to it); five visit types to start, follow-up, new patient, acute, well-child and telephone.
    10. *Vital signs for Objective* (D10): only when asked; the latest clinic day on which a vital sign stands, the newest of each sign that day, a blood pressure as one reading, none in error, each time labeled, "Not today:" when it isn't; added only by "Add to Objective", at the end, after a blank line; the note keeps the text. The clinic day is each moment's calendar day on the clinic's clock, so the hour repeated when the clocks go back changes nothing (the open question on that hour isn't made worse; each time's label says which hour). *Rejected:* a visit's readings (a note has no visit; that link is a change of its own); the newest of each sign whatever its day (mixes days); filling Objective unasked; range flags in the text.
    11. *That read records the part `vitals`* (D11), as the vital signs page's does; the cloud app's allow-list already has the shape. *Rejected:* `note-draft`.
    12. *Dictation in series 2* (D12, D13): the same checks over four fields; Subjective and Objective 3,000 characters each in the schema; one prompt for every visit type. Until then a dictated History is labeled "History (goes into Subjective)" on the review and goes into Subjective once approved, as reviews saved before step 14 will too; series 2's guard refuses new History proposals. *Rejected:* a prompt per visit type (multiplies the eval).
    13. *`Down` refuses once anything is written in four sections* (D14): a signed note can't go back to History without changing it, and drafts' sections would be dropped. It lifts forced row-level security on `clinical_drafts` for that check alone, as the table's owner, and puts it back before refusing; a test proves the table is still enabled and forced after a refused `Down`.
    14. *Known limitations and the baseline* (D15, D16; series 2): the d22 entry's field renamed if the same recording shows the same value in Subjective, any new unflagged value going to Tahseen; the eval run on this Mac, labeled as before; the server baseline best recorded after step 14.
    15. *The seed is unchanged* (D17): it seeds no notes; the benchmark writes its own four-section notes and drafts, and measures the editor and the vital signs offered, on-site and off-site.
    16. *A section that shows nothing is blank* (from the review of series 1): one whose only characters are whitespace of any kind Unicode counts, or the characters that show nothing which step 13a's summary rule strips (zero-width spaces, joiners, direction marks, variation selectors, tags and the like), isn't signed, in any of the four sections. One definition in the database, `ehr.note_section_is_blank` in `50-clinical-notes.sql`, which the signing rule calls for each section and whose list of those characters a test holds to the summary rule's; the app's `NoteContent.IsBlank` is the same rule, checked against the database's on every code point. Only signing checks it: what is saved is what was typed. *Alternative:* leave it as it was, whitespace alone (`[[:space:]]` in the database, `string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace` in the app), under which a section of one zero-width space passed both, and one of a no-break space passed the database's.
    17. *The vital signs endpoint off-site is unchanged* (from the review of series 1): with the clinic's server down the editor, and so the offer, isn't shown, but the endpoint still answers from the cloud copy and queues its read for the clinic's audit log, as the other reads do off-site; the off-site benchmark rows measure that outage view.
  - **Fixed after the review of series 1** (review commits on series 1): the vital signs offered read every standing value of the latest clinic day, not the newest 120, so no sign taken that day, and no half of a blood pressure, is left out: the newest moment, then that day's bounds on the clinic's clock, two statements, each a range of `ix_observations_patient_id_effective_at` (on 116,200 vital signs, 16,200 of them one patient's over 600 days, as `ehr_app`: an index scan backward with a limit of one, cost 0.94..2.01, 0.9 ms cold; then a bitmap index scan of that day's 27 values, cost 24.07..1309.54, 0.2 ms), asked once more when a value marked in error between the two leaves that day with none, so the offer is then the day before's, not none; they are offered only for a patient on the clinician's panel and the clinician's own unsigned draft of that patient, with nothing read or recorded otherwise, under an `h2` (the page checks the panel and the draft, and the drafts' row-level security the panel again; both read the same assignment, so no test can tell the page's panel check from the database's without weakening the test database's policies, which the tests don't do: they check an ended assignment, which both refuse); Ctrl/Cmd+S saves again (the shortcut sent the form's first button, which had become "Vital signs for Objective…"); an earlier draft within the four headings' few extra bytes of the 64 KiB limit is told "This draft is at the size limit: shorten it by a few words to save it in four sections.", and the limit isn't raised; a failed save without JavaScript shows an earlier draft's notice again; decision 16. New tests: `NoteVitalsTests`, `NoteBlankTests`, forced row-level security after a successful `Down`, TC-UC6-15, 22 and 26 checking the note's Objective, and an addendum and a summary on a note signed before step 14.
  - **Found while building, not changed:** a section posted with a null character is refused with 400 and no body, not with the editor's own message ("Remove the null character from …", which `NoteContentTests` checks); nothing is saved either way, and TC-UC14-28 says so.
  - **Tests:** TC-UC14-26 to 34, TC-UC13-07, `SoapNotesMigrationTests`, `ObjectiveVitalsTests` (the repeated hour), the note, summary and plan security tests' new cases, `AuditPartTests` rows for the editor and the vital signs. The editor's page JavaScript is about 4 KB compressed (`note-editor.js` 1,919 to 2,710 bytes); its first contentful paint, in Chromium against the in-memory app, about 20 ms warm and 180 ms cold.

- **2026-11-22, step 14: series 2, dictation in four fields** (proposed FR-60; extends UC 9 and UC 10, Samir's, and UC 13, Satwik's). On series 1's head: the note schema and the structuring prompt in Subjective, Objective, Assessment and Plan (Subjective's and Objective's descriptions are the editor's definitions; the rules are unchanged), the sidecar's fields and limits (3,000 characters each for Subjective and Objective, History's; the checks unchanged in strength), its tests and pinned contract files, the web contract and the Dictate and Review pages and scripts, the eval's loader, tests and the 42 scripts' gold, `eval/clinical-gold.md` regenerated, and the eval run again on the four fields (`eval/results/latest.json`). **Protected paths:** the dictation prompt and note schema (`src/Ehr.Dictation/schema`), and `db/policies/55-ai-review.sql` (`ehr.guard_ai_field_proposal` stages only the four fields).
  - **Decided without Tahseen — to review** (as built):
    1. *A review saved before step 14 is shown as it was saved:* its History, Assessment and Plan, the History labeled "History (goes into Subjective)", source highlighting kept (the web reads a stored result's History; the service never sends one). *Rejected:* refusing such reviews (they would block their drafts' signing until discarded, which a pending review can't be); relabeling History as Subjective on the review.
    2. *The dictation scripts take their fields from the page,* so the Dictate page and either kind of review render with the same code.
    3. *The gold split, by the rule:* what the patient or a carer reports, readings taken at home included, is Subjective; today's measurements, the examination and results the clinician reviewed (older ones included) are Objective. Sentences were moved, not reworded, but for five that held both, split at a clause (d03, d09, d13, d15, d26). d34's clinical gold keeps its dated results whole in Objective ("Blood results from November 9. Cholesterol 6.8, LDL 4.4, HbA1c 44."), not the date in Subjective and the values undated in Objective, as the split first had it (from the review of series 2); its rule-following gold, which moves whole sentences, still has the sentence naming the date in Subjective. Judgment calls, each for a clinician's reading: comparisons of today's measurements with the last visit (d01) and weights not said to be today's (d02, d41) as Objective; scores recorded at the visit, a questionnaire's included (EPDS d07, PHQ-9 d26, EASI d06, PASI d24, SALT d32), as Objective; reported function and appearance (walking with one stick d05, skin better d15) as Subjective; a mental state examination's reported voices (d17) kept whole in Objective; a diagnosis line naming its test (ejection fraction d12, amyloid PET d18, PYP scan d29) kept whole in Subjective; a carer's or teacher's account (d04, d18, d35, d40) as Subjective; marking a wound's edge (d22) as Objective. d33's Objective and d39's Subjective and Objective are empty (both were already tagged missing-field). `clinical-gold.md` now reads 63 of 161 clinical gold values that the checks would discard (47 of 121 before).
    4. *Known limitations* (D15): the run shows d22's babble-5db "metoprolol" in Subjective, the same value from the same recording, and nothing else unflagged, so the entry's field is now `subjective`, with a note; the reason and the owner's acceptance are unchanged.
    5. *A dictation sorted whole into one field is offered with a warning* (from the review of series 2). In the run, d39 (a quick dictation with no history) came back with its whole transcript, its plan included, word for word in Subjective, and the other three fields not dictated; the checks pass it, since every word is grounded. The reviewer counted 15 misfiled sentences of 812 offered across the run: d39 eight, d12 three, d02 two, d27 one, d33 one; plan text sat in Subjective or Objective only in d39. Built: `one_field_holds_dictation` in the sidecar's checks (`validation.py`), a warning on the only value offered, whatever became of the other three (not dictated, or proposed and discarded by the checks, as d39's Plan was in the three-field run), whose sources cover at least 90% of the transcript's words (`WHOLE_DICTATION`); it points at what they cover, and the review shows it beside the field: "This section holds the whole dictation. Sort it into the sections it belongs to before accepting it." Accepting it is still the clinician's choice. It fires on d39 alone in the run (15 of 236 offered values now carry a warning, 14 before), not on d33's addendum, which is all plan but quotes 78% of its words. *Not taken:* changing the structuring prompt (it needs a model run, and may not fix it).
  - **For Tahseen:** the warning is advice, not a stop: a clinician can still accept a whole dictation into Subjective, type a token Plan, and sign with the real plan in Subjective. Whether signing should ask for more is a product decision.
  - **The committed three-field results, to read the run against** (2026-11-15, Apple M5, not a baseline): note-field content F1 against the rule-following gold 0.6677 overall (History 0.5783, Assessment 0.8243, Plan 0.7945), detection F1 0.8273; against the clinical gold 0.6621 (History 0.5694, Assessment 0.8228, Plan 0.7900); word error rate 0.0834; order or allergy changes shown 0; unsupported words shown 0.0.
  - **The run on four fields** (2026-11-22, Apple M5, 24 GB, macOS 26.5.1, the developer Mac, not the server; llama-server b11669 natively, `--reasoning off`; no thermal warning, load average 2.6 as it started; the same audio as before, made again): every recording sorted; order or allergy changes shown 0; unsupported words shown 0.0; retracted values shown 7, 1 of them unflagged (the d22 entry). Content F1 against the rule-following gold 0.7382 overall (Subjective 0.6460, Objective 0.7844, Assessment 0.8552, Plan 0.8007); Subjective and Objective together 0.6913 scored field by field and 0.7128 merged, against History's 0.5783. Detection F1 0.8438 (235 found, 1 wrong, 86 missed); Subjective 0.7287, Objective 0.8060, Assessment 0.9324, Plan 0.8904. Against the clinical gold 0.7305 (Subjective 0.6345, Objective 0.7718, Assessment 0.8537, Plan 0.7984), scored again after d34's gold was corrected (0.7311 before). Word error rate 0.0834, dates and drugs as before (the audio and transcripts are the same). 236 of 281 proposals passed the checks (170 of 217 before); 14 of 236 offered values carried a warning, 15 since decision 5's warning (scored again, no model run). Structuring: mean 22.4 s (21.5 before), max 55.3 s; two-minute dictations 31.0 s on average; the two 3-minute scripts 55.3 s and 48.9 s (mean 52.1; 49.8 before). The overall figures aren't strictly comparable with the three-field run's: the gold now has 321 field values where it had 241. The one wrong detection: d39 (a quick dictation with no history, so empty Subjective and Objective in the gold) came back with its whole transcript, its plan included, in Subjective, word for word, which the checks pass, and Assessment and Plan empty; three fields that failed the checks before. Decision 5 puts a warning on it.
  - **Tests:** TC-UC10-07 and 08, TC-UC13-08 and 09 (the keyboard review of the four fields), TC-UC13-07 now on a review saved before step 14; the sidecar's, the eval's and the setup script's tests.

- **2026-11-22, step 15: dictation proposals beyond the note, series 1 as built** (FR-14 reworded, its row under "Requirements to propose"; extends UC 13 and UC 14, Satwik's, UC 1, Tahseen's, UC 4, Diego's, and, with series 2, UC 10, Samir's). From the coordinator's approved plan (decisions D1 to D19) and its additions: the stub proposes both kinds wherever a test follows a proposal to an order or an allergy; step 13d's cases for "now"; two tabs at once; the order form's checks unchanged; a sign-in drops `proposal`; every read with its part; the review's section at 1,280 and 640 pixels in both themes; the new table named in `42-record-views.sql`. Series 1 is the database, the staging, the two forms and the pages: the dictation service sends no proposals until series 2 (its rules, the eval's cases, scoring and gate, and the run), so the feature is dormant and the committed eval results are untouched. One migration, `AiChartProposals` (the table; no row moves). **Protected paths: `db/policies`** (`10-grants.sql`, `30-rls.sql`, `40-audit.sql`, `42-record-views.sql`, `55-ai-review.sql`); not the dictation prompt or note schema, which don't change, nor any authorization attribute or policy (the pages fall under the clinical area's convention), nor the sync conflict resolver.
  - **For Tahseen first: this is narrower than the design he approved, in three ways,** each chosen as the more protective option, and each his to widen:
    1. *Proposals are found by rules, not by the model.* The dictation service's own checks (`validation.py`) propose a cautious subset of the instructions they already find and keep out of the note (`directives()`, the "not carried over" list); the model is never asked, and the prompt and the note schema are unchanged. *To widen:* an array of proposals in the note schema and a prompt rule asking the model for them (the approved plan's Appendix A has the drafts), each still held to an instruction the rules find; a model run for every change to the prompt; about 2 to 5 seconds more on a 3-minute dictation.
    2. *A proposal carries the clinician's words only, and opens an empty form.* His words were "as drafts": a proposal is a draft of what to do, not a draft order. Nothing in the order form or the allergy form is filled in from it, not the type, the item, the allergen, the dose, the route or the frequency; the clinician picks and types them, with the words beside the form. *To widen:* an item or allergen suggested by an exact match of the words against the catalog or the allergen codes (exact matches pass a misheard drug that is another real drug's name, hydroxyzine for hydralazine, and speech recognition had 0.64 of drug names right in step 14's run), or details read from the words (doses are what corrections and misheard numbers change most); each a lookup and a rule of its own, and the eval scoring what it fills in.
    3. *Only orders and allergy additions are proposed.* His words were "orders and allergy changes". Removing, resolving or changing an allergy, and stopping or changing an order, stay listed as not carried over, for the clinician to do by hand, as every instruction was before: a removal or a stop heard wrong weakens a safety net without anyone noticing (an allergy removed takes step 8's check off every later order), where an addition errs toward more warnings. *To widen:* a proposal kind for each, mapping the words to the entry or the order it is about (an allergy on the list, an active order), opened in the entry's change form or step 1's discontinue form, with eval cases of their own.
  - **For Tahseen: what the rules can't promise** (the third review's residual limits, recorded, not fixed). Words of retraction, doubt or refusal no list will finish ("Nah.", "Oops.", "Hang on.", "Just kidding.", "Wrong patient entirely.", "She is not willing to have blood drawn today.", "Questionable."); a report before an instruction that isn't on the list ("Mom insists. Order a CBC."), and someone else's reaction before an allergy to add; contrived items built from catalog words that still begin with a whole name, and everyday words that are items ("Prescribe water.", water being an ingredient RxNorm names); a mishearing that lands on another catalog item (hydroxyzine for hydralazine), which is proposed as said; and the cue gaps the review found at both commits ("versus" or "vs", "differential", "query", ", I suppose", "to be confirmed", "pending", "apparently", "not clear that", "92 or so"); a finding in a sentence about someone who isn't family (a classmate, a roommate, "the patient in the next bed", "the paramedics were told..."), read as the patient's when the value's source quotes that sentence, since only family words mark someone else, so the third review's six someone-else cases are refused only when the quote is exactly the words the two sentences share; the list rule's false refusals, on the safe side ("No chest pain, mild shortness of breath on exertion.", "No vomiting, loose stools for two days.", "No fever, cough and congestion for three days." each discards what follows the comma); short dictionary words that are brand or product names in the reference data, one-word items beside air, water, fat, yeast and option ("Order act.", "Refill align.", "Prescribe react.", "Order pronto."); lead-in artefacts ("So, will, order a CBC."); and "call in" with a time of day ("I'll call in the morning."), which isn't proposed but is still listed as an order not carried over and cut from the note. The reviewer's judgement, not a measurement: roughly 1 to 2% of proposals false after the third round's fixes, on dictation with spoken punctuation. What makes all of it tolerable: a proposal only ever shows the clinician's own words, and opens an empty form.
  - *What it does:* the review lists, under "Proposed orders and allergies", each order or allergy to add the dictation proposes, in the clinician's words marked in the sentence they said them in and the one before, with any warning and its words, the way into the order form or the allergy form, and "Dismiss proposal"; the forms show "Proposed from dictation: check every field. Nothing here was filled in from it." with the words, fill in nothing, and sign or save through every check they have, deciding the proposal in the same savepoint, then return to the review with the cursor on the next proposal waiting. A proposal is decided once; none holds up the review or signing, and signing decides none: the editor and the signed note tell their author how many wait. Off-site the section is read from the cloud copy and its actions are hidden while the clinic's server is cut off.
  - **Decided without Tahseen — to review** (the approved plan's decisions, as built):
    1. *Where proposals come from* (D1): the rules above; rejected, the model extracting them, a second model call, every medication started in a plan.
    2. *Two kinds* (D2): an order of any type the order form takes, and an allergy to add.
