Resetting the local stack first is his call: its database can't be migrated forward (above), and a reset deletes its synthetic data and accounts. If Safari won't offer a passkey on the stack's sites, `mkcert -install` trusts its certificate authority in the System keychain, with his Mac's password.
  - **CI's first run on the pushed branch** ([45718664981](https://github.com/tarnan3751/EHR-Electronic-Health-Record-Project/actions/runs/45718664981)): TC-UC11-16 failed at its second password step, back on `/sign-in`. The test, not the sign-in: its helper typed the email as soon as the page had loaded, relying on the field's autofocus, which Chromium applies at its next rendering after the load, so on a slow machine the first key landed before it and the email lost its first character ("Wrong email or password."). Reproduced with Chromium's CPU slowed 8 and 16 times (4 of 12 password steps); the helpers now wait for the page's own focus before typing (16 of 16). Its benchmark job's failure, on a row this step doesn't touch, was the runner's noise (the coordinator).
  - **Final review** (one reviewer, on e82b4de, 2026-11-25): "fix first", on two real defects, both in adding a passkey, both reproduced by its probes. Everything else it attacked held: another person's passkey, another state, no password step, the origins, user verification, passkeys off, nothing revealed before the password, commit 4a, the grants, and step 18's limits untouched.
    - *Defect 1, fixed: adding a passkey took a code from a locked-out account, and codes could be guessed without a real limit.* Nothing asked whether the account was locked before its authenticator code was checked; the passkeys page's posts had no rate limit; and wrong codes racing on one account were lost to its concurrency stamp. The reviewer's probes: signed in, five wrong codes locked the account, then 200 more in 1.5 seconds were each checked and answered "did not match", never "locked", and the right code then added a passkey while the account was locked; of 100 wrong codes sent at once, 98 were answered 500 and 2 counted. At those rates a 5-minute window gave roughly an 18 to 42% chance of guessing a code: the walk-up attacker of decision 8. Fixed: each add, at its start and at its finish, and each removal holds the account's row until its transaction ends and reads the account again (`StaffUserLock`, in `Ehr.Data`, as `ClinicalDraftLock` holds a draft), so requests for one account are checked and counted one after another; a locked-out account is refused before any code is checked (`passkey_refused` "locked out", the lockout's words); and the passkeys page's posts share the account posts' limit per client address. Tests failing before: a locked account refused with the right code at an add's start and finish; 40 wrong codes at once, five counted as wrong and the rest refused as locked, none answered 500; the limit on the page's posts.
    - *Defect 2, fixed: two adds finishing at once both saved, past the limit, one without its event, on a page saying "Not saved".* Identity's store saves the passkey's row, then failed to update the account on its concurrency stamp, and the failure came back as a message, so the page's transaction committed the row; the reviewer got 11 passkeys and 10 events. Removing had the same shape, by reading. Fixed: the same hold serialises the count against the insert; a failure from Identity throws, so the request's transaction is rolled back with nothing half-saved; the row and its event commit together, for an add, a removal and a passkey's count at sign-in. Tests failing before: two finishes at once with nine stored leave ten, each with its event; an add and a removal at once leave the rows and the events agreeing.
    - *An adjacent security fix, like commit 4a* (the coordinator's instruction): the code step had the same race since the staff sign-in was built, bounded by the limit on sign-in posts, and the passkey at sign-in this step's shape of it. Both of the code page's posts now hold the account first. A test of 20 wrong codes at once after one password step, failing before, counts five and refuses the rest as locked. What remains: the desktop app's sign-in isn't held (no page transaction; its password is checked in the same request; its posts are limited per address); and, as Identity's lockout works, a correct password resets the count of wrong codes, so someone with the password can try four codes per password step, within the limit on sign-in posts, at the web or the desktop, as before this step.
    - *Remarks, taken:* at 360 pixels a clinician's "Passkeys" link, the app bar row's fifth, starts past the edge, and tabbing to it didn't scroll the row: `app-bar.js`, a few lines on staff pages, scrolls a link the keyboard reaches into view, with a Chromium test; the claim that an administrator's row already ran past the edge, wrong for an administrator-only account, was corrected (above); the code page's error is an alert, so a refusal is announced while the focus is on "Use a passkey"; `docs/UC11.md`'s test case list no longer sits under the by-hand check's heading.
    - *What the reviewer couldn't verify:* the ceremony state's 6-minute expiry, by reading only; real devices and Safari, left to Tahseen's by-hand check.
- **2026-11-25, step 19a: desktop idle lock, as built** (proposed FR-68; extends UC 7 and UC 8, Sara's, and UC 11, Luke's). Built by a developer agent from the coordinator's approved plan and its six adjustments, in a clone of its own, and delivered as patches. Six commits: the 12-hour end of a desktop token, the server's check of the lock's password, the slow hash in the encrypted store, the offline charts' age, the lock itself, and this record with the hands-on check. No migration: the 12 hours count from `ehr.desktop_sessions.created_at`, and `unlock` is a new audit action, plain text. **Protected paths:** how a desktop sign-in stays valid (`DesktopSessions.cs`, `DesktopTokenHandler.cs`), the new password check (`DesktopApi.cs`'s unlock, `DesktopSessions.UnlockAsync`) and the rate-limited paths (`AccountRateLimits.cs`): the line-by-line human read is owed at the pull request to main. No `db/policies`; no authorization policy or attribute changed (the unlock is in the Desktop policy's group by the existing convention); not the sync conflict resolver, nor the dictation prompt or note schema.
  - **Tahseen's rulings, in his words** (2026-11-25, second set, about 01:05 by the Mac's clock): "10a: the password against a slow hash inside the encrypted store." "11b, changed: unlocking the desktop app takes the password only, online or offline, while the 12-hour sign-in limit hasn't passed; after it, a full sign-in with the authenticator code, as the web requires. A lock is not a sign-out." "12b, changed: no WebKit CI run. Write the by-hand checklist and add it to the hands-on check I do at the end of 19a." With his rulings on step 18 (a recording counts as activity and is never locked over; a sign-out never loses typed text) and on the rest of the plan (one plan review and one final review; the 8-hour box).
  - *What was built* is in `docs/UC7.md` (the lock, what opens it, the hash, the server's check, the charts' age), `docs/UC8.md` (the 12 hours and sync) and `docs/UC11.md`: the window locks after 15 idle minutes, warned 2 minutes before, at the sign-in's 12-hour end, warned once, when the server ends the session, and at every launch with a sign-in kept, never over a recording; it covers the window without clearing it, refuses the offline charts to the page while it's up, and lets sync carry on; within 12 hours the password alone opens it, checked by the server when one answers and otherwise against an Argon2id hash in the store; after them a full sign-in, or the password alone while no server can complete one.
  - **Decided without Tahseen — to review** (each with what was rejected):
    1. *The 12 hours are counted on the server, from the sign-in with both factors* (the session's `created_at`), and the token is refused 12 hours after it on every desktop and sync request, on both sites, with `Ehr-Sign-In: ended` on the 401; the token, the session row and its 30 days are unchanged, the 30 days now being only how long the app may open its offline copy without a sign-in, which step 20 builds on. On the computer the sign-in's time is in the encrypted store, by its own clock. *Rejected:* a 30-day token that keeps syncing with the 12 hours checked only at unlock (the limit would be the app's promise only, and a token taken from the keychain would sync for 30 days); `Lifetime` set to 12 hours (the offline copy would close at 12 hours, against 10a); a new column or a refresh token (a migration, and step 20's ground).
    2. *Sync stops at the 12-hour end until a full sign-in, and the app shows what that costs* (the coordinator's adjustment 1, for Tahseen to overrule at the pull request). The offline charts are refreshed only by **Download my charts**, never by the background sync, which does pull drafts changed elsewhere; after the 12 hours neither works until the clinician signs in with both factors, so a computer left running holds an older copy than it might, which matters in an outage that starts later. So the app says "Offline charts last updated <date and time>", on the clinic's clock, under the offline charts, on the lock (highlighted, "Nothing is brought back until you sign in again", when a full sign-in is needed) and in the banner after the 12 hours; each download records its time (a new table in the store); charts downloaded by an earlier build say when isn't recorded. The status line's "Checked at" is the last sync round's time, a round answered "sign in again" included, not the charts' age, as before. *Rejected:* past the 12 hours, keep accepting the old token for the charts' download and the drafts' pull and refuse everything else: a fresher copy in an outage, but a token taken from the keychain would keep reading the panel's charts for 30 days.
    3. *Past the 12 hours with no server, the password alone opens the offline charts* (the hard case; safety and privacy pull apart). Safety: an outage that outlasts a shift's sign-in is when the offline chart is needed, and 10a exists for it. Privacy: past the 12 hours the second factor hasn't been checked, and one factor opens the offline copy of up to 25 patients on a computer that is the clinician's. Built: the lock asks for the password and code; when no server can complete the sign-in (none answers, or the one that does answers 5xx, as the cloud app does with the clinic's server cut off, UC 2's outage), the password alone opens the offline charts to read and to save drafts and recordings, which wait; a banner says a sign-in with the code is needed as soon as the clinic's server can be reached, with the charts' age and **Sign in now**; the window isn't locked again each time a server says the 12 hours are over (the cloud app says so every 10 seconds while the clinic's server is cut off, and can't complete a sign-in), and its next lock asks for the full sign-in again. A server that answers "wrong password" or "locked out" is never followed by a check here (tested, TC-UC7-10). Bounded by the existing 30 days. *Rejected:* nothing readable past the 12 hours offline (fails 10a's purpose in exactly the outage it's for); locking again at every such answer (a lock every 10 seconds during UC 2's outage).
    4. *Online, the server checks the lock's password* (since the final review, only the servers its sign-in was made with: below), `POST /api/desktop/unlock` with the token and the password, for the token's own account (no address in the request, so no enumeration), with the sign-in's lockout (5 wrong, 15 minutes, everywhere) and its rate limit per address; 204, or 403 with the reason, since a 401 always means the token was refused; each unlock recorded as `unlock` and each refusal as `sign_in_failed`, with the desktop session. *Rejected:* `/api/desktop/sign-in` without the code (a second-factor-free door to a token); the hash alone, always (no server record of who opened the window when, no lockout parity; the coordinator kept the server's check).
    5. *A server that can't be reached for a moment:* each is given 5 seconds; a 5xx is passed over; with none, the hash. *Rejected:* waiting (the chart stays shut while the network flickers); the hash first (no lockout or record at the server).
    6. *The hash:* Argon2id from RustCrypto's `argon2` 0.6.0, 64 MiB, 3 passes, one lane, a new 16-byte salt, a PHC string that keeps its parameters. 0.11 s on an Apple M5 in a release build, about 0.2 s in a development build with the crate optimized (1.6 s without; `Cargo.toml` optimizes `argon2` and `blake2` in development builds); CI's macOS and Windows runners print its time (TC-UC7-11), the nearest measure of the team's slower PCs before step 20's Windows session. It adds nothing against someone who can read the store, who can read the charts directly; it's a target for guessing the password away from the app, at Argon2id's cost per guess; the lock is against someone at an unattended computer, not encryption. Libraries, under the rule (all new to `Cargo.lock`): `argon2` 0.6.0, `password-hash` 0.6.1, `phc` 0.6.1, `blake2` 0.11.0, `base64ct` 1.8.3, `digest` 0.11.3, `block-buffer` 0.12.1, `crypto-common` 0.2.2, `hybrid-array` 0.4.15, `cmov` 0.5.4 and `ctutils` 0.4.8, each MIT OR Apache-2.0. *Rejected:* OpenSSL's scrypt or Argon2 through `openssl-sys`, already in the lock through SQLCipher (unsafe FFI for a security primitive, and the encoding by hand); PBKDF2 (not memory-hard); 4 lanes (threads, through the crate's rayon feature).
    7. *Wrong passwords checked here:* 5 in a row and the lock waits 15 minutes; 10 and only a full sign-in opens it; kept in the store, so quitting doesn't reset them; a right one or a full sign-in starts the count again; nothing is ever deleted. *Rejected:* the server's rule alone, 5 every 15 minutes without end (about 14,000 guesses against one factor over the 30 days); a stop at 5 (a hurried clinician in an outage); deleting anything (unsent notes).
    8. *Idleness is measured in Rust, from the page's keys, clicks and taps, wheel and typing* (never mouse movement, never sync), told at most every 10 seconds, so the lock may come up to 10 seconds early; on the wall clock as well as the monotonic one, since Rust's `Instant` stands still while a Mac sleeps; a recording and its save hold it off for 5 minutes at most. *Rejected:* the computer's idle time (typing in another app would keep a chart open, and it needs code for each platform); timers in the page (the web view slows them out of sight, and they can't be unit-tested).
    9. *No hooks on the computer's sleep or screen lock:* time asleep counts, so the window is locked on waking after 15 minutes, and the computer's own lock covers it meanwhile. *Rejected:* locking at the moment of sleep or screen lock (native code on two platforms, untestable in CI, and a password after every short lid close).
    10. *What the lock covers:* the app bar and the page made inert and hidden, kept as they were, and a panel over the window; the Rust side refuses the offline charts to the page while it's up, so a reload or a second copy gets nothing; saves under way finish. *Rejected:* clearing the page (typed text lost); blurring it (readable); a native overlay window (code per platform).
    11. *The app starts locked whenever a sign-in is kept;* a sign-in kept by an earlier build, with no record of the lock's, is forgotten at the first launch (the server ends it at 12 hours anyway). *Rejected:* starting unlocked (quitting would skip the lock); an address field on the lock for that one case.
    12. *A session the server ends locks the window instead of clearing it* (until this step the page was cleared), so the same clinician comes back to it after a full sign-in; **Sign out** on the lock clears it, and says so first. *Rejected:* clearing, as before (typed text lost, which the lock must never do: such an answer can arrive while the window is locked).
    13. *The lock's full sign-in is for the address kept at the last sign-in;* someone else signs out first. *Rejected:* an address field (another account's sign-in over the first one's typed text).
    14. *The warning is the web's dialog, in words for a lock:* "This window will lock soon", **Stay unlocked** and **Lock now**, and for the 12-hour end **Keep working**, shown once. *Rejected:* "Stay signed in" (false: a lock isn't a sign-out).
    15. *A recording near the 12-hour end isn't refused,* unlike the web's (409 `sign_in_ending`): the desktop records to the computer, and the lock waits for it; the upload waits for the sign-in. *Rejected:* the web's refusal (nothing here would cut the recording off).
    16. *The times are constants in `lock.rs`,* named beside `SessionTimes`; the server's refusal stays the authority on the 12 hours. *Rejected:* sending them in the sign-in's answer (contract fields a kept session lacks).
    17. *Development builds take shorter times from `EHR_DEBUG_IDLE_SECONDS` and `EHR_DEBUG_SIGN_IN_SECONDS`, only ever shorter,* for the hands-on check; release builds don't read them. *Rejected:* a setting in release builds; waiting 15 minutes at each check.
  - *Not covered by the tests:* the page's script runs in no automated test: it was walked in Chromium with a stand-in for Tauri's bridge (light and dark; the lock, the warnings, typed text and focus kept, Tab and inert), not in WebKit or WebView2, and not in the real window; the hands-on check is the rest. **Not walked on the local stack** (this lane doesn't hold it).
  - *Known limits:* an unlock made with no server isn't in the audit log; a minimized window's Dock tile, or a window coming back from out of sight, may show its last picture for a moment (the hands-on check looks); WebView2's own keys stay on (Ctrl+R reloads, losing typed text as before, and comes back locked; Ctrl+P and Ctrl+F find nothing while locked); **the lock's behaviour on Windows is unverified by hand until step 20's Windows check**; after the lock's last control Tab can reach the window itself, nothing of the page.
  - *What step 20 builds on (none of it built):* the `Ehr-Sign-In` header (a `revoked` value for remote wipe); `lock.rs`'s "a full sign-in only" state, for locking when the 30 days run out offline (`offline_access`, which the app already enforces); the hash lives in the store, so a wipe takes it; `DesktopSessions.Lifetime` and revocation unchanged; a restart for an update starts locked; the rename moves the store and the keychain entries, so a kept sign-in needs a new one.
  - **Final review** (one reviewer, on c1de8e5, the series as pushed): "fix first", on one real defect, fixed in three commits on top, with no rewriting since the branch was pushed.
    - *The defect:* any 2xx answer from a configured server opened the lock, whatever the password. `EHR_SYNC_SERVERS` was read in every build, release included, and took plain HTTP, and the unlock took any 2xx as yes. The reviewer's case: with the window locked, or the app quit, so it starts locked, someone at the unlocked computer quits the app, starts a listener that answers 204 to anything, and starts the app's own binary again with `EHR_SYNC_SERVERS` naming it; the app opens its keychain entry as usual and starts locked, and any password opens it, showing up to 25 offline charts with their drafts and recordings, and the listener receives the bearer token. The lock's full sign-in past the 12 hours had the same trust: a crafted 200 replaced the kept session. It made FR-68's and `docs/UC7.md`'s "checked by the server" untrue. A probe on c1de8e5 showed it: the app started locked for the password, a wrong password came back `Unlocked`, and the stand-in was reached once.
    - **The fix, decided without Tahseen — to review** (each with what was rejected):
      1. *Only the servers a sign-in was made with answer for the lock.* The lock's record keeps them, the built-in ones or `EHR_SYNC_SERVERS` as the sync client reads them, at each sign-in with both factors. *Rejected:* trusting whatever servers the app is started with (the defect); pinning certificates (one per site, renewed by hand).
      2. *Started with other servers, the app forgets the kept sign-in and its record before anything is sent,* the token included, and the sign-in form says so: "Signed out: this app was started with servers other than the ones you signed in with, and only those can open its lock. Sign in again; nothing on this computer was deleted." The hash on this computer isn't used then either: the offline charts open only after a sign-in with the servers the app now has. The unlock checks the same again before asking any server. *Rejected:* keeping the sign-in and opening the lock with the hash alone while other servers are named (the sync would still send them the token); refusing to start.
      3. *A release build takes HTTPS servers only;* a plain-HTTP server in `EHR_SYNC_SERVERS` stops the sync client, saying why. *Rejected:* allowing plain HTTP to 127.0.0.1 (the stand-in's own address); ignoring `EHR_SYNC_SERVERS` in release builds (the README's way to point the app at other servers).
      4. *A store made by this step's first build gains the column,* its record naming no servers, so that sign-in is forgotten at the next start. *Rejected:* a new table, which would leave the old one's hash of the password behind.
      5. *Only the lock's own word opens the window* (the review's U1: a redraw's view computed just before the lock could uncover the page until the next round). Each view of the lock carries a count that moves on with every lock, opening and change of what opens it, and the page drops one older than it has shown; and only the lock's events and the answers to unlocking, locking now and signing out may open the window. *Rejected:* either half alone (the count alone still lets a view nobody asked to open with do it; the rule alone still lets a stale event).
    - *Its remarks, taken in the same round:* the hands-on check's times, which collided when run in order (the 12-hour end fell inside step 5, and Safari's warning in the middle of the desktop steps), now work top to bottom, with the idle time alone shortened, Safari's steps where they fall due, the 12-hour steps last after a fresh start, and a step for other servers (R1); the README's "lasts thirty days" (R2); the banner's "the clinic's server can be reached", untrue when only the cloud app answers, the outage it is for (R3); "signed out on another computer", which can't happen, replaced by the reasons that can (R4); `docs/UC11.md`'s step 19a section, moved after the earlier steps' test list (R5); this plan's log, which described a rebase that hadn't happened (R6); `Program.cs`'s comment on "the anonymous account POSTs" (R7).
    - *Known limits, from its remarks:* a recording finished after the server ended the session can't be saved, as before this step, since the save needs the session, and the page says it couldn't be saved (R8); the computer's clock: setting it back delays the 12-hour lock on the computer, though the server's refusal still comes, and setting it forward shortens the 15-minute wait after 5 wrong passwords, though the stop at 10 holds (R9); a sign-out past the 12 hours isn't recorded on the server, which refuses the token it carries (R10).
    - *Still unverified:* the real web views (WebKit in the macOS app, WebView2) and what the operating system shows of a window (a minimized window's Dock tile, the window's last picture coming back from out of sight). The hands-on check covers them.
- **2026-11-25, step 21: real deployment and operations, prepare only, as built** (proposed NFR-17 and NFR-18; extends UC 2, Tahseen's (the restore alternative, outage alerts), UC 4's TC-UC4-06 (Diego's), and UC 20 (the hand-over)). Built by a developer agent from the coordinator's approved plan, its decision D1 and six adjustments, in a clone of its own on the integration branch at d0bbf31. Seven commits: the app roles' idle limit, the Barman pin, the restore drill, the monitoring profile, the Tailscale rules, the deployment guide, and this record. No migration. **Protected path: `db/policies/00-roles.sql`**: the line-by-line human read is owed at the pull request to main; not on AGENTS.md's list, but for the same read, the `monitor` role in `infra/compose/postgres/primary/initdb/10-infra-roles.sh` and its line in `pg_hba.conf`. Nothing was hosted, no account was created and nothing was spent.
  - **Tahseen's rulings, in his words** (2026-11-25, second set, about 01:05 by the Mac's clock): "21: 16a (no Grafana), 17a, 18a (stated plainly as untested against a network), 19a (provider prices dated, checked when written), 20a, 21a with (b) as the fallback." The numbers are the options of the list he answered (`phase6-decisions-2026-11-25.md`, the coordinator's wording): 16a, Prometheus, Alertmanager and their exporters, all Apache-2.0, with Prometheus's own pages for graphs; 17a, alerts by email through the stack's mail, a real relay a setting at deployment; 18a, the Tailscale rules as a policy file with its own tests section, checked for syntax only, the docs saying they haven't run against a network; 19a, docs for any Ubuntu LTS server with Docker, with a table of sizes and monthly cost at two or three providers and no provider's own tooling; 20a, the restore drill in CI's stack job on every run if it adds under about 3 minutes, otherwise a weekly workflow; 21a, `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` of 60 seconds on both app roles in `db/policies/00-roles.sql`, with a test, falling back to (b), a lock timeout on the lab hand-over alone, if a handler rightly idles in a transaction. And his rules for steps 18 to 21 (2026-11-25, twelfth set of the 24th's session), in his words: "One plan review and one final review per step; no extra review rounds unless the final review finds a real defect." "No zero-tolerance gates or new eval sets I haven't asked for." "Decide anything new by the standing principles and log it; come to me only for what's on the "what still stops" list." "Time box: if a step passes 8 hours of work, stop and tell me where it stands and what shipping it as-is would mean."
  - *21a, the audit first: no request holds a transaction across a call out of the app,* so (b) isn't needed. The app's only outbound HTTP clients are `DictationSidecar` (`Api/Dictation` only) and `OnPremApp` (forwarding and the audit shipper); email is queued (`Program.cs:99-107`; `Email/QueuedEmailSender.cs:14-18` returns once the message is queued, and a background service with no transaction sends it). As of d0bbf31, every place a request opens one:
    1. *Every page handler* (`DatabaseTransactionFilter.cs:19-26`): the transaction wraps the handler alone; model binding has read the body before it begins, and the page renders after the commit. No page handler uses either client.
    2. *Off-site forwarding* (`Forwarding/CloudForwardingMiddleware.cs:29-94`, registered at `Program.cs:206`, before any page filter): no transaction; its one database call is `WalPosition.HasReachedAsync` (`:94`), one statement; the forward itself (`ActivityTimeout` 30 s, `:25`) holds nothing, and the on-prem app runs the forwarded request in a page transaction of its own.
    3. *Dictation staging and the sidecar:* each transaction commits before the call, and the write after the answer opens a new one once the answer is read: `Api/Dictation/DictationApi.cs:170-178` (commits before `next`); `DictationReviewApi.cs:40-62` before the call at `:65`, the write at `:72`; `:88-92` before the stage call at `:104`, the write at `:119`; `:141`, discarding, calls nothing; `AmbientApi.cs:100-121` before `:125`, the write at `:146`, the cancels at `:134` and `:156-160` outside any; `:218-222` commits before audio is forwarded. Every sidecar call is capped at 20 seconds anyway (`DictationSidecar.cs:20`).
    4. *The lab's door* (`Api/Lab/LabApi.cs:116-118`, `:160-162`): one database call each; the POST's body is read at `:137`, before.
    5. *Sync and the desktop* (`Api/Sync/QuickTextSync.cs:42-49`, `:67-74`; `Api/Desktop/DraftSyncApi.cs:56-61`, `:81-`; `Api/Desktop/DesktopApi.cs:75-77`): database work only, the body bound before the handler.
    6. *FHIR* (`Api/Fhir/FhirApi.cs:306-312`): the answer is written by its result after the commit (`:385-400`).
    7. *The audit import on-prem and the background services* (`Api/Audit/CloudAuditApi.cs:86-88`, `Ambient/AmbientTranscriptPurgeService.cs:39`): database only; the cloud's shipper has no database, and its audit flush (`CloudAudit/RequestAuditEvents.cs:87-97`) runs after the page filter has committed.
    8. *The migration job and the seed* run as `ehr_owner`, which the setting doesn't reach, statement after statement (the seed's one transaction is busy, not idle).
  - *21a as built:* `ALTER ROLE ehr_app SET idle_in_transaction_session_timeout = '60s'`, and the same for `ehr_read`, in `00-roles.sql`. The server ends such a session and rolls its transaction back, so nothing is half-written; Npgsql fails the session's next command (25P03) and drops the connection, and the user sees the page's error. By the audit, only a debugger paused over a minute or someone's own console signed in as one of these roles meets it. The superuser's and `ehr_owner`'s sessions aren't limited, and a statement still running isn't idle: either still holds the hand-over back until it ends (the README, UC 20 and the stack's README say so). Role settings are catalog rows, so the replica's `ehr_read` has it (CI's stack job checks both databases). Tests: `RuntimeRoleTests.Runtime_roles_end_a_transaction_left_idle_for_a_minute`; `LabSecurityTests.A_session_left_idle_in_a_transaction_is_ended_after_a_minute_and_no_longer_holds_back_the_lab`, which waits out the real minute in its class's own database.
  - *The Barman pin, changed from the approved plan's series check:* the plan assumed PostgreSQL's apt repository keeps only the newest Barman, so an exact pin would stop resolving; `apt-cache madison` showed it keeps earlier ones (3.21.0, 3.20.1, 3.20.0), and 3.21.0, which the stack has never run, was already the newest, so a 3.20 series check would have failed every build at once. The image now installs `barman` and `python3-barman` 3.20.1-1.pgdg13+1, the version the stack and CI ran with; the Dockerfile says what to check before moving it. The `-pgdg13` names Debian 13, so a new Debian under `postgres:18` fails the build too. Barman is GPL-3.0+ (its Debian copyright file), as it was before: an unmodified program in its own container, chosen before the expansion; this step only pins it.
  - *The restore drill* (20a): `infra/compose/restore-drill.cs`, as its header and the stack's README describe; CI's stack job runs it after Barman's first backup. On this Mac, on a stack of its own (project `lane21`, its own settings, ports and volumes, torn down after), under two other agents' test suites (load average 20 to 24): passed in 45 seconds. The CI runner's time is the coordinator's first run's; over about 3 minutes there, the weekly workflow comes back to him.
  - *Monitoring* (16a, 17a): the `monitoring` profile, as the stack's README's "Monitoring" describes, at Prometheus 3.16.0, Alertmanager 0.35.0, blackbox_exporter 0.29.1, node_exporter 1.12.1 and postgres_exporter 0.20.1, each the newest release at least two weeks old on 2026-11-25; Apache-2.0 each, from its repository's LICENSE at that tag (fetched the same day), and the Prometheus image's own LICENSE file (the others carry none). The profile adds about 300 MB. The alert end to end, on the same stack and load (20 to 36): the replica stopped, and the administrators' email was in Mailpit 167 seconds later. The coordinator's adjustment 2, least privilege: the `monitor` role has LOGIN and a connection limit of 3 and nothing else: no table privilege, no predefined role (the one metric family the alerts read, `pg_replication_slots_*`, comes from `pg_replication_slots` and `pg_current_wal_lsn()`, which every role may read), no REPLICATION; every other collector is off, so none reads a table's or a statement's text; `MonitorRoleTests` holds the role to all of it and runs the collector's own queries as it. Its `pg_hba.conf` line names the `postgres` database alone, from `samenet`: Docker picks the stack's address ranges, and fixing one could clash with another stack on the machine, as the replica's and Barman's lines already reason; on a real server it names the monitoring host's address.
  - *Tailscale* (18a): `infra/tailscale/policy.hujson`, its header the access model in words and "untested against a network", with a tests section; `infra/tailscale/check.cs` in the web job. What the check proves and doesn't is in both files.
  - *The deployment guide* (19a, D1): `infra/deploy/README.md`, which says first that it has never been run against a server, was written from the stack and CI, and which three parts were exercised locally. Its provider table: DigitalOcean's 4 GiB Basic Droplet and Akamai's Linode 4 GB at $24.00 a month, Hetzner's CPX21 in its US locations at €32.49 excl. VAT, each from the page named beside it, read 2026-11-25 without an account; whether each signs a BAA is "to check". **What the standing decision asked and what was delivered:** it asked for "deployment documentation for the clinic server and a single low-cost cloud server, plus deployment scripts (dotnet run or PowerShell, no bash)". Delivered: the documentation, and three `dotnet run` scripts, the restore drill, the site check (`infra/deploy/check-sites.cs`) and the Tailscale check. **No script that deploys anything was written**, and no per-server Compose files (D1): config nobody can run would be untested config. The guide lists each server's services and settings instead, and names the files as work before a first deployment.
  - **Decided without Tahseen — to review** (by patient safety and privacy first, then the course document, then the smallest choice that's easy to reverse):
    1. *Barman on the cloud server in a deployment:* off the clinic's premises, in its own container and volume, listening on nothing; the replica already holds the whole record there. *Rejected:* the clinic server (lost with it), a third server (cost; he chose one cloud server), object storage (an account).
    2. *Monitoring on the cloud server,* which must see the clinic fail; the clinic exposes `node-exporter` to it alone over Tailscale; the monitoring pages on 127.0.0.1, reached through SSH. *Rejected:* monitoring on the clinic server (an outage there would silence it), on both, and the pages on the tailnet without a sign-in. The cost, said in the guide: while the cloud server is down, nothing alerts.
    3. *No metrics endpoint in the app:* the sites' sign-in pages are the probes (every page opens a database transaction), and the audit queue's backlog isn't measured, its cause, the clinic link, is. *Rejected:* a `/metrics` endpoint (a new door, which would need a key of its own: network position isn't identity).
    4. *The `monitor` role at first initialization,* like `replicator` and `barman`, and `MONITOR_PASSWORD` optional in `.env`, the exporter refusing to start without it (as `llama` does). *Rejected:* reusing `barman`'s credentials (a replication role: a leak would stream every row) or `ehr_read`'s. A stack initialized before this step has no such role until it is reset.
    5. *The Barman pin* (above). *Rejected:* the series check (3.21.0 already out), installing from PyPI.
    6. *The drill writes only to its own database* and restores only inside the Barman volume, into the existing `restore-db`; it takes the newest base backup that ended before the target, and fails plainly when none did. *Rejected:* marker rows in `ehr`; `latest` (a backup newer than the target can't reach it).
    7. *Alert thresholds* (each with its reason in `alerts.yml`): 2 minutes for a site, the clinic link and the replica, 5 for Barman's stream, 30 for Barman's check (a first backup over the WAN), 25 hours for the newest backup (one every 23), 15% for a disk, 14 days for a certificate, 5 minutes for monitoring itself; every alert to one address, the administrators'. *Rejected:* a separate route by severity (one clinic, one inbox).
    8. *Alertmanager's configuration as files:* the stack's committed, a real server's from `infra/deploy/alertmanager.example.yml`, the relay's password in a file. *Rejected:* filling the file from the environment with `sed` at start.
    9. *A disk alert per disk,* not per place it is mounted (the stack's one disk is mounted four times in the exporter's container).
    10. *Staff devices by tag,* which only administrators may give. *Rejected:* a group of staff users (any device one signed in on).
    11. *SSH through OpenSSH on the Tailscale address.* *Rejected:* Tailscale SSH.
    12. *The off-site staff site stays on the cloud's public address beside the portal,* as the course design has it, behind its sign-in. *Rejected:* a staff site only on the tailnet (a proxy or the app telling them apart, not built, and a change to UC 2's off-site path).
    13. *The images:* `ehr-web` from CI's registry; publishing `ehr-migrate`, `ehr-barman` and `ehr-dictation` is work before a first deployment, not done here (the publish job runs only on main, and `ehr-barman`'s arm64 build needs a runner or emulation). *Rejected:* building them on a server (AGENTS.md: images are built in CI).
    14. *The idle test waits the real minute,* in `LabSecurityTests`' own database. *Rejected:* lowering the limit inside the test's session (that tests PostgreSQL, not the setting).
    15. *The guide lives in `infra/deploy/README.md`,* beside its site check and the relay example.
    16. *A dedicated, long-lived Data Protection certificate in a deployment,* never the sites' ACME certificate, as a warning in the guide and a line before a first deployment: `Program.cs:120` reads the key ring with the current certificate only, so a renewal would sign everyone out and lose every authenticator enrollment.
    17. *The initdb script stops at its first failure* (`set -eu`): with the monitor's password set after the roles, a failed role creation would otherwise have let the primary's first start succeed.
  - *Before a first deployment* (the guide's last section): the owner's accounts (a cloud server, Tailscale, a mail relay, a domain), the money, a BAA if real patient data is ever involved, and the full page time with its commit measured on the clinic server (the 20 ms target CI's runners can't check); and the work: per-server Compose files, with the primary's address that `bootstrap-standby.sh` and `onprem.conf` hard-code as `wan` made a setting, and the clinic's `pg_hba.conf` naming the cloud server's address; publishing the other images from CI; a Data Protection certificate made for that alone; the databases' own certificate authority.

Everyone with a local stack: `.env` gains optional `MONITOR_PASSWORD`, `PROMETHEUS_PORT` and `ALERTMANAGER_PORT` (copy them from `.env.example`); the `monitor` role exists only on a primary initialized after this step; the `restore_drill` database name is the drill's.

Its own measurements: on its stack the drill took 30 seconds of work and the alert 171 seconds; five mutated rule files were each caught by the rule tests. Fixed in a round of follow-up commits on 78e91f1, nothing rewritten:
    1. *Defect: the restore onto the clinic server* used `barman recover --remote-ssh-command` from the cloud server, which the Tailscale rules refuse (the cloud server has no SSH to the clinic, by design). The guide now has the restore made on the cloud server and the restored folder copied to the clinic by an administrator, who reaches both.
    2. *Defect: the guide's monitoring differences left out `blackbox.yml`,* whose probes verify against the stack's root and name two of its sites, so `SiteDown` would fire for every real site from the first start. The guide now says to remove both.

The one the developer's first run left on this Mac (created 19:47:12Z) was checked empty and attached to nothing, and removed by its ID alone.

    6. *Taken: the guide* warns that ports Docker publishes bypass `ufw`, and says which address each is published on (the clinic's 5432 and 9100 on its Tailscale address only).
    7. *Taken: a first base backup that hangs raised no alert,* since Barman's state is written only after a pass. `BackupStateMissing` fires when it has been absent for an hour, with its rule tests. *Known limit:* once written, a state that stops being updated (a later backup that hangs) isn't caught by it; `BackupTooOld` is, at 25 hours, and `BackupStreamDown` if WAL stops.

After: "- Restore (a high or critical package advisory fails it), a Release build, `dotnet format --verify-no-changes`, the Tailscale policy file's syntax check, and `dotnet test`." Before: "- The local server stack: the migration job applies cleanly, and the stack serves all three sites, replicates, backs up, restores to a point in time, and emails an alert when the replica stops." After: "- The local server stack: the alert rules pass their tests, the migration job applies cleanly, and the stack serves all three sites, replicates, backs up, restores to a point in time, and emails an alert when the replica stops."

The reviewer's fetch of its page was refused; the developer's fetch tool read it on 2026-11-25, at about 13:30 and again at about 18:00 Central, both times "Linode 4 GB | $24.00 | $0.0360 | 4 GB | 2 | 80 GB | 4 TB", so the figure stays, read by one tool only. Whether Tailscale accepts the policy file is unknown until it is applied.

Seven benchmark runs later: with SQL timing on, **every statement the off-site app sends takes about 0.2 to 1.3 ms where on-site they take almost nothing**, so each extra statement on the off-site chart costs milliseconds, and so did making the visits' statement more complex. The chart was reduced to one plain query for vital signs. I did not find the cause of the off-site cost per statement; my guess, not checked, is that the cloud app runs each statement on its own (no request-long transaction), so PostgreSQL re-plans the row-level-security helper functions every time. **For Tahseen and Luke:** that predates this step and is probably worth more than anything done here; fixing it would bring every off-site page down.

  - *From the review* (failed on correctness; the database rules held, with one hardening), all fixed with tests:
    - **Ticking the body mass index beside its weight or height made the whole set refuse**, five times in eight, with no retry that worked: the weight's own update had already marked it. The body mass index is no longer offered to tick; it follows its weight and height.
    - **A trend flagged every point against the newest reading's range,** wrong once ranges go by age: each point is now marked against the range it was recorded with, and the chart says so when they differ.
    - A weight corrected by itself lost its body mass index for good: one is now recorded when the other measure stands.
    - Only the six newest sets could be corrected, and an older entry in error could not be shown: "Show earlier sets" lists up to 24.
    - `observation_codes` still said 84 for the adult diastolic, used whenever the banded table is empty (between two steps of a deploy): the generator and its file now say 79.
    - One statement could record someone else as the person who marked a reading, by changing who is signed in between its rows: the update policy now holds the row to the person the statement began as (`30-rls.sql`).
    - Rolling the migration back failed part-way once anything had been corrected, and failed outright on a database with its policies: it now refuses with the reason when a reading is marked, and drops the policy that names the removed column. **`VitalCorrections` is one way once used.**
    - The migration no longer lifts forced row-level security on `patients` (its owner can already read it), so it takes no lock there. It holds `observations` for its length: 17 seconds for 900,000 vital signs on a laptop (the reviewer's measurement). The migration job's command timeout is now ten minutes (`infra/migrate/migrate.sh`), where the driver's default is 30 seconds.
    - Ages in months are worked out between timestamps without a zone, so a session's time zone can't move a month boundary.
  - *A behaviour that changed, for Tahseen to know:* typing a set again for a moment that has one used to be refused whole ("already recorded"). Now the values that already stand, the same, are left, and anything new is added to that set. It is what makes a correction possible; it also means a second submit with one more sign adds that sign.

No sideways scrolling. **Not looked at on screen:** the "not the note's author" label and the inbox item, which need a second clinician account the local stack doesn't have; both are asserted in TC-UC14-13.
  - *From the review* (failed; signed text held immutable by every route tried), all fixed with tests unless noted:
    - **Sending the "Mark sensitive" form twice unmarked the note** (a double click, Back and send, or a form left open while a colleague marked it): the form now posts the change it offered, and a change already made is answered "already", with nothing done.
    - **An addendum signed while its note was being marked could stay non-sensitive,** its text then in an administrator's audit page: signing an addendum and marking its note now take turns, on an advisory lock on the note. (A row lock was the first fix; it follows the update policy, which would have stopped colleagues adding to a sensitive note at all.)
    - **The rollback failed once an addendum inbox item existed** (forced row-level security hid the rows from its delete), and left this step's policies and functions behind: it now lifts it for the delete and drops them all. Until the earlier release's `db/policies` is applied after a rollback, signing a note fails; apply it straight after.
    - **The unmark rule is in the update policies too,** decided on the row as it stood and the person the statement began as, so a statement that changes who is signed in part-way gets no row to unmark. The cascade to addenda now fails the mark if any addendum is left unmarked.
    - A note or addendum signed as sensitive between the migration and the policies step of a deploy has nobody recorded as marking it: its signer or author counts.
    - The note now names who marked it; the page for an addendum someone hasn't asked to see records the view; the comments and README lines that still said "immutable" or "append-only" are corrected.
    - *Not changed, and why:* a statement that changes `app.user_id` part-way can still attribute a **mark** (not an unmark) to someone else, and step 9b's entered-in-error has the same property for a single row. Doing it needs arbitrary SQL as the app's database role, and whoever has that can set `app.user_id` at the start of the transaction and be anyone outright: the database trusts the app for who is signed in. Recorded so nobody takes the trigger-set columns as proof against a compromised app.
    - *Left open:* a signer who has left the patient's panel gets the inbox item, and its link takes them to the emergency-access page first; if the person who marked a note is also its signer and their account is locked, nobody can unmark it; after a mark, the audit page labels the note's earlier views "a sensitive item changed", which is the existing wording for any event of a sensitive item.
  - *The proxy test, said plainly:* it proves a proxy is given no note, and that a real view made through the app before the mark (a colleague's, in TC-UC14-14) is still in the log afterwards, dated before it. It cannot prove a **proxy's** view stays, because no proxy can view a note today.

Three of them failed once each under load today (TC-UC1-17 and TC-UC1-12 in CI, TC-UC4-22 here) and passed on every rerun; what they assert is unchanged. Other owners' browser tests are not touched.

Vital-sign readings (Tahseen's step; a phase 2 follow-up on `expansion/phase-2`; extends UC 4; FR-50 extended).** Built by a developer agent from an approved plan, in a clone of its own, and applied here as patches. Migration `VitalReadings` (`observations.reading_id`; one standing value per patient, sign and time whatever the visit; a hand-written data move). **Protected paths: `db/policies/75-results.sql`** (`ehr.file_result`: a lock per patient and moment, the visit decided from the time taken and inherited by the moment's later values, the reading ID assigned by the database; `ehr.mark_reading_in_error`; `ehr.check_vital_reading`, SECURITY DEFINER, on deferred constraint triggers) **and `db/policies/30-rls.sql`** (`observations_owner_select`). What was decided is in the decision log's entries for this step.
  - *Tahseen's five points:* (1) the unique index no longer includes the visit, and failures A and B are tests (TC-UC4-25 and 26, before and after check-in); (2) a vital sign's visit is the latest held visit that had already begun at the time taken, never one that begins later, so the order of typing and check-in changes nothing; (3) a blood pressure is one reading of two values sharing a reading ID; the database refuses, at the end of the transaction and for every role, a reading that isn't exactly one standing systolic and one standing diastolic or wholly in error; marking either marks both; the flowsheet, chart, trends, portal trends and offline copy pair by reading ID only; (4) and his later answer: the migration, below.
  - **What the migration reported on the local stack** (the existing data here; 878,262 vital signs): 0 vital signs moved to another visit; 0 duplicates marked; 0 body mass indexes marked or worked out again; **1 blood pressure value marked entered in error because its partner was** ("partner value was marked in error before readings existed (migration 9c)"); 0 values that never had a partner; 585,474 standing values made into 292,737 readings; 2 values in error given readings. On a fresh 50,000-patient seed: every count 0, and 401,822 values into 200,911 readings, in about 10 seconds. The migrate job prints these lines now (`DatabaseNotices`); before this step it printed no notice at all.
  - *Decided by me, for Tahseen to confirm:* a value that never had a partner is marked too, with its own reason (none exists in the seed or the stack); visit moves are audited; the chart's up or down mark for a blood pressure shows only when its two numbers didn't move in opposite directions; a duplicate weight or height that is marked takes its body mass index with it, and the index is worked out again from the values kept.
  - **For Tahseen:** keeping the most recently entered value of each sign can leave a pair that was never entered together (120/80, then 130/85 typed whole after a check-in, then 85 marked in error alone: the migration leaves 130/80). The alternative treats 130 as a value whose partner is in error and leaves 120/80. Neither the seed nor the stack has such rows.
  - **A change clinicians will notice:** vital signs taken before the patient is checked in are no longer tied to the visit, whenever they are typed, so the chart's line for that visit says they were not taken at it. That follows from point 2 as worded; the earlier rule allowed 15 minutes before a visit's start.
  - *Review:* the review agent tried savepoints, SET CONSTRAINTS, multi-row and ON CONFLICT statements, COPY, role changes and two-connection races against the rules and none got through. It failed the first series on the migration (a body mass index left standing after its weight was marked as a duplicate) and on point 2 (my instruction to keep the 15-minute window didn't meet Tahseen's words); both fixed, with the smaller findings.
  - *Found in the screenshots, older than this step, fixed:* with entries in error shown the flowsheet pushed the page sideways (hidden screen-reader text escaped the table's container), and at desktop width the flowsheet's last columns were cut off with no way to scroll.
  - *Gates:* format clean; Release build; 907 tests, 871 pass, 36 performance cases skipped without a results file; benchmark `--assert` passed (developer's run, on a busy machine: chart 11.2 ms, vital signs 2.8 ms, off-site chart 18.7 ms at p99 excluding the commit) and in CI. Screenshots, light and dark at 1280 and 640, looked at: chart, flowsheet, entries in error, the mark form.
  - *Not done here:* a second review of the fixes; the stack's desktop app was not run (its contract is unchanged).
  - *Resume at step 9d,* the children's ranges, on `expansion/phase-2`; then tag `expansion-phase-2.1`, merge the phase 2 branch into the integration branch, and finish step 11's blood pressure.
- **2026-11-20, step 11. Read-only FHIR R4 API (new use case UC 19; proposed FR-53).** Built by a developer agent from an approved plan, in a clone of its own, and applied here as patches; reviewed four times by the review agent. No migration, no `db/policies` change. **Authorization change, for line-by-line review:** the `/fhir` group takes the existing Clinician policy (`Program.cs`); the staff cookie answers a refused `/fhir` request with 401 or 403 instead of a redirect, as it does under `/api` (`AccountSchemes.FhirPath`, `AccountServiceCollectionExtensions`); and the gate and its order, lookup then gate then read, are in `FhirApi.cs` (`MayReadAsync`, `ReadAsync`, `SearchAsync`). What was decided, including Tahseen's five changes of 19 November, is in the decision log's step 11 entries.
  - *What it serves:* `GET /fhir/metadata`; Patient by ID and by MRN; AllergyIntolerance, MedicationStatement, Condition, Observation and Appointment by ID and searched by patient, as `application/fhir+json`, never cached, with no CORS headers. Clinicians only, for a patient on their panel or under emergency access already in force. Sensitive diagnoses and medications are left out unless `show-sensitive=true`, which records the same event the chart does and marks them restricted. Every answered request is one audit event; a refused or not-found one is none.
  - *Blood pressure* is one Observation per reading (LOINC 85354-9, systolic and diastolic components in mm[Hg]), its id the reading's, built from step 9c's reading ID only. Every vital sign claims its R4 profile and is validated against it in the tests.
  - *Dependencies, tests only:* Hl7.Fhir.R4 and Hl7.Fhir.Specification.Data.R4 6.8.0, Firely.Fhir.Validation.R4 3.6.0 and Fhir.Metrics 1.4.0, all BSD-3-Clause (transitive packages MIT). Nothing new in the app. Every answer is parsed strictly and validated offline against R4's definitions; the codes themselves (LOINC, RxNorm, ICD-10-CM, UNII) can't be checked that way.
  - *Tahseen's other changes:* the MRN's system `http://swiftchart.example/fhir/sid/mrn` with type MR; the seed's thyrotropin unit is UCUM (`m[IU]/L`) and `UcumTests` checks every unit in the reference data and the seed; a capability statement generated from the API's own list and tested against the routes; errors as OperationOutcome; the endpoints in the render benchmark.
  - **What CI caught, and my part in it:** the first blood pressure build passed its tests and reviews and then ran for sixteen minutes on one benchmark row in CI. The cause was a guard I asked for in a review round (join the diastolic "of the same reading in the same state"): EF Core wrote that join so that a null reading ID matches a null one, which no index answers, and every request read every diastolic in the table. I first told Tahseen it was table statistics; it wasn't. The read now has no join (the patient's rows by the patient's index, paired in memory by reading ID) and reads by ID use unique keys. `FhirPlanTests` runs every FHIR read with statistics cleared and gathered and fails on a sequential scan, and the benchmark stops a row that runs over a minute.
  - *Also found on the way:* a timing-dependent test of mine from step 9b (TC-UC4-20, and the same pattern in TC-UC14-10 and -15) read a cut-off page before that app knew the clinic's server was down; each now asks first.
  - *Reviews:* (1) the API: passed with fixes (an MRN the database refuses gave 500; an MRN search told an off-panel clinician whether an MRN exists; a needless count; ordering; metadata's parameters). (2) the blood pressure panel: passed with fixes (405 before 406; Accept precedence; a test path; the guard above). (3) the no-join rewrite: passed, with every page of a mixed patient matching a brute-force list at every limit, and one latent edge (a laboratory row carrying a blood pressure code), fixed. No review found a way to read a patient off the caller's panel, or an unrecorded read.
  - *Gates:* format clean; Release build; 942 tests, 898 pass, 44 performance cases skipped without a results file; CI green, its benchmark job passing `--assert` in 7 minutes. FHIR rows in CI at p99, commit included: patient 5.2 ms, observations 4.9, conditions with sensitive items 4.6, observation by ID 6.3; off-site 3.4, 4.2, 3.6 and 5.7. The on-site chart measured 16.8 ms at p99 excluding the commit on that runner: still the page nearest the gate.
  - *Not done:* no screenshots (the step adds no page); the API has not yet been called on the local stack, which is on the phase 2 line's build until that line is merged in (to do then, and noted here when done); the desktop app is untouched.
  - *Open, for Tahseen:* oxygen saturation claims only the base vital-signs profile, because R4's own profile wants LOINC 2708-6 and the record holds 59408-5 (send both?); an already-seeded database keeps the old thyrotropin unit on its seeded results, which the API then sends as text.
  - *Resume:* merge the phase 2 line (steps 9c's follow-ups and 9d) into the integration branch once it is tagged `expansion-phase-2.1`, then step 12, the simulated lab.
- **2026-11-20, step 9d. Children's ranges from the primary sources, with Tahseen's answers on step 9c (a phase 2 follow-up on `expansion/phase-2`; extends UC 4; FR-50 extended).** Built by the developer agent that built step 9c, from an approved plan, and applied here as patches; reviewed three times. Migration `ChildrenRanges` (the range table by age in months and sex, with a kind; `observations.reference_kind`; existing readings ranged again), and the hand-written rule inside the already-merged `VitalReadings` migration edited in place, on Tahseen's word that no rerun is needed. **Protected paths: `db/policies/75-results.sql`** (`ehr.file_result`: the range by age and sex and its kind, the visit rule with its 15 minutes, a shared lock per patient; `ehr.check_in_visit` and `ehr.attach_vital_signs`, new) **and `db/policies/30-rls.sql`** (`observations_owner_update`). What was decided is in the decision log's entries for step 9d and for Tahseen's answers on step 9c.
  - *The numbers:* read from the two PDFs Tahseen supplied by me, by the developer and by the reviewer, each independently, text checked against the rendered pages; all 82 rows agree in the reference SQL, the C# table, the migration and a literal copy in the tests. Girls, age 1, diastolic is 54 in the original (the reproduction used before had 57). The AAP file carries "ROUGH GALLEY PROOF" in a hidden line just below the bottom edge of 66 of its 72 pages; the citation is the published article and says so beside it.
  - **What the `ChildrenRanges` migration reported on the local stack:** heart rate, 59,017 standing values re-ranged, 4,251 newly flagged Low, 396 no longer flagged; respiratory rate, none recorded; systolic, 59,017 re-ranged, 1,895 newly "needs follow-up", 40,723 changed from High to "needs follow-up"; diastolic, 50,602 re-ranged, 3,212 newly, 34,460 changed; as blood pressure readings, 59,017 re-ranged, 838 newly flagged, 46,610 changed; in all 168,636 standing values, 9,358 newly flagged, 396 no longer flagged, 75,183 changed; no value in error. On a 50,000-patient seed made before the step: 68,551 values, 4,275 newly flagged, none no longer flagged, 10,365 changed, in about 9 seconds. The stack's children were generated for the old ranges, which is why so many are flagged; the seed now shapes children to the new tables and flags about one in ten per sign.
  - *Tahseen's answers on step 9c, as built:* (1) a vital sign taken up to 15 minutes before a visit's start belongs to it whenever it is typed: `ehr.file_result` takes the latest held visit that began no later than 15 minutes after the time taken, and a check-in ties the sets already recorded, so typing order changes nothing; his two tests (10 minutes: attached; 20 minutes: not) pass typed before and typed after. (2) a moment's standing blood pressure is the most recent pair entered together; his example gives 120/80 with the 130 marked "not entered together with a standing partner (migration 9c)". (3) and (4) as he said.
  - *Checked on the local stack through the pages:* a heart rate taken 10 minutes before a check-in and typed before it was tied to the visit at check-in, with one audit event naming the clinician; one taken 20 minutes before was left; a blood pressure taken 5 minutes before and typed after is on the visit as one reading; the visit's start is the database's clock.
  - *Reviews:* (1) failed: the check-in rule trusted the visit's start as sent, so SQL as the app role could backdate a visit and re-tie old readings (the reviewer moved a two-year-old one). First closed by refusing visits not starting now; (2) the second review found that window was measured from the transaction's start, so a held transaction could still backdate, and that a refusal reached the clinician as an error page. Now the database sets the start itself, as it sets `booked_at` and `issued_at`, and only the status can be refused. Also fixed: the migration's report counted values in error as flag changes; a tie between two loaded pairs kept a different pair from the one the docs named; two visits starting at one instant. No test about authorization, PHI or the audit log was weakened.
  - *Found in the screenshots, older than this step, fixed:* the trend's table cut its value text off at the card's edge.
  - *Gates:* format clean; Release build; 942 tests, 906 pass, 36 performance cases skipped without a results file; CI green with its benchmark job passing (chart 11.8 ms and vital signs 4.8 ms at p99 excluding the commit on CI's runner; off-site 10.3 and 3.9). Screenshots, light and dark at 1280 and 640, looked at: a child's flowsheet, trends with their tables open, and chart. The portal's view of a child's trend was not screenshotted (TC-UC4-29 reads it).
  - **For Tahseen:** the items listed under "Step 9c, for Tahseen" in the phase 2 pull request draft; whether to seed the local stack again so its children aren't mostly flagged (it means emptying its patients, so it waits for his word); the coordinator's decisions on the ranges (other or unknown sex, no lower value, the Birth row).
  - *Resume:* merge `expansion/phase-2` into the integration branch, check the FHIR API on the stack, then step 12, the simulated lab.
- **2026-11-20, phase 2.1 merged into the integration branch, and step 11b.** `expansion/phase-2` (tag `expansion-phase-2.1`: steps 9c's follow-ups and 9d) was merged into `expansion/integration`, where it met the clinic time zone (step 10d) and the FHIR API (step 11). Four files conflicted (the plan, `docs/UC4.md`, `_Trend.cshtml` and `Trend`'s summary) and were resolved keeping both sides: clinician trends show the clinic's clock and the "needs follow-up" wording. 977 tests on the merged tree, 933 pass, 44 performance cases skipped without a results file; CI green on the merge (run 44848508598), its benchmark job passing.

A unit test maps a child's and an adult's reading and validates both against R4's blood pressure profile. CI green; benchmark on the merged tree at p99 excluding the commit: chart 11.6 ms, vital signs 4.8, FHIR observations 2.9, off-site chart 10.5.
  - **The FHIR API checked on the local stack,** which step 11's entry left to do: signed in as the clinician, on-site and off-site, metadata, a patient (with the MRN's system), and an adult's and a child's vital signs answered as `application/fhir+json`, never cached, with no CORS headers; each blood pressure is one Observation with two components, an adult's ranges as numbers and a child's as the screening sentence, and no systolic or diastolic appears alone; anonymous is 401 with an OperationOutcome, an unknown patient 403, a write 405 on both sites; each answered request wrote exactly one audit event, the off-site ones arriving through the queue.
