- **2026-11-21, step 12. Simulated lab (new use case UC 20; proposed FR-54).** Built by a developer agent from an approved plan, in a clone of its own, and applied here as patches; reviewed three times. Migration `LabKeys` (the lab key's hash; no data moves). **Protected paths: `db/policies`** (`76-lab.sql`, new; `30-rls.sql`, five owner-only policies behind `app.lab_door`; `75-results.sql`, the `observations_file` trigger's condition; `40-audit.sql`, the write's source; `20-change-log.sql`; a comment in `10-grants.sql`) **and authorization** (`Api/Lab/LabApi.cs`, its mapping in `Program.cs`, `AccountRateLimits.cs`, `CloudForwardingMiddleware.cs`). What was decided is in the decision log's step 12 entry.
  - *What it does:* a lab that presents its key in `X-Lab-Key`, with nobody signed in, asks the on-site app for lab orders signed since a watermark and posts results for one order at a time. The hand-over carries the order's ID, when it was signed, the tests still waiting with the clinic's unit and range, and the patient's current diagnosis codes that aren't sensitive: no name, MRN, birth date, sex or age. Results are filed through `ehr.file_result`, all or none and once; the ordering clinician is told through the inbox; the audit log shows each hand-over (`sent_to_lab`) and each write with source `lab` and no actor. The database checks the key itself, by its hash, so SQL as the app's role without the key hands over and files nothing.
  - *The simulator* (`src/Ehr.Lab.Simulator`, base class library only, HTTP only) draws each value from the test's own range, shifts it for the diagnoses the reference data has (diabetes, hyperlipidemia, hypothyroidism, vitamin D deficiency, chronic kidney disease, anaemia), is abnormal otherwise at a low rate, and is deterministic for an order's ID. It runs as a command, and as the `lab` service of the local stack.
  - **The lab is optional,** which the plan did not say: developers' existing `.env` files have no key, and I may not edit them. With none the stack, the app and the migration job behave as before, and the `lab` service says so in one line and idles.
  - *Mine to own:* my brief asked for sex and age in the hand-over and for the lab service to exit when it has no key; the developer showed lab ranges don't use sex or age (not sent), and that a service that exits fails `docker compose up --wait` (it idles). Its plan also caught that an insert made inside an owner-run function skipped `ehr.file_result` altogether, which my design would have shipped.
  - *Reviews:* (1) failed, on the HTTP door, though the database layer held against every attempt: keyless requests, forwarded by the cloud app, used up the door's one rate-limit window and locked the real lab out; a collection time not in UTC was an error page, and the simulator would have retried forever under a non-UTC database session; the app passed the database the hash of its own key, so the database's check was no second layer for an HTTP caller; a staff cookie beside the key was admitted; the owner-only policies were open to any owner-run function. All fixed. (2) confirmed those fixes and could not break them (floods of every shape, 21 path and method variants through the cloud app, 362 orders in mixed batches each handed over once); six smaller items fixed, among them a test that could not see one kind of function and a time with no offset read in the server's zone.
  - *Checked on the local stack,* with a throwaway key passed from a scratch file, never from `.env`: with no key, the stack came up as before and the lab idled; with it, a TSH order and a lipid panel signed through the pages came back in 19 seconds as TSH 6.3 (High; the patient has hypothyroidism), LDL 112 and total cholesterol 239 (both high; hyperlipidemia), HDL and triglycerides in range; the audit log showed 2 hand-overs, 5 results, 2 orders completed and 2 inbox items, all source `lab` with no actor; the database held one hash. With keyless requests against both addresses throughout, a new order was still filed in 13 seconds. The lab's container sees only the public certificate.
  - *Gates:* format clean; Release build; 1,038 tests, 992 pass, 46 performance cases skipped without a results file; CI green on the final head, its stack job checking the door with a key and its benchmark job passing (lab hand-over 5.1 ms, lab filing 8.4 ms, chart 15.4 ms at p99 excluding the commit). Screenshots, light and dark at 1280 and 640, looked at: the Results page waiting with its notice, and with the lab's results.
  - *Not done:* no browser test (no new page; the Results page gained a notice); the simulator's image was built for arm64 only on this Mac, and by CI on its runner; the desktop app is untouched.
  - **For Tahseen:** the four defaults in the decision log's step 12 entry, and whether to set `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` for the app roles. To see the lab on your stack, add an `EHR_LAB_KEY` line to `infra/compose/.env` (`.env.example` says how); the stack is running now with my throwaway key, and goes back to no lab the next time it is brought up from `.env` alone.
- **2026-11-21, end of phase 3.** Tag `expansion-phase-3` and branch `expansion/phase-3`, at the merge of step 12. The pull request title and description are under "Phase merges"; Tahseen opens it, after phase 2's. Nothing was pushed to `main`, no pull request was opened, no history was rewritten and no branch or tag was deleted or moved.
  - *Resume at phase 4,* step 13a, visit summaries: rewrite the sensitive-note proxy test there (Tahseen's note under 13a).
- **2026-11-21, step 13a. Visit summaries (extends UC 6 and UC 14; proposed FR-55).** Built by a developer agent from an approved plan, in a clone of its own, and applied here as patches; reviewed twice. Migration `VisitSummaries` (one new table, `ehr.visit_summaries`; no data moves). **Protected paths: `db/policies`** (`52-visit-summaries.sql`, new: the plain-words rule, the guard and no-delete triggers, the inherit-and-follow trigger, the withdrawal's inbox item, and three owner-run functions, `ehr.standing_visit_summaries`, `ehr.withdrawn_visit_summaries` and `ehr.withdrawn_summary_was_viewed`; `30-rls.sql`, the table's policies, with no policy for portal sessions; `10-grants.sql`; `40-audit.sql`). No authorization attribute or policy changed.
  - *What it does:* from a signed note, its signer or another clinician with the patient on their panel writes a summary for the patient in three parts (what we talked about, what we found, what happens next), which the database refuses while it holds a diagnosis, medication, lab or order code, a record number or an ID, or a line copied from the note. Released, it can't be changed, only withdrawn with a reason; the portal then keeps Tahseen's placeholder sentence in its place, the patient gets the generic email only if the summary had been viewed, and a releaser who isn't the one withdrawing gets an inbox item. A summary starts sensitive when its note or an addendum is, can be marked on its own, and follows its sources afterwards under the note-and-addendum rules; a proxy without sensitive items loses it at once and is given no hint of it. The patient and proxies read summaries at `/portal/summaries`.
  - *Tahseen's test:* the sensitive-note proxy test (`NoteSensitivitySecurityTests`) is rewritten as he asked, keeping every earlier assertion: a proxy is given no note, no addendum and, without sensitive items, no summary of a sensitive note, and no portal session reads a row of the table at all.
  - *Reviews:* (1) failed, though no route to a note's text, a withdrawn summary or another patient's data worked: a portal session could read, from the database, the note's own sensitivity reason copied onto the summary, and that a summary had once been sensitive; the plain-words rule scanned the code tables for every code-shaped word, twice a release and under the note's lock (16 to 18 seconds at worst); one clause of the update policy refused marking a note after a colleague's withdrawal and did not do what its comment said. Fixed: portal sessions get summaries only through `ehr.standing_visit_summaries`, seven columns and no more, and an inherited mark carries a fixed reason; the rule looks each word up by index, once, before the lock (the same worst case now takes 2 to 12 ms; a release with twenty such words went from 605 ms to 10 ms); the clause is gone and the comment says what holds. Also fixed: characters that show nothing or look alike (zero-width, fullwidth, other dashes) are read as they look, with what is stored left as typed; the "was it viewed" check answers only for the transaction's own withdrawal; a note with no addenda has its own test; the portal page records its reads in one statement. (2) passed: each fix confirmed by the reviewer's own probes, 47 ordinary phrases ("A1c", "B12", "COVID-19", "10–20 mg") still accepted, no test weakened, every mutation but one harmless one caught.
  - *Known limits, written down in `docs/UC14.md` and the decision log:* the rule can't catch a code typed with a space in it (a hair space is nearly invisible) or with another alphabet's look-alike letter; SQL as the app role can change the user mid-statement (the same limit as steps 9b and 10b); a clinician can withdraw, ask whether the summary was viewed and roll back; a view made off-site while the link is down reaches the audit log late, so a withdrawal in between sends no email.
  - *Mine to own:* my first brief let the portal read the table through a policy; the review showed that hands a portal session every column. And the Mac's disk filled during this step (several lanes' clones, test databases and Docker's build cache), which stopped Docker for every lane for about an hour and a half: finished clones and the unused build cache were removed, and lanes now keep one clone, run the full gate once on a finished series, and leave the 50,000-patient benchmark to CI.
  - *Checked on the local stack through the pages, before and after the fixes:* a summary written from a new signed note; "J06.9" refused with only its own part marked; released; read in the portal as the patient at 1280 and 360 px; withdrawn with a reason; the placeholder in its place, padded as the summary's card is. No ID or MRN in the portal's markup.
  - *Gates:* format clean; Release build; 1,125 tests, 1,074 pass, 51 performance cases skipped without a results file; cargo fmt, clippy and tests pass (the desktop's copy of the stylesheet changed); CI green on the final head, its benchmark job passing (summary form 9.0 ms, withdraw form 8.0, portal summaries 4.9 as the patient and 5.3 as a proxy, at p99 excluding the commit). Screenshots, light and dark, looked at: the note with and without a summary, the form, a refusal, the withdraw form, the chart's tag, and the portal page at 1280 and 360.
  - *Not done:* after a refusal the keyboard focus returns to the first part, not the part named (the message names it and the part is marked); the other portal cards (messages, appointments, the record chooser, access) have the same missing padding the summary cards had.
  - *Heads-up for teammates:* Simar (UC 6: a new portal page, a home link, TC-UC6-15 to 23), Satwik (UC 14: the note's page gains the summary section and its sensitivity; TC-UC14-18 to 25), Diego (UC 4: a "summary" tag on the chart's note list), and UC 15 gains the inbox kind `summary-withdrawn`.
  - *Resume:* merge steps 13b and 13c as their reviews pass, then 13c2, 13d and 13e.
- **2026-11-21, step 13b. Download my record (extends UC 6; proposed FR-56).** Built by a developer agent from an approved plan, in a clone of its own, and applied here as patches; reviewed three times; merged with step 13a on its branch before the last check. No migration, no new table, no new package. **Protected paths: `db/policies`** (`30-rls.sql`, one policy: `medication_statements_own_select` no longer gives a portal session a medication entered in error) **and, authorization-adjacent, where a sign-in lands** (`Accounts/Links.cs`; the portal's `SignIn.cshtml.cs`; the staff sign-in's `SignIn.cshtml.cs`, `Code.cshtml.cs` and `Enroll.cshtml.cs`; `SearchQuery.Parameters`).
  - *What it does:* at `/portal/record` the patient, or someone they share the record with, downloads a copy as a FHIR R4 collection bundle or opens a page laid out to print. The copy holds allergies, medications, diagnoses, released results, vital signs and appointments, and the printable page the 50 newest visit summaries at most; never a note's text, a draft, a withdrawn summary, an entry in error, an unreleased result, a cancellation reason or anything of the audit log. Sensitive items are left out unless asked for by someone allowed them, are marked R in the file, and the download is then recorded as `download_sensitive`. Every id in the file is made for that download, so no database key leaves the clinic. The FHIR mapping moved out of the clinician API (`src/Ehr.Web/Fhir`) and both doors use it; the API's answers are byte for byte what they were (38 requests compared by the reviewer). The stylesheet gained its first print rules, for every page.
  - **This is the first time the portal shows allergies, medications and diagnoses** (the developer's decision 7, for Tahseen to review with the others listed in the decision log).
  - *Reviews:* (1) failed on two findings: a link from another site, followed through sign-in, completed a download with sensitive items and without the warning; and the print rules hid every band, so a proxy's printout of any portal page no longer said whose record it was. Fixed: after sign-in the app opens the page a link named and drops its query; bands print. Also fixed then: HEAD of the file or the printable page is refused before anything is read; the database, not only the app, now withholds a medication entered in error from the portal; the cap on visit summaries is stated; two test gaps closed. (2) confirmed those and found the staff sign-in could still carry a link's query into the portal for a browser holding both sessions. Fixed for every staff landing; the staff sign-in keeps only a patient search's own criteria, which an existing test holds. (3) passed, with `docs/UC11.md` to add (added): 22 crafted return addresses, all eight mutations caught, no test weakened.
  - *Mine:* the withdrawn summary's sentence, which steps 13a and 13b each had a copy of, is one partial (`_WithdrawnSummary.cshtml`).
  - *Checked on the local stack through the pages:* a crafted link through the portal sign-in lands on the download page with the box unticked; the file is `application/fhir+json`, an attachment named for the date, `no-store`, a collection of 47 resources with no database id; HEAD of it is 405; the printable page at 1280 and 360 px, and printed (navy on white, the app bar and buttons gone, no row split).
  - *Gates:* format clean; Release build; 1,149 tests on the merged branch (architecture 11, data 3, security 419, web 723 less the reviewer's two probes), none failed, 58 performance cases skipped without a results file; CI green on the final code, its benchmark job passing (the file 8.7 ms and the printable page 10.4 as the patient, 8.9 and 10.5 for the largest seeded record, chart 15.7, at p99 excluding the commit). Screenshots, light and dark at 1280 and 360, looked at: the download page, the printable page, and its print.
  - *Not done, and why:* an app-wide refusal of HEAD on signed-in pages. Every page runs its GET for a HEAD and records the read with nothing shown (on the visit summaries page that counts a summary as viewed), but TC-UC2-07, an audit test of Tahseen's, asserts a HEAD's event is written before the response, and I don't change an audit test's expectation. Steps 13b and 13d refuse HEAD on their own pages.
  - **For Tahseen:** decision 7 above; the HEAD question; a staff link can still make a clinician run a patient search on arrival, each listed patient recorded as their read (`docs/UC11.md`; closing it changes what `LoggingTests` expects, and it is listed under step 13c2); `docs/UC11.md` is new, in Luke's area.

- **2026-11-21, step 13c. Who viewed my record (extends UC 6; proposed FR-57).** Built by a developer agent from an approved plan, in a clone of its own, and applied here as patches; reviewed five times; merged with steps 13a and 13b on its branch (seven files conflicted, each resolved keeping both steps' changes: the README, `docs/UC18.md`, the plan's requirement rows, the portal home's links, the benchmark's portal rows, and the cloud audit allow-list with its tests). No migration. **Protected paths: `db/policies`** (`42-record-views.sql`, new: the owner-run `ehr.record_views`, the part rule `ehr.record_view_part`, and a guard trigger on `ehr.patient_assignments`; `30-rls.sql`: one owner-only SELECT policy on `ehr.break_glass_accesses`, and a comment). `CloudAuditEvents` accepts one new shape.
  - *What it does:* `/portal/views` lists, newest first and 50 a page, each time the record was shown to someone in the last 12 months: a staff member by name and role, "You", someone the record is shared with by the address access was given to, or the lab's hand-over; when; which part as the log records it; and a view through emergency access marked, never its reason. The audit log stays the admins' alone: no policy on it was added, and a portal session reads only through the one function, which takes no patient and answers only for the record the session is looking at. Looking is itself one recorded read.
  - **Decided without Tahseen, the main one** (decision 9 in the step's entry): the build showed someone the record is shared with every staff member's views, emergency access included, where the requirement said the care team's. Now a proxy without sensitive items sees a staff member's view only if that person had the patient on their panel at that moment, so never an emergency-access line; a proxy with sensitive items sees every staff view, marked, as the patient does. The emergency-access reason page tells clinicians the patient sees that they used it and when, never the reason (UC 12, Luke's).
  - *Reviews:* (1) found no leak through the function (a proxy's year of pages unchanged after 24,000 hidden events) and failed the step on the proxy rule above and three unproven clauses of the emergency mark. (2) the fix read the panel's history only when needed, so a page's time told a restricted proxy that one hidden off-panel view existed; and SQL as the app role could backdate an assignment and change what a proxy saw. Fixed: the history is read for every such page, and a guard keeps an assignment's start and end at the present. (3) that read worked the session out again for every row of the panels' table: 0.6 s a page at 60,000 rows, which CI could not see with 100. Fixed by working it out once (about 2 ms); a plan test fails if it returns, and the benchmark now has 5,000 ended assignments. The guard was changed to set a time that is more than a minute off to the database's clock, not refuse it: refusing would have blocked "Add to my patients" on a machine whose clocks had drifted, and in a clinic a refused assignment forces emergency access. (4) passed with one low finding, fixed by me: an assignment could be made to end up to two minutes before it began. Each round's surviving mutations were closed in the next.
  - *Known limits, in the decision log:* a restricted proxy can still learn roughly how many views are hidden from how long a page takes, never who or when (each probe is itself a view the patient sees); a clock set wrong on the cloud server can show or hide one view at the edge of an assignment; a clinician with tens of thousands of past assignments slows the emergency mark's lookup (21 to 26 ms a page at 60,000 rows), which the index planned for step 13c2 fixes; SQL as the app role can insert audit events, which the patient would now see as views.
  - *Mine to own:* I approved a plan whose proxy rule did not match the requirement's words, and my first fix instruction for the timing leak produced the 0.6 s page.
  - *Checked on the local stack through the pages:* a clinician opened the chart, then asked for its sensitive items; the patient's page listed those views by name and role with "Your record, with its sensitive items", the day's earlier download as "Your record, downloaded", and the patient's own views as "You"; no ID, MRN or staff address in the markup; `no-store`; nothing scrolls sideways at 360 px.
  - *Gates:* format clean; Release build; brand check; on the merged branch the security project (433 tests) and the UC 6, UC 11, UC 12 and cloud audit web test cases pass locally, and CI is green on the merged code with the full suite and its benchmark job (who viewed 5.9 ms as the patient and 6.5 as a proxy, with the clinic-sized panels table; chart 15.1; at p99 excluding the commit). Screenshots, light and dark at 1280 and 360, looked at: the page as the patient, and the emergency-access reason page.
  - *Not done:* "which part" is coarse until step 13c2: opening a chart reads as "N visit notes" because the chart records a read of each note it lists. The page does not refuse HEAD (the question recorded under step 13b).
  - **For Tahseen:** the proxy rule (decision 9) and the clamping guard (decision 10); the limits above; and, from step 13b's review, that a staff link can make a clinician run a patient search, which this page now shows those patients.
  - *Heads-up for teammates:* Simar (UC 6: a home link, a page, TC-UC6-40 to 50), Luke (UC 12: one sentence on the reason page and in `docs/UC12.md`; patients now see emergency access), UC 18 and UC 2 (a line each), and Diego (UC 4): adding a patient to a panel now has its times checked by the database.
  - *Resume:* step 13c2 (mine), while steps 13d and 13e finish in their lanes.
- **2026-11-22, phase 2.2: the tests read the database's clock (a phase 2 follow-up on `expansion/phase-2`, merged into the integration branch).** Tests only; no product code, no `db/`, no protected path.
  - *What was wrong:* during this phase's work, vital-sign, lab and note tests failed now and then with "A vital sign is taken in a lifetime, and not in the future", on unchanged code. I first took it for the time of day, then for load. A developer agent traced it: this Mac had been going to sleep in cycles of about 15 minutes (`pmset -g log`, some logged as "Dark Wake Thermal Emergency"), Docker Desktop's clock stops while it sleeps, and for 5 to 35 seconds after each wake the test databases were up to 16 minutes behind the Mac. The tests sent times built from the Mac's clock (some fixed when a test class loaded) to rules the database judges by its own clock. Under load alone the two clocks stayed within a fifth of a second. One test was truly tied to the hour: a five-year-old's birth date counted from today and not from the reading's day, which failed daily from 00:00 to 00:10 UTC.
  - *The fix:* each test reads the database's clock once at its start (`MigratedDatabase.NowAsync()`) and builds the same offsets from it; brackets around times the database stamps use its clock; two download tests accept the file name of the UTC day just before or just after the download. Five commits on `expansion/phase-2` (tag `expansion-phase-2.2`, CI green), merged into `expansion/integration` with the six calls in later tests folded into the merge, and two more commits there for the lab, FHIR and clinic-time tests.
  - *Checked:* the developer moved the database's clock with libfaketime (17 minutes behind, and to six hours of the day): the old tests fail as seen, the fixed ones pass. An independent review went hunk by hunk: every expected value, tolerance and case count is unchanged, and eight mutations each turned a test red.
  - *Not fixed:* TC-UC4-18, a browser test that submits the form's default time, still fails if the database lags by more than five minutes; the render benchmark sends a time ten minutes old and has the same exposure past fifteen.
  - *Mine:* I kept the Mac from idle sleep with `caffeinate` for eight hours from 05:30 UTC, which changes no setting; the thermal sleeps are Tahseen's to look at.
  - **For Tahseen, a product question this turned up:** on the night the clocks go back, the vital-signs form offers a default time in the repeated hour, which the page reads as its first occurrence, so a set saved with the default is stored an hour early and can miss a visit checked in minutes before (next on 2027-11-07; TC-UC4-23 fails in that hour). Either keep the rule and make the test avoid the hour, or have the form carry the offset. And off-site, a cloud server whose clock runs more than a minute ahead of the clinic's would have the form's default time refused.
- **2026-11-22, step 13d. Refill requests (new use case UC 21; extends UC 6; proposed FR-58).** Built by a developer agent from an approved plan, in a clone of its own, and applied here as patches; reviewed three times. Migration `RefillRequests` (one new table, `ehr.refill_requests`; no data moves). **Protected paths: `db/policies`** (`77-refill-requests.sql`, new: the guard, the no-delete trigger, delivery to the care team's inboxes, two triggers on `ehr.patient_assignments`, the sensitivity trigger on `ehr.medication_statements`, and the portal's owner-run `ehr.portal_refill_requests`; `60-orders.sql`, **step 1's duplicate-order rule in `ehr.sign_order`**; `30-rls.sql`, `10-grants.sql`, `40-audit.sql`, `70-inbox.sql`; `42-record-views.sql`, one line). No authorization attribute or policy changed.
  - *What it does:* at `/portal/refills` the patient, or someone they share the record with whose access includes messages, asks the care team to renew an active medication that is coded as a catalog product, one open request a medication, with an optional note. Each clinician with the patient on their panel gets an inbox item; a request stays with the care team as it changes (whoever joins the panel gets the waiting requests, whoever leaves loses theirs). A clinician approves only by signing, through the order form and every check of steps 1 and 8, an order for the same product that was asked for and that the chart still has; or declines with a message the patient reads in the portal. Each request is answered once and kept. A request is sensitive when its medication is. The patient is told by the generic email, at most once on each of the clinic's days.
  - **Decided without Tahseen, the two that matter most** (the step's entry lists them all): *an approval is the same product.* The plan as first approved accepted any order with the same ingredients; the first review had a request for an extended-release tablet approved by an injection of another salt. A different strength, form, salt or route is now declined with a message and ordered separately. *"Already on order" goes by product, for every order* (UC 1's rule): the clinic's common items and RxNorm's are the same products under two catalog codes, and the duplicate check compared codes, so the same-product rule let a refill be approved beside an active order for the clinic's item with no warning. It now matches by RxNorm product, in the database and on the form alike.
  - *Reviews:* (1) failed, the safety core and every privacy boundary holding: a waiting request was stranded for good when the care team changed; "same medication" was too loose; approving could deadlock with marking the medication sensitive; a clinician locked out for 15 minutes counted as no care team; the once-a-day email rule sent only the first email ever (the same defect in step 13a's summaries, fixed with it). (2) confirmed those fixed and failed on what my same-product ruling opened: the duplicate gap above; an approval checked against today's chart entry and not what was asked for; and a request stranded when a patient asks at the moment a clinician joins. Fixed, with a lock for each patient's care team. (3) passed with two comments to correct (done, by me): every mutation caught, no test weakened.
  - *Mine to own:* the same-product ruling was right but I did not look at what else compared catalog codes. And in my first walk on the local stack the approval went through beside an active order for the clinic's own metformin item, with no warning, and I did not notice; the second walk, on the fixed build, shows the warning naming both orders.
  - *CI caught one test of the step's own:* its care-team test asserted the shared fixture clinician's inbox held no refill item at all, and failed whenever another test of the class ran first. It now checks its own request's item and link (by me).
  - *Checked on the local stack through the pages:* the patient asked to renew metformin with a note; the clinician opened it from the inbox, the order form came with the product fixed and read-only, and signing needed the reason for ordering again because the patient already had two active orders for that product (RxNorm's, and the clinic's own item: both named); the request then read "Approved", every inbox item for it was done, and the portal showed what was ordered. No ID in the portal's markup; nothing scrolls sideways at 360 px. The local database's refill table was altered by hand to match the migration, which changed after the stack had applied it.
  - *Gates:* format clean; Release build; brand check; 1,245 tests, 1,181 pass, 64 performance cases skipped without a results file; CI green on the final head (run 44992889310), its benchmark job passing (in the last benchmark run read, on the head before the merge of phase 2.2: the request's page 5.8 ms, the order form approving a refill 7.0, the portal's page 5.9 as the patient and 6.1 as a proxy, chart 12.7, at p99 excluding the commit). Screenshots, light and dark at 1280 and 360, looked at: the portal's page asking, waiting and approved; the inbox; the request's page; the order form with its duplicate warning.
  - *Known limits, in `docs/UC21.md` and the decision log:* a renewal at another strength is two steps (decline, then order); two answers committed at the same instant may each send the day's email; transactions that join several patients' panels must take them in one order (the app joins one at a time); a patient cannot withdraw a request.
  - **For Tahseen:** the two decisions above (UC 1's duplicate rule is yours); lab orders have the same two-codes shape and are not matched by product (left alone: a doubled lab is a much smaller risk than a doubled medicine); the benchmark logs about 65,000 antiforgery warnings that a no-store page's cache header was overridden (every such page with a form does it; not fixed here).
  - *Heads-up for teammates:* Simar (UC 6: a home link, a page, TC-UC6-51 to 58), Diego (UC 4: a trigger on medications follows their sensitivity; adding or removing a patient from a panel now moves refill items), Satwik (UC 14: the visit-summary email is once on each clinic day, not once in any 24 hours), and UC 15 (a refill item is done only by answering it).
- **2026-11-22, step 13e. Pre-visit forms (new use case UC 22; proposed FR-59).** Built by a developer agent from an approved plan, in a clone of its own, and applied here as patches; reviewed five times. Migration `PreVisitForms` (two new tables, `ehr.questionnaire_requests` and `ehr.questionnaire_responses`; a `sensitive` column, a new kind `form-alert` and a check on step 3's `ehr.inbox_items`; no data moves). **Protected paths: `db/policies`** (`85-pre-visit-forms.sql`, new: the scoring function, the guards on asking and answering, the no-change trigger, delivery of an answer to review, and owner-run triggers on `ehr.patient_assignments` and on `ehr.staff_users`; `30-rls.sql`, `10-grants.sql`, `40-audit.sql`, `42-record-views.sql`; a comment in `77-refill-requests.sql`) **and authorization** (`requestId` among the IDs a portal handler may take, `PortalSubjectTests`; a clinician who holds an open inbox item for an answer may open that one answer without emergency access, `PhiReader.FormAnswersAsync` and `Forms/Response.cshtml.cs`). `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: the web job's time limit, 30 to 45 minutes (mine).
  - *What it does:* a clinician with the patient on their panel asks the patient to fill in a questionnaire before a booked appointment; the PHQ-9 and GAD-7 are built in, as data, worded as their authors publish them. The patient, or someone they share the record with who is allowed sensitive items, answers once in the portal, from seven days before the appointment until it ends (a checked-in appointment's forms stay open while the visit is in progress, up to twelve hours past the scheduled end). The database scores the answers and never takes a score from the app. Clinicians see, for that visit and as a trend drawn with step 9's chart, the score, its severity band and whether it needs follow-up. Every request, answer and score is a sensitive item and can never be unmarked; the patient sees their answers, never the score or band; nothing is emailed.
  - **Patient safety, decided without Tahseen** (the step's entry lists every decision): every form says it is not read as it arrives, with 911 and 988; any answer to the PHQ-9's ninth question above "Not at all" shows a fixed help message, which does not repeat the answer, and puts an item at the top of the inbox of the appointment's clinician and the patient's care team. If none of them can be told, every enabled clinician is; a clinician who joins the care team later is told; and when an account holding such an item is disabled, the answer is told again. A clinician holding an open item may read that one answer without emergency access, until they mark it done. **The help wording and the choice of 911 and 988 are mine: a clinician should read them before any real use.**
  - *Reviews:* (1) failed on the safety path, the database held: an answer to review could be stored with nobody told; a recipient off the panel had to claim emergency access to read it; a form that closed while the patient was filling it in returned a blank page, even after a question-9 answer; one proxy was shown another's name; three mutations survived. (2) passed with fixes: an answer held only by a clinician later disabled reached nobody; and, with the every-clinician fallback, the way in from the inbox stayed open for good (narrowed by me: it closes when the item is marked done). (3) passed with four small items: a link left in the inbox's Done tab, a missing test, a bound on how long an answer waits behind an admin's account change (5 seconds, and the answer is then stored and told regardless), and a lock for a clinician disabled at the moment they join a panel. (4) passed, with one doc line and one safety margin, both done: when an answer cannot get the alerts' lock within its 5 seconds it is now told to every enabled clinician as well, so it cannot rest with an account being disabled at that moment; that last change (a few lines of `85-pre-visit-forms.sql`, with its test and mutation) was not sent back for a fifth check. Each round's surviving mutations were closed in the next; 30 rounds of five things at once left no deadlock and no answer without an enabled clinician holding it.
  - *Found by me on the local stack:* at desktop width the page that shows a form's answers was broken, each answer squeezed into a sliver at the far right and the page scrolling sideways; the browser test looked at 360 px only. Answers now sit under their questions at every width, for the patient and the clinician, tested at 1280, 640 and 360.
  - *Sources and licence:* `docs/expansion/reference-data.md` quotes every statement found, with URLs, dates and the PDFs' SHA-256. The publisher's manual says the instruments are in the public domain and need no permission; LOINC's notice and the 2001 paper say "Copyright © Pfizer Inc." beside the same no-permission sentence. Tahseen's plan names them as free to use, so the lane did not stop. The GAD-7's band below 5 reads "Below mild", marked as the clinic's wording: the 2006 paper that names it could not be read (a browser check), and I do not write a band from memory.
  - *Checked on the local stack through the pages:* a visit booked, a PHQ-9 asked for, answered as the patient with question 9 "Several days": the help message led the page, no score or band was shown to the patient, the item was first in the clinician's inbox, the chart said "Needs review", and the forms page read "Score 7 of 27 · Mild" with "An answer needs review: question 9". No ID in a page title.
  - *Gates:* format clean; Release build; brand check; 1,294 tests, 1,224 pass, 70 performance cases skipped without a results file; CI green on the code before the last two small commits, its benchmark job passing (the clinician's forms page 6.3 ms hidden and 9.5 with sensitive items, a form's answers 7.9, the portal's forms page 5.8 and a form 5.2, chart 16.4, at p99 excluding the commit). Screenshots, light and dark at 1280 and 360, looked at: the clinician's forms page asking and answered, the portal's list, a form, the page after sending, the answers.
  - *Not built, for Tahseen to decide:* forms in the FHIR API and in Download my record (the step does not ask for them; fewer ways out for sensitive answers); an inbox item for a high score without a question-9 answer; telling administrators when no clinician account is enabled.
  - **For Tahseen:** the safety decisions above and the wording; the licence statements; the GAD-7 paper (Spitzer et al., Arch Intern Med 2006) if he can supply it; the twelve-hour end for a checked-in appointment's forms (the database is not told the clinic's time zone, so "the end of the clinic's day" could not be enforced); the web job's 45 minutes.
  - *Heads-up for teammates:* Simar (UC 6: a permanent home link for those allowed, one sentence on Download my record, a part on Who viewed), Diego (UC 4: the chart's link, count and notice), Sara (UC 7/8: the offline copy's "not kept" count includes forms; no desktop code changed), and UC 15 (step 3's table gains a column, a kind and a check; alert items sort first), UC 16 and UC 18 (a few sentences each). Disabling a staff account now runs a trigger (UC 11, Luke).
- **2026-11-22, step 13c2. Who viewed my record: which part (extends UC 6; FR-57's "which part").** Built by the step 13c developer agent from an approved plan, applied here as patches, replayed onto steps 13d and 13e so that its migration is the last; reviewed three times. Migration `AuditEventParts`, partly written by hand: a nullable `part` column on `ehr.audit_events` (no rewrite of the table; a check added `NOT VALID`, so the log is not read under the lock; a 5-second lock timeout, so a busy log fails the migration job cleanly instead of stalling the apps) and the index `ix_patient_assignments_history`. **Protected paths: `db/policies`** (`42-record-views.sql`: the part names a line and never decides whether it is shown; the function is replaced in place; `10-grants.sql`: the column's read grant; **`61-order-safety.sql`, step 8's file in UC 1**: the same in-place replacement). The audit path changed: `AuditRecorder`, `AuditEventBatch`, `AuditEventImport`, `CloudAuditEvents`.
  - *What it does:* every read of patient data records which part of the record it was for, a fixed key from a closed list that the database checks (never free text, a route or an ID), set by the PHI reader, carried through the off-site queue, and shown as a column and a filter on the admin's audit page. "Who viewed my record" names it: "Your test results", "Your vital signs", "A visit note", "Your name, in their list of patients", and "Your chart: the summary page, which lists your visit notes" as one line where step 13c said "3 visit notes". Views recorded before the column keep step 13c's words.
  - **Decided without Tahseen** (twelve, in the step's entry; the ones that matter): someone the record is shared with, without sensitive items, is told a part only where it cannot hint at what is hidden from them, and reads "The record" for the lists, orders, the inbox, access, notes and refill requests (they can tell by elimination that such a line was one of those, never which); a view of a pre-visit form is never shown to them at all; the part is a closed list in a database check, so a new part needs a migration; a read recorded without a part is refused by the recorder, so a forgotten one fails a test.
  - *Reviews:* (1) nothing a proxy sees had changed (pages identical under 510,000 events with hidden ones of every part), and it failed on: the who-viewed function being dropped and created again on every run of the policies, so a page calling it at that moment failed (7 of 423 calls); the forms rule having no test that would notice its removal; and six pages given a wrong part going unnoticed. All fixed; the same drop-and-create defect was found and fixed in step 8's order-safety function (the old file failed 2 of 34 calls). (2) on the replay, all held and it failed on wording: a visit to a forms page that showed no form read "A pre-visit form", and two such visits "2 pre-visit forms", to a patient with one. Now "Your pre-visit forms page, with the forms hidden", counted as views. (3) passed, with one false comment corrected (by me).
  - *What CI caught:* the render benchmark failed its gate on the clinician's home page (20.4 ms at p99 excluding the commit, from 13.9). The cause was not the long panel history the benchmark's clinician was given: the part column's check adds about 13 microseconds to each single-row audit insert, and the home page wrote a hundred of them one by one. A list's reads are now written in one statement in the page's transaction (0.42 ms for a hundred rows, with or without the check), as the visit summaries page already did; the review compared the rows written both ways and found them identical. The web job was also cancelled at its 30-minute limit with no test failed, and now has 45 (step 13e's entry).
  - *Checked on the local stack through the pages:* a clinician opened the chart, then asked for its sensitive items; the patient's page read "Your chart, with its sensitive items: the summary page, which lists your visit notes" and "Your name, in their list of patients", with earlier views still "Your record"; no ID, MRN or staff address in the markup.
  - *Gates:* format clean; Release build; brand check; 1,364 tests, 1,294 pass, 70 performance cases skipped without a results file. CI's result on the final head, and its benchmark figures, are in the end-of-phase entry below.
  - *Known limits, in the decision log:* a chart that lists more notes than a page holds can put its one line on two pages; the migration makes audit inserts, and "Add to my patients", wait for at most 5 seconds if the log is busy when it runs; what the cloud standby does while the lock is replayed is documented from PostgreSQL's documentation and not verified here.
  - **For Tahseen:** the decisions above; step 8's file changed (UC 1 is his); a staff link that makes a clinician run a patient search on arrival is still open (step 13b's entry), and this page now shows those patients the clinician as a viewer.
  - *Heads-up for teammates:* Simar (UC 6: the words of TC-UC6-40, 41, 42, 44, 45, 48 and 50 changed; TC-UC6-63 to 70 are new), Luke (UC 12: the audit page's Part column and filter), and UC 2 (the queue carries the part; deploy order is migrate first, then the apps).
- **2026-11-22, end of phase 4.** Steps 13a, 13b, 13c, 13d, 13e and 13c2 are merged into `expansion/integration`, each on a green CI run of its final head; the last, step 13c2's (run 45010042022: 1,364 tests, 1,294 pass, 70 performance cases skipped; the benchmark job passing with chart 15.1 ms, the clinician's home page 7.4, search 10.7, who viewed 7.4 and 9.2, at p99 excluding the commit; the web job 29 minutes 11 seconds of its 45). The phase's tag, `expansion-phase-4`, and branch, `expansion/phase-4`, are one plan-only commit after `expansion-step-13c2` (this entry and the pull request draft under "Phase merges"). Nothing was pushed to `main`, no pull request was opened, no history was rewritten and no branch or tag was deleted or moved.
  - **A tag is wrong and waits for Tahseen:** `expansion-step-13e` points at 1f0db79, the commit before step 13e's merge (b5c758a). The merge stopped on a conflict in this file's status table; my command, whose failure a pipe had hidden, went on to create and push the tag. The merge was then finished and pushed, and the integration branch is right. Moving the tag means deleting a pushed tag, which is his to allow. The same habit (reading a filtered command's last line as its result) had earlier made me report a failed CI run as finished; I now check each such step's own exit status.
  - *How the phase ran:* in parallel lanes, as Tahseen asked: about three developer agents at a time, each in a clone of its own, each step reviewed by an agent that had not written it, three to five times, before it was merged. The reviews found the defects listed in the pull request draft. It took longer than any earlier phase: each round was a fix, a CI run of about 35 minutes and a re-check; the Mac's disk filled once and stopped Docker; and the Mac slept in cycles overnight, which stalled the session and produced test failures that took their own investigation (phase 2.2).

- **2026-11-22, step 14. SOAP notes and visit-type templates (extends UC 9, UC 10, UC 13, UC 14; proposed FR-60).** Built by a developer agent from an approved plan, in a clone of its own, and applied here as patches, in two series on one branch; each series reviewed twice. Migration `SoapNotes` (two columns on drafts and on signed notes, a template code on drafts, a new reference table `ehr.note_templates`; it changes no row and says so in its report). **Protected paths: the dictation prompt and the note schema** (`src/Ehr.Dictation/schema/structuring-prompt.md`, `note-fields.schema.json`: four fields) **and `db/policies`** (`50-clinical-notes.sql`: `ehr.finalize_clinical_note` requires and compares four sections and refuses a History, and the new `ehr.note_section_is_blank`; `52-visit-summaries.sql`: the plain-words rule compares with Subjective, Objective and an older note's History; `55-ai-review.sql`: proposals only for the four fields; `10-grants.sql`). `eval/known-limitations.toml`, Tahseen's list: its one entry's `field` renamed from `history` to `subjective`, nothing else. No authorization attribute or policy and not the sync conflict resolver.
  - *What it does:* a note, written or dictated, has four sections, Subjective, Objective, Assessment and Plan, each required before signing. **Notes signed before this step are not moved or relabelled:** they keep History, Assessment and Plan exactly as signed and are shown under those headings. An older unsigned draft opens with its History in Subjective and a notice to sort it; nothing changes in the database until its author saves. A visit type chosen in the editor shows prompts beside each section, from reference data, and never inserts text. "Vital signs for Objective…" offers the latest clinic day's standing readings as text, added only when the clinician chooses. The desktop's draft contract is unchanged; its offline copy shows four sections, and older builds still read it.
  - *The eval:* regenerated on this developer Mac from the models already on disk (nothing downloaded; no thermal warning before or after; about 35 minutes; nothing else heavy running). 82 of 82 recordings sorted; no order or allergy change shown; the only value without a warning is the d22 entry already accepted. Content F1 against the rule-following gold: overall 0.7382 (0.6677 with three fields, not strictly comparable: the gold has 321 values where it had 241), Subjective 0.6460 and Objective 0.7844 where History was 0.5783, Assessment 0.8552 (0.8243), Plan 0.8007 (0.7945); word error rate 0.0834, unchanged; the longest dictation sorted in 55.3 s of its 90. There is no accepted baseline file; the gate checks that the results come from this commit's prompt, schema, pipeline, scoring, gold and scripts, which CI's Dictation job confirmed.
  - **Decided without Tahseen** (the step's two entries list all): signed notes stay as signed (rejected: renaming History to Subjective, which would relabel findings as what the patient said and rewrite what was signed); all four sections required; a "visit type" is the template picked in the editor, prompts only; the vitals offer is the latest day's readings, since a note has no link to a visit; a section holding only invisible characters counts as blank, in the app and the database; **and a new warning, `one_field_holds_dictation`**, for a dictation that comes back whole in one section (d39: the transcript, plan included, verbatim in Subjective with the rest empty): the review says so and asks the clinician to sort it. It needed only a rescore. Changing the prompt was not tried: it needs a model run and may not fix it.

(2) passed with three low items, fixed. - **2026-11-22, step 15. Dictation proposals beyond the note (extends UC 10, UC 13, UC 14, UC 1, UC 4; FR-14 reworded).** Built by a developer agent from an approved plan, in a clone of its own, in two series, and reviewed by reviewer agents in clones of theirs: a full review of each series, two re-reviews of the second, and a last verification. The step's decision-log entry has every decision, the residual limits and the catalog gaps; this is what happened and what is Tahseen's to read.
  - *What it does:* the dictation service proposes, by its own rules and never the model's, an order or an allergy to add that the clinician dictated; the review of the dictation lists each one in the clinician's own words, marked in their sentence, labelled as proposed, to open in the order form or the allergy form, which fill in nothing from it and sign or save through every check they have, or to dismiss. Nothing is placed or saved by the system; a proposal is decided once and holds up nothing. New table `ehr.ai_chart_proposals` with its guard; one order cannot both answer a refill request and decide a proposal, held in the database.
  - *Narrower than the design approved, three ways, each the more protective and each Tahseen's to widen* (the entry's head): rules, not the model; the clinician's words and an empty form, not a pre-filled draft; orders and allergy additions only, not removals, changes or stops.
  - *What review changed, and why it took five rounds:* the first rules decided "plain" by lists of what must not appear, and two reviews in a row found false proposals in fresh sentences (about 220 of 420 in the second: "Ordering a CBC is unnecessary", "Order a CBC. No."). **Decided without Tahseen:** the design became a closed sentence: a proposal is made only when every word of the instruction's sentence is accounted for (a closed lead-in, the verb, an item the reference data names, its quantities, a closed set of trailers), the neighbouring sentences read for a listed set of cues; anything else is listed as not carried over, never proposed. Rejected: longer lists, which no review could finish. The cost is recall, below.
  - *The dictation checks changed for every dictation, beyond this step's scope,* because the same reviews found gaps that were already there: an order after "No," stayed in the Plan; an allergy change to "your" list was not seen; "I don't think she has pneumonia" passed as "she has pneumonia" (a cue's reach stopped at the pronoun); a negation stopped at the first comma of a list ("Denies fever, chills, night sweats" passed "Chills, night sweats"); a prescription "sent … to the pharmacy" was found only within 60 characters. Each is fixed with tests both ways; every stored answer was scored again and no field changed status but for these.
  - *The eval:* two model runs on this developer Mac (98, then 100 recordings with two trap scripts added after the second review), the rest scored again from stored answers without the models. Final: word error rate 0.0821, field F1 0.737 (0.7303 against the clinical gold); no order or allergy text in any field; **14 proposals, 14 right, none false, none misheard; 14 of the 18 the closed sentence can take, and 14 of the 63 the test set's authors marked as plainly said (0.22).** The list design had proposed 46 of the 63, with four misheard ("atageban", "ataapan", "a ts") that would have needed Tahseen's acceptance and, outside the test set, the false ones above; under the closed sentence a name the reference data lacks is never proposed, so nothing waits for his acceptance. The gate now holds the results to exactly the recordings the scripts define and scores the proposals again itself.
  - *Looked at:* screenshots of the review, both forms, the editor and the signed note in both themes at 1,280 and 360 pixels; five small things changed from them (what the waiting notice says on a signed note and beside the editor's own notice, warnings only while a proposal waits, a decided proposal links to its order or allergy on the chart, spacing). **Not walked on the local stack:** the dictation services do not run there; the browser tests and the eval are what cover it.
  - *Gates:* format clean; Release build; brand check; the web, security, architecture and data tests in CI; sidecar 1,664, eval 1,491 and setup-script 15 Python tests; the eval gate; CI green on every job (run 45102951108).
  - **For Tahseen:** (1) the three narrowings, above all the empty form where he said "drafts"; (2) recall of 0.22: most of what a clinician dictates as an order is listed, not proposed; the largest single cause is the catalog (the reference data names "Penicillin G" and "Natural latex rubber" but not "penicillin" or "latex", and has no sed rate, CRP, EKG, echo, PSA, A1c on its own, or sulfa): synonyms and abbreviations in the clinic's catalog are the way to raise it; the next is that an instruction said after another step of the plan in one sentence is not plain; (3) what the rules can't promise, listed at the head of the entry (a retraction no list has, a report before an instruction, a mishearing that lands on another real item, a sentence about a classmate read as the patient's), with the reviewer's judgement of about 1 to 2 false proposals in 100 on dictation with spoken punctuation, which is not a measurement; (4) unpunctuated dictation proposes nothing; (5) the server baseline for the eval is still his to record, now on 100 recordings.
  - *Heads-up for teammates:* Samir (UC 9/10: the instruction detector and the field checks changed as above; the eval has 51 scripts, two golds for proposals, and a generated `catalog.py`), Satwik (UC 13/14: the proposals section on the review, the notices on the editor and the signed note), Tahseen (UC 1: the order form opens from a proposal; an order answers a refill request or decides a proposal, never both), Diego (UC 4: the allergy form opens from a proposal; the chart's order and allergy rows carry ids a decided proposal links to), Luke (UC 12: `ai_chart_proposals` events on the audit page), Simar (UC 6: its reads count as the note's in Who viewed my record).

- **2026-11-24, step 16. Ambient listening (new UC 23; extends UC 9, UC 10, UC 13, UC 14; proposed FR-62).** Built by developer agents from the coordinator's approved plan (decisions D1 to D27, amendments A1 to A9), in clones of their own, in two series and a fix round after the record model session, and applied here as patches. The step's three decision-log entries hold every decision; this is what happened and what is Tahseen's to read.
  - *What it does:* at the clinic only, with everyone who will speak agreeing aloud first (recorded in the audit log before the microphone opens), a clinician records a visit's conversation; after Stop they name every voice, and the dictation service drafts the note's four sections a part at a time into the same review as a dictation, Subjective only from the patient's or a companion's words, each value checked as dictation's are and by the speaker rules. Nothing goes into the note until the clinician accepts it, and nothing is proposed for orders or the allergy list. The transcript is kept apart, never in the audit log, and deleted when the review is finished or 7 days after it was written.

So no session holding it can sit idle in a transaction for a minute.
